# DOMAIN_ CL — Clinical Operations (6)



DOMAIN: CL — Clinical Operations

CL-CP-001 — Plan of Care Development & Approval

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the requirements for developing, approving, implementing, and maintaining an individualized plan of care (POC) for every patient admitted to home health services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The plan of care is the foundational clinical document that authorizes, defines, and guides all services delivered to a patient during a certification period. It is simultaneously a clinical roadmap, a legal authorization for skilled services, and a billing instrument — any deficiency in its development, physician approval, or content directly compromises patient safety, regulatory compliance, and reimbursement integrity.

This policy ensures compliance with 42 CFR § 484.60 — Condition of Participation: Care Planning, Coordination, and Quality of Care, which mandates that a plan of care be established and periodically reviewed by a physician for every patient receiving home health services. It also ensures compliance with CMS PDGM episode management requirements, OASIS data collection obligations, and the agency's documentation and billing alignment standards.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses (RNs) responsible for completing the comprehensive assessment and establishing the plan of care

All clinical disciplines contributing to the plan of care (PT, OT, SLP, MSW, HHA)

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

All intake and admissions staff who initiate the plan of care process

Physicians and allowed practitioners who review and approve plans of care

The Medical Records and Health Information staff

The Revenue Cycle team responsible for billing based on the approved plan of care

This policy applies at Start of Care (SOC), Resumption of Care (ROC), and Recertification (RECERT) for every patient served by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. It does not govern individual visit notes, which are governed by CL-CD-001, or OASIS-specific data collection requirements, which are governed by CL-OA-001 through CL-OA-019.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall establish a written, individualized plan of care for every patient admitted to home health services prior to or at the initiation of services, as required by 42 CFR § 484.60(a).

4.2 The plan of care shall be developed by the registered nurse responsible for the patient's care in collaboration with the patient, the patient's caregiver(s), the patient's attending physician or allowed practitioner, and all clinical disciplines involved in the patient's care.

4.3 No skilled home health services shall be provided to a patient without a physician-approved plan of care. Verbal orders for services may be initiated prior to written physician signature, provided the verbal order is received, documented, and authenticated in compliance with policy CL-CP-004 (Verbal Order Receipt & Authentication).

4.4 The plan of care shall be signed and dated by the physician or allowed practitioner before the agency submits a claim for Medicare home health services. For initial certification periods, the physician must sign the plan of care — which may be the OASIS-generated CMS-485 or equivalent — within the timeframe required by CMS.

4.5 The plan of care shall contain all elements required by 42 CFR § 484.60(a), as defined in Section 5 of this policy and detailed in Appendix A.

4.6 The plan of care shall be patient-centered, based on the findings of the comprehensive assessment (CL-CA-001), and shall reflect the patient's goals, functional limitations, and individual clinical needs.

4.7 No services shall be provided that are not authorized by the plan of care and a corresponding physician order. Any service not reflected in the current plan of care requires a new or amended physician order before the service is rendered, except in clinical emergencies where safety requires immediate action followed by immediate physician notification per CL-CP-003.

4.8 The plan of care shall be reviewed, revised, and recertified at least every 60 calendar days and whenever a significant change in the patient's condition warrants modification, per policy CL-CP-002.

4.9 The physician-approved plan of care, the OASIS assessment, the physician orders, and all visit documentation must be internally consistent. Discrepancies between these documents constitute documentation integrity failures governed by policy CO-DC-004.

4.10 Only the most current physician-approved version of a patient's plan of care shall be operative. Superseded plan versions shall be retained in the clinical record but clearly marked as no longer active.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Initiating the Plan of Care Process at Start of Care

6.2 Required Elements of the Plan of Care

The plan of care for each patient shall contain, at minimum, all of the following elements as required by 42 CFR § 484.60(a) and CMS billing requirements. Absence of any required element constitutes a documentation deficiency subject to correction per CO-DC-003.

6.3 Physician Approval Process

6.4 Multidisciplinary Coordination in Plan of Care Development

6.5 Patient and Caregiver Engagement in Plan of Care

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors conducting a standard survey under the State Operations Manual (SOM) Appendix B will specifically verify under Tag G160 (42 CFR § 484.60):

Evidence that a plan of care exists for every patient. Surveyors will request clinical records and verify that a written, physician-approved plan of care exists for each active patient and for each certification period of sampled past patients. Missing plans of care or unsigned plans are Condition-level deficiency risks.

Evidence that the plan of care contains all required elements. Surveyors will review plans of care for completeness per 42 CFR § 484.60(a). Commonly cited deficiencies include: missing homebound status narrative, vague or unmeasurable goals, incomplete medication lists, and visit frequencies that do not match delivered services.

Evidence that the plan of care is patient-centered and individualized. Surveyors distinguish between plans that are genuinely tailored to the patient and plans that appear to be templated without individualization. Cookie-cutter goal language (e.g., "patient will improve") without patient-specific metrics is a common deficiency.

Evidence of physician approval. Surveyors will verify physician signatures on plans of care and cross-reference with service dates to confirm that signed plans of care predate or accompany the services billed. Surveys will also verify that the certifying physician is eligible (not employed by or financially related to the agency in a prohibited manner).

Evidence that the plan of care is consistent with services delivered. Surveyors will compare ordered visit frequencies against actual visit delivery records. Significant and unexplained variances are cited as documentation integrity failures and potential False Claims Act concerns.

Evidence of patient participation. Surveyors may interview patients and caregivers about their knowledge of their plan of care and whether they were involved in developing their goals.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 State Requirements

9.4 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All registered nurses responsible for conducting SOC assessments and developing plans of care shall receive competency-validated training on plan of care development requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter. Training shall be conducted by the Director of Nursing or a qualified designee and shall include: (a) all required elements of the plan of care per 42 CFR § 484.60(a); (b) ICD-10 primary diagnosis selection for PDGM; (c) SMART goal-writing for home health; (d) physician order management and the verbal order process; (e) plan of care transmission and tracking; (f) common survey deficiencies and how to avoid them; (g) the agency's EHR plan of care workflow.

10.2 All other clinical disciplines (PT, OT, SLP, MSW) shall receive orientation to plan of care processes relevant to their discipline-specific contributions within 14 calendar days of hire, covering: (a) how to read and confirm the plan of care; (b) their obligation not to deliver services outside the plan of care; (c) how to initiate a discipline-specific order change request through the assigned RN.

10.3 All personnel within scope of this policy shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new hire/appointment.

10.4 The Director of Nursing shall verify training completion and competency for all RNs in plan of care development prior to their independently conducting SOC assessments. Competency shall be documented using the SOC Competency Assessment Tool (Appendix D).

11. Version Control

11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.

11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."

11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.

11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

CL-CP-002 — Plan of Care Review & Update

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy mandates the periodic review and update of every patient's plan of care throughout the episode of home health care, at each recertification period, and whenever a significant change in the patient's condition warrants modification. The plan of care is not a static document. It must be a living clinical instrument that evolves with the patient's condition, reflects the patient's current functional status and goals, and accurately represents the services being delivered at any point during the certification period. A plan of care that does not reflect the patient's current clinical reality fails its primary function as both a clinical guide and a billing authorization document, and constitutes a documentation compliance failure under 42 CFR § 484.60.

This policy ensures that plans of care at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. are reviewed at defined intervals, that clinical changes trigger prompt plan modifications with corresponding physician orders, and that the recertification process is completed within the regulatory timeline established by CMS.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses managing active patient caseloads

All clinical disciplines (PT, OT, SLP, MSW) providing services under the plan of care

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

Clinical Coordinators responsible for recertification tracking

Physicians and allowed practitioners who review and sign updated plans of care

Medical Records and Revenue Cycle staff involved in episode management

This policy applies during every active certification period and at every recertification for all patients served by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 The plan of care for every active patient shall be formally reviewed by the responsible registered nurse at a minimum of every 30 calendar days during the certification period, regardless of whether a change is indicated.

4.2 The plan of care shall be comprehensively reviewed and updated at each recertification period — no later than every 60 calendar days — and the updated plan of care shall be transmitted to and signed by the certifying physician within the recertification timeline defined in policy CL-CP-008.

4.3 A plan of care modification shall be initiated within 24 hours whenever any of the following significant change conditions are identified: (a) a new diagnosis or significant worsening of an existing diagnosis; (b) a new medication, medication change, or medication discontinuation that affects the clinical picture; (c) a change in the patient's functional status that affects the type, frequency, or intensity of services needed; (d) a new physician order that adds, removes, or modifies any service; (e) a hospitalization, emergency department visit, or other inpatient event; (f) a patient or caregiver-reported change in condition or safety concern; (g) a fall or other adverse event; (h) a change in the patient's discharge goals or the clinical outlook for discharge.

4.4 All modifications to the plan of care shall be supported by a corresponding physician order. No change in service type, frequency, or discipline shall be implemented before a verbal or written physician order is obtained per policy CL-CP-003.

4.5 The updated and physician-approved plan of care shall be internally consistent with all OASIS data collected during the applicable assessment time point and with all visit documentation in the patient's clinical record.

4.6 The Director of Nursing shall conduct a supervisory review of all active patient plans of care no less than every 30 calendar days to identify cases where the plan of care is outdated, services do not match orders, or recertification is approaching without a completed assessment.

4.7 A LUPA (Low Utilization Payment Adjustment) risk review shall be integrated into the plan of care review process to identify patients at risk of not meeting the minimum visit threshold, per policy QA-PI-006.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Routine 30-Day Plan of Care Review

6.2 Mid-Episode Plan of Care Modification (Significant Change)

6.3 Recertification Plan of Care Review

6.4 Documentation of Discharge Planning Integration

6.5 LUPA Risk Monitoring Integration

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors will specifically examine:

Evidence that plans of care are reviewed at least every 60 days (and more frequently as indicated). Surveyors will check clinical records for documented reviews. A plan of care that is unchanged from SOC through a full 60-day episode without any documented review is a strong indicator of passive care management and will be cited.

Evidence that significant clinical changes prompt plan modifications. Surveyors will identify patients who had hospitalizations, significant medication changes, or new diagnoses during the episode and verify that the plan of care was updated. An unchanged plan following a clinical change is a common deficiency.

Evidence that all plan modifications are physician-ordered. Surveyors will cross-reference changes in service type or frequency between the original and current plan of care with the physician orders on file. Service changes without supporting orders are a False Claims Act risk and a CMS deficiency.

Evidence of recertification within the required window. Surveyors will request all OASIS assessments and cross-reference their dates with the certification period dates. Recertification assessments outside the Day 56–60 window are a documentation compliance failure and may affect billing.

Evidence that plans of care reflect the patient's actual current condition. Surveyors will compare the plan of care goals and functional status descriptions against visit notes from the same period. A plan describing a patient as "independent in ambulation" while visit notes document the patient as bed-bound is a documentation integrity failure.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All registered nurses shall receive training on plan of care review requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: (a) the criteria for significant change in condition; (b) the structured 30-day review process and required documentation; (c) the recertification assessment window and timeline; (d) the LUPA risk review process; (e) the EHR Plan of Care Review Note documentation standard.

10.2 The Director of Nursing shall receive training on supervisory review obligations, including the monthly caseload review process and escalation procedures, at hire and annually thereafter.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days of the policy effective date, any revision, or new appointment.

11. Version Control

11.1 This policy is maintained per policy EN-LC-001. Only the most current approved version is valid. Superseded versions shall be archived as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."

11.2 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval documented in meeting minutes, re-acknowledgment by all in-scope personnel within 14 calendar days, and policy index update per EN-TG-001.

11.3 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with Governing Body notification at the next regular meeting.

CL-CP-003 — Physician Orders & Order Management

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the requirements for obtaining, documenting, managing, and authenticating all physician orders at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Every skilled home health service, treatment, medication, and clinical intervention delivered to a patient must be authorized by a valid, current physician order. The physician order is the legal authorization for clinical action — without it, services are not reimbursable under Medicare, are not defensible clinically, and may constitute unauthorized practice. This policy ensures that all physician orders are obtained before services are rendered (except in clinical emergencies followed immediately by physician notification), are properly documented in the clinical record, are authenticated within required timeframes, and are maintained in a manner that supports the clinical record's integrity, billing compliance, and survey defensibility.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses and licensed vocational nurses receiving, transcribing, or documenting physician orders

All clinical disciplines (PT, OT, SLP, MSW) receiving discipline-specific orders

Physicians, nurse practitioners, clinical nurse specialists, and physician assistants issuing orders

Medical Records staff responsible for order management in the EHR

Clinical Coordinators managing physician order tracking

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

Revenue Cycle staff verifying order support for billed services

This policy does not govern OASIS assessment time points or recertification processes specifically (see CL-CA-004 and CL-CP-008), though all orders generated at those junctures fall under this policy.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 No skilled home health service shall be provided to any patient without a valid, current physician order authorizing that service. This requirement applies to all disciplines, all visit types, and all clinical interventions including medications, treatments, procedures, equipment, and supplies.

4.2 All physician orders shall be obtained from a physician, nurse practitioner, clinical nurse specialist, or physician assistant who is legally authorized to practice in California, who is not excluded from participation in federal healthcare programs, and who does not have a financial relationship with the agency that violates the Stark Law or Anti-Kickback Statute.

4.3 Written orders from the physician are the preferred format. Verbal orders are permissible when a written order cannot be obtained in advance of a clinical need, provided they are received and documented per policy CL-CP-004 and authenticated by the physician within the timeframes specified in Section 6.3.

4.4 All orders shall be specific, legible, dated, and signed (for written orders) or documented and authenticated (for verbal orders). Orders containing ambiguous language, missing elements, or requiring clinical interpretation shall not be executed without clarification from the ordering physician.

4.5 All orders in effect for a patient shall be current, consistent with the active plan of care, and reflected in the patient's medication and treatment lists. No order may be executed beyond its effective date or outside its specified scope without renewal or modification.

4.6 The agency shall maintain a complete and organized Order Management Record in each patient's clinical record, documenting every order received, the date received, the ordering physician, and the authentication status.

4.7 Physician orders and the services delivered under those orders must be in alignment. The Revenue Cycle team shall not bill for any service that is not supported by a dated, authenticated physician order on file, consistent with policy CO-DC-004.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Obtaining Physician Orders — General Requirements

6.2 Written Order Management

6.3 Verbal Order Authentication

6.4 Emergency Orders

6.5 Order Changes and Discontinuation

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

Surveyors will request clinical records and cross-reference every service in visit notes against the physician orders on file. They will specifically verify that verbal orders are authenticated and that no service was rendered outside the scope of an active order. Missing or unauthenticated orders linked to billed services are False Claims Act risk items and are commonly referred for further investigation.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.4 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All clinicians authorized to receive physician orders shall receive competency-validated training on order management requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: (a) what constitutes a valid written vs. verbal order; (b) the elements required for a complete order; (c) the order clarification process for ambiguous orders; (d) the verbal order authentication requirement and timeline; (e) the EHR Order Management workflow; (f) consequences of executing services without orders.

10.2 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire.

11. Version Control

Same standards as all preceding policies — maintained per EN-LC-001; substantive revisions require Governing Body approval; non-substantive revisions approved by Administrator.

CL-CP-004 — Verbal Order Receipt & Authentication

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the protocols for receiving, reading back, documenting, and authenticating verbal orders (including telephone orders) at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Verbal orders are a necessary and accepted component of home health clinical practice, particularly in situations where a patient's clinical condition changes between visits and a written order cannot be obtained before a clinical action is needed. However, verbal orders carry significant risk of miscommunication, transcription error, and authentication failure — each of which can result in patient harm, documentation compliance failures, and billing integrity violations. This policy ensures that every verbal order is handled with a standardized, auditable protocol that protects patient safety, fulfills the California Nursing Practice Act requirements for verbal order management, and supports the agency's documentation integrity standards.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses authorized to receive verbal orders from physicians and allowed practitioners

Licensed vocational nurses who receive verbal orders within their California BPC § 2859 scope limitations

All physicians, nurse practitioners, clinical nurse specialists, and physician assistants issuing verbal orders

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

Medical Records staff managing verbal order authentication in the EHR

Clinical Coordinators tracking pending verbal order authentications per CL-CP-009

Revenue Cycle staff who must confirm authentication before billing

This policy does not apply to orders received in fully written form at the time of issuance. It applies to any order — including plan of care modifications, medication changes, service additions or discontinuations, treatment orders, and discharge orders — that is communicated orally and requires transcription by the receiving clinician.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Only a registered nurse (RN) or a licensed vocational nurse (LVN) acting within the scope of practice defined by California Business and Professions Code § 2725 (RN) and § 2859 (LVN) may receive and transcribe verbal orders at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. No other clinical discipline shall receive or transcribe verbal orders for clinical services requiring physician authorization.

4.2 All verbal orders shall be received using the standard Read-Back Protocol defined in Section 6.1 of this policy. A verbal order is not valid and shall not be documented as received until the read-back is completed and the ordering physician confirms the order as accurately transcribed.

4.3 Verbal orders shall be documented in the patient's Order Management Record in the EHR within 24 hours of receipt, or before the end of the clinical shift in which the order was received, whichever is sooner.

4.4 Every verbal order shall be authenticated — reviewed, confirmed, and signed by the ordering physician — within 7 calendar days of the verbal order being given. This requirement is mandatory and non-negotiable. Services rendered under an unauthenticated verbal order that has not been authenticated within 7 calendar days are not billable and are subject to overpayment assessment.

4.5 The agency shall maintain an active verbal order authentication tracking system per policy CL-CP-009 that identifies every pending verbal order, the date received, the authentication deadline, and the current status.

4.6 No verbal order shall be executed if the transcribing clinician has any doubt about the accuracy of the transcription, the clinical appropriateness of the order, or the identity of the ordering physician. In cases of doubt, the clinician shall contact the Director of Nursing before proceeding.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Verbal Order Receipt — Read-Back Protocol

6.2 Verbal Order Documentation in the EHR

6.3 Authentication Tracking and Follow-Up

6.4 LVN Limitations on Verbal Orders

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

Surveyors will select patients with active verbal orders and examine whether: the read-back is documented, the order is entered within the required timeframe, and the physician signature is on file within the authentication period. They will also ask clinical staff to demonstrate their read-back process. An inability to describe the read-back process is a training deficiency citation under 42 CFR § 484.60.

Auditors from the MAC or OIG will specifically look for verbal orders on claims that have not been authenticated, as these constitute unsupported billed services.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.4 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All RNs and LVNs shall receive competency-validated training on verbal order receipt and the read-back protocol within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter. Competency validation shall require demonstrated proficiency in the read-back protocol in a simulation or role-play format.

10.2 LVNs shall receive specific training on the scope limitations for verbal order receipt at hire and annually.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire.

11. Version Control

Maintained per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval. Non-substantive revisions approved by Administrator.

CL-CP-005 — Coordination of Care

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy mandates the coordination of services among all disciplines involved in a patient's care at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. and with external providers — including physicians, hospitals, skilled nursing facilities, pharmacies, community agencies, and other healthcare entities — involved in the patient's overall care. Coordination of care is not merely a documentation requirement; it is a clinical and ethical imperative that directly affects patient outcomes, patient safety, and the efficiency of care delivery. Fragmented care — where disciplines operate in silos, where the attending physician is not kept current on the patient's status, or where the home health team is unaware of care provided by other providers — creates clinical risk, medication errors, unnecessary hospitalizations, and documentation inconsistencies that threaten billing integrity. This policy ensures that Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. operates as a fully integrated member of the patient's care team at all times.

This policy implements the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.60(c) — Standard: Coordination of Care, which requires that the agency coordinates services provided and makes referrals to appropriate community resources.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses managing patient care

All clinical disciplines (PT, OT, SLP, MSW) providing patient services

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

The Medical Social Worker (primary community resource coordination role)

Operations staff managing scheduling and visit logistics

The attending physician and referring providers

Intake and discharge planning staff

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall ensure that all services provided to each patient are coordinated among all disciplines involved in the patient's care and with all external providers who contribute to the patient's overall health management.

4.2 The assigned registered nurse shall serve as the primary care coordinator for each patient, responsible for ensuring that all disciplines contributing to the patient's care are aware of the current plan of care, recent clinical changes, and the patient's progress toward goals.

4.3 Care coordination shall be documented in the patient's clinical record and shall include: (a) interdisciplinary communications regarding clinical status, plan changes, and goal progress; (b) communications with the attending physician, including all notifications of significant changes; (c) communications with external providers including hospitals, SNFs, pharmacies, and community agencies; (d) referrals to community resources.

4.4 The agency shall maintain ongoing, proactive communication with the patient's attending physician throughout the episode. At minimum, the physician shall be notified of: (a) any significant change in the patient's condition; (b) any new diagnosis identified during home health; (c) any medication concern or adverse reaction; (d) any patient safety risk identified during home assessment; (e) the patient's progress at recertification; (f) the patient's discharge status and estimated discharge date.

4.5 When a patient is transitioning from an inpatient setting to home health, the agency shall obtain and review all available discharge documentation, including discharge summaries, medication reconciliation records, and follow-up physician appointment schedules, to ensure continuity of the care plan.

4.6 The Medical Social Worker (MSW), when involved in a patient's care, shall serve as the primary coordinator for community resource referrals, support service linkage, and psychosocial needs coordination.

4.7 A formal interdisciplinary care coordination communication shall occur no later than 14 calendar days after SOC for any patient receiving three or more disciplines, and at any point when a significant change in condition occurs.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Internal Interdisciplinary Coordination

6.2 Physician Communication

6.3 Transition from Inpatient Setting

6.4 External Provider Coordination

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors will examine care coordination documentation under Tag G160 (42 CFR § 484.60). They will interview patients and caregivers to assess whether the patient felt their care was coordinated and whether the patient knew who to contact with concerns. They will look for documented physician communications, care conferences, and community resource referrals. The absence of physician contact notes in a complex patient's clinical record is a commonly cited deficiency.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.4 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All clinical staff shall receive training on care coordination requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, covering: the 24-hour physician notification requirement; the care conference requirement; community resource referral documentation; and warm handoff procedures.

10.2 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of the effective date, revision, or hire.

11. Version Control

Maintained per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval. Non-substantive revisions approved by Administrator.

APPENDIX A — Policy Acknowledgment Form

CL-CP-006 — Discharge Planning & Criteria

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the criteria, processes, and documentation requirements for discharging patients from home health services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Discharge planning is a clinical process, not an administrative event — it begins at the time of admission and evolves throughout the episode as the patient progresses toward or away from identified goals. A well-executed discharge process ensures that patients leave home health care with the knowledge, support, and community resources necessary to maintain their health independently, that the clinical record reflects a complete and justified basis for discharge, and that the transition to the next setting or self-management is coordinated with the patient, the attending physician, and all relevant external providers.

This policy governs all types of discharges, including: goal-achieved discharges, physician-ordered discharges, patient-initiated discharges (including against medical advice), safety-related discharges, and discharges due to non-compliance. It ensures compliance with 42 CFR § 484.60, which requires the agency to document the basis for discharge, and with OASIS discharge assessment requirements under CMS guidelines.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses managing active patient caseloads

All clinical disciplines providing services at discharge

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

Medical Social Workers coordinating community resources at discharge

Clinical Coordinators managing discharge scheduling and documentation

Medical Records and Revenue Cycle staff managing discharge OASIS and final billing

The attending physician who must be notified of and, where required, authorize discharge

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Discharge planning shall begin at the time of the Start of Care assessment and shall be an ongoing, documented component of every patient's plan of care throughout the episode.

4.2 Discharge criteria — the specific clinical, functional, and support-system milestones that must be achieved before a patient is safely discharged from home health — shall be identified and documented in the plan of care at SOC, reviewed at every 30-day review, and updated as the patient's condition evolves.

4.3 Discharge shall occur when one or more of the following criteria are met: (a) the patient has achieved the goals established in the plan of care; (b) the patient no longer meets the Medicare homebound status criteria; (c) the physician orders discharge; (d) the patient or legal representative requests discharge; (e) the patient's condition has stabilized to the extent that skilled services are no longer required; (f) the patient has transferred to another care setting (hospital, SNF, hospice, assisted living); (g) the patient has relocated outside the agency's service area; (h) the patient or household poses an unresolvable safety risk to agency staff; (i) the patient is non-compliant with the plan of care in a manner that renders the provision of skilled services clinically meaningless and all reasonable efforts to resolve non-compliance have been exhausted and documented.

4.4 Discharge shall not occur based on: (a) the patient's race, ethnicity, national origin, disability, sexual orientation, gender identity, or religion; (b) the patient's diagnosis alone, without clinical justification; (c) financial considerations, including the patient's inability to pay for non-covered services or the likelihood of a LUPA.

4.5 The attending physician shall be notified of all planned discharges and shall issue a discharge order before the discharge OASIS is completed, except in emergency situations where the patient is transferred to an acute care setting.

4.6 The discharge OASIS assessment shall be completed within the timeframes required by CMS and transmitted to the OASIS database per policy CL-OA-001.

4.7 The patient shall receive a written discharge summary and home safety education appropriate to their discharge status before or at the time of the final visit.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Discharge Planning Throughout the Episode

6.2 Discharge Criteria Assessment and Physician Notification

6.3 Patient-Initiated Discharge and Discharge Against Medical Advice

6.4 Safety-Related Discharge

6.5 Non-Compliance Discharge

CL-SD-001 — Skilled Nursing Assessment & Services

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the scope, frequency, clinical standards, and documentation requirements for skilled nursing assessment and services delivered by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Skilled nursing is the cornerstone of the Medicare home health benefit — it is the discipline most frequently ordered, most frequently surveyed, and most frequently the basis for the patient's initial and continued eligibility for home health services. The skilled nurse's clinical judgment, assessment competency, documentation accuracy, and care coordination are the primary determinants of clinical quality, regulatory compliance, and billing integrity across every episode.

This policy ensures that every skilled nursing visit is conducted with a clear clinical purpose, is documented with the specificity and timeliness required by CMS, and is aligned with the physician-approved plan of care. It implements the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.75 — Condition of Participation: Skilled Professional Services, and the skilled nursing coverage requirements defined in the CMS Home Health Benefit Manual, Chapter 7, § 40.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses (RNs) providing direct patient care

All licensed vocational nurses (LVNs) providing skilled nursing services under RN supervision and within their California BPC § 2859 scope

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for nursing service oversight

Clinical Coordinators managing nursing visit scheduling

Revenue Cycle staff verifying nursing visit documentation supports billed claims

This policy governs all skilled nursing visits — assessment visits, routine skilled visits, supervisory visits, recertification visits, resumption of care visits, and discharge visits. It does not govern OASIS-specific data collection requirements (CL-OA-001 through CL-OA-019), plan of care development (CL-CP-001), or discipline-specific services of other professions (CL-SD-002 through CL-SD-008).

4. Policy Statement

4.1 All skilled nursing services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall be provided by registered nurses (RNs) or licensed vocational nurses (LVNs) who are currently licensed by the California Board of Registered Nursing or the California Board of Vocational Nursing and Psychiatric Technicians, whose licensure status has been verified per HR-TA-004, and who have demonstrated clinical competency per HR-TD-003.

4.2 Every skilled nursing visit shall have a documented clinical purpose that is directly tied to the physician-approved plan of care. No skilled nursing visit shall be conducted without a current, active physician order authorizing the visit per CL-CP-003. Visits without a clinical purpose tied to the plan of care are not billable under Medicare and expose the agency to False Claims Act liability.

4.3 At each skilled nursing visit, the RN or LVN shall perform and document, at minimum: (a) a focused clinical assessment relevant to the patient's diagnoses and plan of care goals; (b) skilled interventions as ordered in the plan of care; (c) an assessment of the patient's response to the interventions provided; (d) patient and caregiver education as indicated; (e) evaluation of progress toward the plan of care goals; (f) communication with the physician or other care team members as clinically warranted.

4.4 The scope of LVN practice at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall be limited to the services permitted under California BPC § 2859, provided under the direction of a registered nurse. LVNs shall not independently perform comprehensive assessments, develop or modify the plan of care, receive verbal orders outside the scope defined in CL-CP-004, complete OASIS assessments, or make clinical judgments that are within the exclusive scope of RN practice.

4.5 The Director of Nursing shall ensure that RN clinical supervision of LVN practice is documented per CL-SD-008 and that LVN caseloads are reviewed at minimum every 14 calendar days.

4.6 All skilled nursing visit documentation shall be completed and locked in the EHR within 24 hours of the visit per CL-CD-004. Documentation shall meet the minimum content standards defined in CL-CD-001 and shall include sufficient clinical detail to support the medical necessity of the visit and the skilled nature of the services provided.

4.7 Skilled nursing services shall be provided at the frequency and duration specified in the physician-approved plan of care. Any deviation from the ordered frequency — whether a missed visit, an additional visit, or a frequency change — shall be managed per CL-SD-024 (missed visits) and CL-SD-025 (ordered visit frequency compliance) and shall require a physician order for any change.

4.8 All skilled nursing visits shall include an assessment of medication management per CL-SD-012, safety hazards per RM-PS-001, and fall risk per CL-SD-015 as part of the standard nursing assessment protocol.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Pre-Visit Preparation

6.2 Skilled Nursing Visit Execution

6.3 Visit Documentation

6.4 LVN Practice Oversight

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G184 (42 CFR § 484.75) will verify that: skilled nursing services are provided by qualified nurses with current licensure; each visit has a documented clinical purpose; visit documentation supports the skilled nature of the service; and LVN practice is appropriately supervised. ADR (Additional Documentation Request) auditors from the MAC will focus on whether each visit note demonstrates medical necessity and skilled care — they are trained to identify "maintenance-level" documentation that does not support Medicare coverage.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 State Requirements

9.4 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All RNs and LVNs shall receive competency-validated training on skilled nursing visit requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: visit documentation standards demonstrating medical necessity and skilled care; focused assessment techniques; scope of practice boundaries (especially LVN limitations); medication review protocol; and fall risk and safety assessment integration.

10.2 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of the effective date, revision, or hire.

11. Version Control

Maintained per EN-LC-001. Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval. Non-substantive revisions approved by Administrator.

CL-SD-002 — Physical Therapy Services

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the clinical standards, documentation requirements, competency expectations, and discharge criteria for physical therapy (PT) services provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Physical therapy is one of the qualifying skilled services under the Medicare home health benefit and may serve as the sole qualifying service for a patient's admission to home health. PT services in the home setting address functional mobility limitations, balance and gait deficits, pain management through therapeutic exercise, strength and endurance training, transfer training, assistive device training, and fall prevention interventions. This policy ensures that all PT services are delivered by qualified physical therapists and physical therapist assistants (PTAs) in accordance with the physician-approved plan of care, California Physical Therapy Practice Act, and the CMS Home Health Benefit Manual coverage requirements.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All physical therapists (PTs) providing direct patient care

All physical therapist assistants (PTAs) providing services under PT supervision

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for clinical service oversight

Clinical Coordinators managing PT visit scheduling

Revenue Cycle staff verifying PT documentation supports billed claims

This policy governs all physical therapy evaluations, re-evaluations, treatment visits, and discharge visits.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Physical therapy services shall be provided only by physical therapists licensed by the Physical Therapy Board of California, or by physical therapist assistants working under the supervision of a licensed physical therapist in accordance with California Business and Professions Code § 2630 et seq. and 16 CCR § 1398 et seq.

4.2 All PT services shall be authorized by a physician order and reflected in the physician-approved plan of care per CL-CP-001 and CL-CP-003. No PT service shall be rendered without a current, active physician order specifying the discipline, frequency, and duration of services.

4.3 The physical therapist shall conduct an initial evaluation within 48 hours of receiving the patient referral (or within the timeframe specified in the physician order), which shall include at minimum: (a) a review of the patient's medical history, current diagnoses, and surgical history relevant to PT; (b) a standardized functional mobility assessment including gait, balance, transfer ability, strength, range of motion, and endurance; (c) a pain assessment per CL-SD-014; (d) a fall risk assessment per CL-SD-015; (e) an assessment of the home environment for PT-relevant safety hazards and accessibility; (f) identification of patient-specific, measurable, time-bound goals; (g) a recommended treatment plan including visit frequency, duration, and anticipated discharge criteria.

4.4 PTAs shall not independently conduct initial evaluations, establish goals, develop or modify the plan of care, or determine discharge. PTAs shall deliver treatment interventions under the direction and supervision of the licensed physical therapist, with documented supervisory review at minimum every 30 calendar days or every 6th visit, whichever occurs first, or per California Physical Therapy Practice Act requirements if more stringent.

4.5 All PT visit documentation shall be completed within 24 hours of the visit per CL-CD-004 and shall demonstrate the skilled nature of the service, patient response to treatment, and measurable progress toward goals.

4.6 The physical therapist shall conduct a re-evaluation at each recertification period, or more frequently when warranted by a significant change in the patient's condition, and shall update goals, the treatment plan, and the recommended visit frequency.

4.7 Discharge from PT services shall occur when: (a) the patient has met all PT-specific goals; (b) the patient has plateaued and further skilled PT services will not result in meaningful functional improvement; (c) the physician orders discontinuation of PT; (d) the patient declines further PT services. The discharging PT shall document a discharge summary including final functional status, goals achieved, and home exercise program provided.

4.8 When PT is the sole qualifying skilled service for a patient's home health admission, the physical therapist shall collaborate with the assigned RN to ensure that the patient's overall eligibility for home health is maintained, including homebound status documentation per CL-CA-005.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Initial PT Evaluation

6.2 PT Treatment Visits

6.3 PTA Supervision

6.4 PT Discharge

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors will verify that PT evaluations are comprehensive, goals are measurable, visit notes demonstrate skilled intervention and progression, and PTA supervision is appropriately documented. MAC auditors will specifically examine whether PT documentation supports medical necessity and skilled care versus maintenance-level services — maintenance therapy documentation is the leading cause of PT claim denials.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 State Requirements

9.4 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All PTs and PTAs shall receive training on agency-specific PT service requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: documentation standards supporting skilled PT; goal-writing standards; PTA supervision requirements under California law; and the discharge summary process.

10.2 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days of the effective date, revision, or hire.

11. Version Control

CL-SD-003 — Occupational Therapy Services

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the clinical standards, documentation requirements, competency expectations, and discharge criteria for occupational therapy (OT) services provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Occupational therapy in the home health setting addresses the patient's ability to perform activities of daily living (ADLs) and instrumental activities of daily living (IADLs), upper extremity function, fine motor skills, cognitive-perceptual function, home safety and adaptive equipment needs, energy conservation, and functional independence training. OT is a qualifying skilled service under Medicare and may be the sole qualifying service for continued home health eligibility after the initial qualifying service (nursing, PT, or SLP) has been discharged. This policy ensures that all OT services comply with 42 CFR § 484.75, the California Occupational Therapy Practice Act, and CMS coverage requirements.

3. Scope

This policy applies to all occupational therapists (OTs), certified occupational therapy assistants (COTAs), the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, Clinical Coordinators, and Revenue Cycle staff verifying OT documentation supports billed claims.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 OT services shall be provided only by occupational therapists licensed by the California Board of Occupational Therapy (CBOT), or by certified occupational therapy assistants (COTAs) working under OT supervision in accordance with California BPC § 2570 et seq.

4.2 All OT services shall be authorized by a physician order and reflected in the physician-approved plan of care per CL-CP-001 and CL-CP-003.

4.3 The OT shall conduct an initial evaluation within 48 hours of receiving the patient referral, including: (a) review of the patient's medical history, current diagnoses, and relevant surgical history; (b) assessment of ADL and IADL performance using standardized functional assessment tools; (c) upper extremity assessment including strength, range of motion, sensation, coordination, and fine motor skills; (d) cognitive-perceptual assessment as indicated; (e) home environment assessment for adaptive equipment needs, home modifications, and safety concerns; (f) identification of patient-specific, measurable, time-bound functional goals; (g) a recommended treatment plan including visit frequency, duration, and discharge criteria.

4.4 COTAs shall not independently conduct initial evaluations, establish goals, develop or modify the plan of care, or determine discharge. COTAs shall deliver treatment interventions under the direction of the supervising OT, with documented supervisory review at minimum every 30 calendar days or every 6th COTA visit, whichever occurs first, or per CBOT requirements if more stringent.

4.5 All OT visit documentation shall be completed within 24 hours of the visit and shall demonstrate the skilled nature of the service, the patient's response to treatment, and measurable progress toward functional goals.

4.6 The OT shall conduct a re-evaluation at each recertification period, or more frequently when warranted by a significant change in the patient's condition, and shall update goals and the treatment plan.

4.7 Discharge from OT shall occur when: (a) the patient has met all OT-specific goals; (b) the patient has plateaued and further skilled OT services will not result in meaningful functional improvement; (c) the physician orders discontinuation; or (d) the patient declines further services. The OT shall document a discharge summary including final functional status, goals achieved, and recommendations for ongoing functional maintenance.

4.8 When OT is the continuing qualifying service (the initial qualifying service has been discharged), the OT shall ensure that homebound status and ongoing eligibility documentation is maintained per CL-CA-005.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Initial OT Evaluation

6.2 OT Treatment Visits

6.3 COTA Supervision

6.4 OT Discharge

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

Same structure as CL-SD-002, adapted for OT: initial evaluation, treatment visit notes, COTA supervisory review, re-evaluation notes, discharge summary, and policy acknowledgment — all within 24 hours; retained minimum 7 years.

CL-SD-004 — Speech-Language Pathology Services

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the clinical standards, documentation requirements, competency expectations, and discharge criteria for speech-language pathology (SLP) services provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. SLP services in the home health setting address communication disorders (aphasia, dysarthria, apraxia, voice disorders), cognitive-linguistic deficits, swallowing disorders (dysphagia), and related safety and functional impairments. SLP is a qualifying skilled service under the Medicare home health benefit. This policy ensures that all SLP services comply with 42 CFR § 484.75, the California Speech-Language Pathology and Audiology Practice Act, and CMS coverage requirements.

3. Scope

This policy applies to all speech-language pathologists providing direct patient care, the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, Clinical Coordinators, and Revenue Cycle staff verifying SLP documentation. Note: Speech-language pathology assistants (SLPAs) are not independently billable under Medicare home health and their use is subject to California licensing board restrictions and direct on-site supervision requirements.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 SLP services shall be provided only by speech-language pathologists holding a current California license issued by the Speech-Language Pathology and Audiology and Hearing Aid Dispensers Board per California BPC § 2530 et seq. and holding the Certificate of Clinical Competence in Speech-Language Pathology (CCC-SLP) from the American Speech-Language-Hearing Association (ASHA), or meeting equivalent qualification standards per CMS requirements.

4.2 All SLP services shall be authorized by a physician order and reflected in the physician-approved plan of care per CL-CP-001 and CL-CP-003.

4.3 The SLP shall conduct an initial evaluation within 48 hours of receiving the patient referral, including: (a) review of the patient's medical history, current diagnoses, neurological history, and any imaging or diagnostic reports relevant to communication or swallowing function; (b) assessment of speech articulation, fluency, and voice; (c) language assessment including receptive and expressive language abilities; (d) cognitive-linguistic assessment including attention, memory, problem-solving, and executive function; (e) swallowing assessment including oral motor examination, clinical swallowing evaluation, and determination of whether an instrumental swallowing assessment (MBSS or FEES) is indicated; (f) identification of patient-specific, measurable, time-bound goals; (g) a recommended treatment plan including visit frequency, duration, diet texture and liquid consistency recommendations if applicable, and discharge criteria.

4.4 SLP visit documentation shall be completed within 24 hours and shall demonstrate the skilled nature of the service, patient response to treatment, and measurable progress.

4.5 The SLP shall conduct a re-evaluation at each recertification period, or more frequently when warranted by a significant change in the patient's condition, particularly any change in swallowing status or neurological function.

4.6 Discharge from SLP shall occur when: (a) the patient has met all SLP-specific goals; (b) the patient has plateaued and further skilled SLP will not result in meaningful improvement; (c) the physician orders discontinuation; or (d) the patient declines further services. The SLP shall document a discharge summary including final communication and swallowing status, goals achieved, and recommendations.

4.7 For patients with dysphagia, the SLP shall coordinate all diet texture and liquid consistency recommendations with the assigned RN, the physician, the caregiver, and any other disciplines involved in the patient's nutrition and hydration. All diet modifications must be ordered by the physician.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Initial SLP Evaluation

6.2 SLP Treatment Visits

6.3 SLP Discharge

6.4 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

Same structure as previous discipline policies: initial evaluation, treatment visit notes, re-evaluation notes, discharge summary, and policy acknowledgment — all within 24 hours; retained minimum 7 years.

8.2 Common Failure Points

CL-SD-005 — Medical Social Work Services

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the scope, referral criteria, clinical standards, and documentation requirements for medical social work (MSW) services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Medical social work in home health addresses the psychosocial, emotional, financial, environmental, and community resource needs that affect the patient's ability to participate in and benefit from the medical plan of care. MSW services are a Medicare-covered home health service under 42 CFR § 484.75 when they are necessary to resolve social or emotional problems that are expected to impede the effective treatment of the patient's medical condition. MSW is not a qualifying skilled service for home health admission — it may be provided only in conjunction with a qualifying skilled service (nursing, PT, OT, or SLP).

3. Scope

This policy applies to all medical social workers (MSWs) providing direct patient services, the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, assigned RNs making MSW referrals, and Revenue Cycle staff verifying MSW documentation.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 MSW services shall be provided only by individuals holding a master's degree in social work (MSW) from a Council on Social Work Education (CSWE) accredited program, or a doctoral degree in social work, and who meet the qualifications defined in 42 CFR § 484.115(d).

4.2 MSW services shall be authorized by a physician order and reflected in the plan of care per CL-CP-001 and CL-CP-003. An RN or other discipline identifying a psychosocial need shall request a physician order for MSW services.

4.3 Referral criteria for MSW services include but are not limited to: (a) psychosocial factors interfering with the patient's medical plan of care (depression, anxiety, caregiver stress, social isolation, family conflict); (b) financial barriers to care (inability to afford medications, equipment, or supplies); (c) need for community resource linkage (transportation, meal programs, financial assistance, adult protective services, support groups); (d) advanced care planning needs (advance directive discussions, end-of-life planning, hospice referral counseling); (e) abuse, neglect, or exploitation concerns requiring psychosocial assessment and mandatory reporting per CL-PR-006; (f) substance abuse concerns; (g) grief and bereavement counseling needs.

4.4 The MSW shall conduct a psychosocial assessment within 48 hours of receiving the referral, including: (a) the patient's emotional and psychological status; (b) the patient's social support system; (c) financial status and barriers; (d) community resource needs; (e) advance directive status; (f) caregiver stress and coping; (g) safety concerns including abuse, neglect, or exploitation indicators; (h) cultural and spiritual considerations affecting care.

4.5 MSW visit documentation shall be completed within 24 hours and shall demonstrate the clinical necessity of the social work intervention and its relationship to the medical plan of care. MSW documentation that reads as non-medical counseling or general social conversation does not support Medicare coverage.

4.6 The MSW shall serve as the primary coordinator for community resource referrals per CL-CP-005. All referrals shall be documented with the resource identified, the reason for referral, the date, and the outcome.

4.7 Discharge from MSW services shall occur when: (a) the psychosocial goals have been met; (b) the community resource linkages have been established; (c) the psychosocial barriers to the medical plan of care have been resolved or mitigated; (d) the qualifying skilled service has been discharged; or (e) the patient declines further MSW services.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 MSW Referral and Initial Assessment

6.2 MSW Treatment Visits

6.3 MSW Discharge

6.4 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

Same structure as previous policies: initial psychosocial assessment, treatment visit notes, community resource referral documentation, discharge summary, and policy acknowledgment — within 24 hours; retained minimum 7 years.

9. References

10.2 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days.

Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. | Policy Reference: CL-SD-005 | Version: 1.0

CL-SD-006 — Home Health Aide Services & Supervision

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the service delivery standards, supervision requirements, and documentation expectations for home health aide (HHA) services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Home health aide services are a Medicare-covered service available only in conjunction with a qualifying skilled service and only when the patient requires personal care assistance as part of the physician-approved plan of care. The HHA provides hands-on personal care, simple health-related services, and assistance with activities that support the skilled care plan — but does not perform skilled clinical services, medication administration, or clinical assessments. The CMS Conditions of Participation impose the most prescriptive supervision requirements on HHA services of any home health discipline, and non-compliance with HHA supervision requirements is one of the most commonly cited deficiencies in CMS surveys. This policy implements 42 CFR § 484.80 — Condition of Participation: Home Health Aide Services.

3. Scope

This policy applies to all home health aides providing direct patient care, all RNs providing HHA supervision, the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, Clinical Coordinators managing HHA scheduling, and Operations staff coordinating HHA assignments.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 HHA services shall be provided only by individuals who have completed a home health aide training program meeting the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.80(b) and have passed a competency evaluation per 42 CFR § 484.80(c) and agency policy CL-SD-007.

4.2 HHA services shall be authorized by a physician order and reflected in the plan of care, which shall specify: (a) the type of personal care services to be provided; (b) the frequency and duration of HHA visits; (c) any specific clinical parameters the HHA must observe and report (e.g., skin checks, intake/output monitoring, vital signs — if trained and competency-validated).

4.3 Each patient receiving HHA services shall have a written HHA care plan (also called an HHA assignment sheet or HHA task list) developed by the assigned RN that specifies, for each visit: (a) the specific tasks the HHA is authorized to perform for this patient; (b) the sequence of tasks if clinically relevant; (c) any precautions, safety measures, or clinical observations to make and report; (d) parameters for when to contact the RN immediately (e.g., skin breakdown, vital sign abnormalities, patient complaints, behavioral changes).

4.4 A registered nurse shall make a supervisory visit to the patient's home to observe and assess the HHA's provision of care no less frequently than every 14 calendar days, as required by 42 CFR § 484.80(h). The supervisory visit shall be made while the HHA is providing care, so the RN can directly observe the aide's skills, interaction with the patient, and compliance with the HHA care plan. At minimum every other supervisory visit (i.e., every 28 days), the RN supervisory visit must be conducted on-site with the HHA present.

4.5 The RN supervisory visit shall include: (a) direct observation of the HHA performing assigned tasks; (b) assessment of the patient's satisfaction with HHA services; (c) assessment of whether the HHA care plan remains appropriate; (d) assessment of the HHA's competency in assigned tasks; (e) documentation of the supervisory visit findings, any corrective guidance provided, and any changes to the HHA care plan.

4.6 If the HHA's primary supervising discipline is a therapist (when therapy is the only qualifying skilled service), the therapist shall conduct the supervisory visits per the same 14-day schedule, but an RN shall also make a supervisory visit at least once every 60 calendar days per 42 CFR § 484.80(h)(2).

4.7 The HHA shall not perform any of the following: (a) medication administration; (b) sterile procedures; (c) clinical assessments; (d) any task not assigned in the written HHA care plan; (e) any task for which the HHA has not been trained and competency-validated.

4.8 The HHA shall immediately report to the assigned RN any of the following: (a) changes in the patient's condition; (b) patient complaints; (c) refusal of care by the patient; (d) safety concerns in the home; (e) inability to complete assigned tasks; (f) any incident or injury during the visit.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 HHA Assignment and Care Plan Development

6.2 HHA Visit Execution

6.3 RN Supervisory Visits

6.4 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G270 (42 CFR § 484.80) will specifically: (a) review the 14-day supervisory visit schedule for compliance; (b) verify that written HHA care plans exist for every patient; (c) interview patients about HHA service quality; (d) interview HHAs about their understanding of the care plan and their reporting obligations; (e) verify that supervisory visits are conducted while the HHA is present. HHA supervision non-compliance is one of the top 5 most cited deficiencies nationally.

10.1 All HHAs shall receive training on agency-specific HHA service requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: scope of HHA practice; the HHA care plan; documentation requirements; the reportable events requirement; and infection control.

10.2 All RNs responsible for HHA supervision shall receive training on 42 CFR § 484.80 supervisory visit requirements within 14 calendar days of being assigned HHA supervisory responsibilities.

CL-SD-007 — Home Health Aide Competency Evaluation

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the initial and ongoing competency evaluation requirements for home health aides (HHAs) at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. per 42 CFR § 484.80(c). CMS requires that home health aides demonstrate competency in each of the skills listed in 42 CFR § 484.80(c)(1) before they are permitted to provide direct patient care, and that competency is re-evaluated at minimum annually. Competency evaluation failures that are not identified and remediated represent direct patient safety risks and are a frequent citation in CMS surveys. This policy ensures that every HHA employed by or contracted with the agency has been rigorously evaluated, that competency is maintained through ongoing assessment, and that HHAs who do not meet competency standards are not permitted to deliver patient care until remediation is complete.

3. Scope

This policy applies to all home health aides (employees and contracted staff), the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, all RNs conducting HHA competency evaluations, and HR staff managing competency records in personnel files per HR-WM-007.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every HHA shall complete an initial competency evaluation before being assigned to provide patient care. The initial evaluation shall cover all skill areas required by 42 CFR § 484.80(c)(1) including but not limited to: (a) communication skills; (b) observation, reporting, and documentation of patient status and services delivered; (c) reading and recording temperature, pulse, and respiration; (d) basic infection control procedures; (e) maintenance of a clean, safe, and healthy environment; (f) recognizing emergencies and knowledge of emergency procedures; (g) safe transfer and ambulation techniques; (h) range of motion and positioning; (i) adequate nutrition and fluid intake; (j) personal care services including bathing, skin care, hair care, nail care, oral hygiene, toileting, dressing, and feeding; (k) safe operation of medical equipment as assigned; (l) sensitivity to the patient's rights.

4.2 The competency evaluation shall include both a written (or oral) knowledge assessment and a direct observation of skills performance (return demonstration) conducted by a registered nurse. A passing score of 80% on the knowledge assessment and satisfactory performance on all return demonstration skills is required.

4.3 Competency shall be re-evaluated at minimum annually (every 12 calendar months) for every HHA. The annual re-evaluation shall include direct observation of skills in the home setting (during a supervisory visit) or in a simulated clinical setting, and shall cover all skill areas in Section 4.1.

4.4 If an HHA fails any component of the initial or annual competency evaluation, the HHA shall be immediately removed from patient care for the failed skill area, shall receive remediation training, and shall be re-evaluated within 14 calendar days. The HHA shall not resume patient care in the failed skill area until competency is demonstrated.

4.5 In addition to the annual evaluation, an HHA shall receive a targeted competency evaluation whenever: (a) a supervisory visit reveals a performance deficiency per CL-SD-006; (b) a patient complaint regarding the HHA's care quality is substantiated; (c) a new task is assigned that the HHA has not previously been evaluated on; (d) an incident or adverse event related to the HHA's care occurs.

4.6 All competency evaluation results — initial and ongoing — shall be documented and maintained in the HHA's personnel file per HR-WM-005 and HR-WM-007.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Initial Competency Evaluation

6.2 Annual Competency Re-Evaluation

6.3 Remediation Process

6.4 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

What Surveyors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G276 (42 CFR § 484.80(c)) will request HHA personnel files and verify: (a) that an initial competency evaluation was completed before the HHA began patient care; (b) that the evaluation covers all required skill areas; (c) that annual re-evaluations are documented; (d) that failed evaluations resulted in remediation and re-evaluation before return to care. Surveyors may also directly observe HHA skills during the survey and ask HHAs to describe their training and evaluation experience.

10.2 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days.

CL-SD-008 — Clinical Supervision & Oversight

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the requirements for clinical supervision of all professional and paraprofessional staff providing patient care at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Clinical supervision is the systematic process by which the agency ensures that all clinical services are delivered safely, competently, and in accordance with the plan of care, professional standards, and regulatory requirements. This policy establishes the supervision structure, frequency, documentation requirements, and escalation procedures for the oversight of RNs, LVNs, PTs, PTAs, OTs, COTAs, SLPs, MSWs, and HHAs. It implements the requirements of 42 CFR § 484.75 (skilled professional services) and 42 CFR § 484.80 (home health aide services) as they relate to clinical supervision.

3. Scope

This policy applies to the Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager, all clinical supervisors (RNs, PTs, OTs), all supervised staff (LVNs, PTAs, COTAs, HHAs), and all professional staff (RNs, PTs, OTs, SLPs, MSWs) subject to administrative and clinical oversight.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager is responsible for the oversight and supervision of all clinical services provided by the agency, as required by 42 CFR § 484.115(b). The Director of Nursing shall be a registered nurse who is currently licensed in California, meets the CMS qualification requirements, and has the authority and accountability to ensure the clinical quality and regulatory compliance of all services.

4.2 The supervision structure shall operate at three levels:

Level 1 — Director of Nursing Oversight: The Director of Nursing provides enterprise-level clinical oversight of all disciplines and clinical functions, including: review of clinical outcomes, quality indicators, documentation compliance, and survey readiness.

Level 2 — Discipline-Specific Professional Supervision: Licensed professionals (PTs, OTs) supervise their respective assistants (PTAs, COTAs) per discipline-specific practice act requirements. RNs supervise LVNs. These supervisory relationships are defined in the discipline-specific service policies (CL-SD-001 through CL-SD-007).

Level 3 — HHA Supervision: RNs (or therapists, when applicable) supervise HHAs per the 14-day supervisory visit schedule defined in CL-SD-006.

4.3 The Director of Nursing shall conduct a supervisory review of each clinical discipline at minimum monthly, covering: (a) documentation quality and timeliness; (b) plan of care adherence; (c) visit frequency compliance; (d) clinical outcomes and patient satisfaction; (e) competency concerns identified.

4.4 The Director of Nursing shall conduct a direct on-site supervisory visit with each clinician at minimum once during their first 60 days of employment and at least annually thereafter. For clinicians with identified performance concerns, the Director of Nursing shall increase the frequency of supervisory visits to no less than monthly until the concern is resolved.

4.5 All supervisory activities shall be documented including: the date, supervisor name, staff member supervised, observations made, findings, any corrective guidance provided, and any follow-up actions required.

4.6 If a supervisor identifies a clinical practice deficiency that poses a risk to patient safety, the supervisor shall: (a) immediately address the safety concern; (b) remove the staff member from the clinical situation if warranted; (c) report the event to the Director of Nursing within 4 hours; (d) initiate incident reporting per RM-ER-002 if patient harm has occurred or was narrowly avoided.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Director of Nursing Monthly Clinical Review

6.2 New Employee Clinical Supervision

6.3 Ongoing Professional Staff Supervision

6.4 LVN, PTA, and COTA Supervision

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

10.2 All supervisors shall receive training on their supervisory obligations per this policy and the applicable discipline-specific policy at hire and annually.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall acknowledge this policy within 14 calendar days.
