Version 6.0 | Effective Date: 2025-07-10

CL-CA-002 — OASIS Data Collection & Accuracy

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the standards for accurate, timely, and complete OASIS data collection in compliance with CMS requirements at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The Outcome and Assessment Information Set (OASIS) is not merely a documentation instrument — it is the financial, clinical, and regulatory backbone of the Medicare home health benefit. Every OASIS data element directly affects one or more of the following: the patient's PDGM payment group assignment (which determines how much Medicare pays the agency for the episode); the agency's publicly reported quality measures on Home Health Compare (which affect the agency's star ratings, referral relationships, and competitive standing); CMS survey compliance (inaccurate OASIS data is a direct deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.55); and the clinical validity of the agency's patient population data (which underpins every quality improvement decision the QAPI program makes).

OASIS accuracy failures are among the most consequential and most commonly cited deficiencies in home health. They occur along a spectrum: individual item errors driven by misunderstanding of CMS guidance; systematic coding patterns driven by financial pressure (upcoding to maximize payment); systematic undercoding driven by unfamiliarity with the instrument; and documentation inconsistencies where OASIS responses are not supported by the clinical narrative. All of these failure modes have regulatory, financial, and legal consequences. The False Claims Act imposes liability not only for fraudulent claims but for claims submitted with reckless disregard for their accuracy — a pattern of OASIS inaccuracy without a robust accuracy program may meet that standard.

This policy establishes the complete framework for OASIS data collection accuracy at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., incorporating: the clinical decision rules that govern response selection; the documentation standards that must support every response; the quality review process that validates accuracy before submission; the training and authorization program that ensures only competent assessors collect OASIS data; and the audit and correction process that identifies and resolves errors after submission. This policy implements 42 CFR § 484.55(b) — which requires that the comprehensive assessment accurately reflect the patient's current health status — and CMS Conditions of Participation requirements for OASIS data integrity under 42 CFR § 484.20.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses (RNs) authorized to conduct comprehensive assessments and collect OASIS data

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for OASIS quality oversight

The OASIS Quality Reviewer (a designated RN with advanced OASIS training who conducts pre-submission and post-submission OASIS audits)

OASIS submission staff responsible for transmitting data to the CMS OASIS repository

The QAPI Coordinator who uses OASIS-derived quality measures for performance improvement

Revenue Cycle staff whose payment calculations depend on accurate OASIS data

This policy does not apply to non-RN clinical staff (LVNs, therapists, HHAs), who do not collect OASIS data. It governs all OASIS time points: SOC, ROC, RECERT, FU (SCIC), TRN, and DC.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 All OASIS data elements shall be collected by a registered nurse who has completed the agency's OASIS Competency Program per CL-CA-001 and is listed on the current OASIS Authorized Assessor Roster. No OASIS data element shall be collected, modified, or finalized by any non-RN clinical staff member, administrative staff, revenue cycle personnel, or billing coder, regardless of the purpose of the modification.

4.2 Every OASIS response shall be selected using the current CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual as the definitive authority for all coding decisions. When agency-developed coding guidance conflicts with CMS guidance, CMS guidance governs. Agency-created coding rules, internal coding conventions, or OASIS "cheat sheets" that deviate from or contradict CMS guidance are prohibited.

4.3 Every OASIS response shall be supported by documented clinical evidence in the patient's clinical record. The evidentiary hierarchy for OASIS coding shall follow CL-OA-006 (Documentation Hierarchy and Evidence Source Prioritization). An OASIS response that is not supported by documented clinical evidence is an unsupported response and constitutes a data integrity failure, regardless of whether the response happens to be clinically plausible.

4.4 OASIS data shall reflect the patient's actual clinical status at the time of the specific assessment encounter — not the patient's best possible performance, not the patient's worst-case scenario, not an average of performances across recent visits, and not an extrapolation from prior assessments. Every OASIS assessment is a contemporaneous point-in-time assessment per CL-OA-009.

4.5 The agency shall implement a multi-level OASIS quality review process: (a) a self-review by the assessing RN using the Pre-Submission Quality Review Checklist per CL-OA-019 before locking the assessment; (b) a designated OASIS Quality Reviewer review of all SOC assessments within 5 business days of locking; (c) a monthly random sample OASIS audit by the Director of Nursing covering ≥5% of all submitted assessments per CL-OA-002; (d) quarterly OASIS accuracy trending analysis presented to the QAPI Committee.

4.6 The agency shall maintain an OASIS data accuracy rate — defined as the percentage of audited OASIS items where the agency's response matches the correct response as determined by the OASIS Quality Reviewer — of ≥95% as the minimum acceptable standard. An accuracy rate below 95% on any audited item category shall trigger a Performance Improvement Project per QA-PI-001.

4.7 Any OASIS response that is identified as inaccurate, whether before or after submission, shall be corrected per the procedures established in CL-CA-003 (OASIS Transmission & Correction). All corrections shall be documented with the reason for the change and the clinical evidence supporting the corrected response.

4.8 Financial pressure, revenue targets, payment optimization goals, or any external influence on OASIS response selection are explicitly prohibited. OASIS responses shall reflect the patient's actual clinical status — the payment consequence of an accurate response is never a justification for an inaccurate one.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 OASIS Data Collection — General Requirements

6.2 Domain-Specific OASIS Accuracy Standards

The following domain-specific accuracy standards shall govern response selection for the highest-risk OASIS item categories. These standards supplement the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual and do not supersede it.

6.2.1 Functional Status — GG Items (GG0130, GG0170)

6.2.2 Wound Assessment — M1311–M1350

6.2.3 Behavioral Health — M1700–M1750

6.2.4 Medication Management — M2001, M2003, M2010

6.2.5 Homebound Status and Eligibility

6.3 Pre-Submission Self-Review

6.4 OASIS Quality Reviewer Review — Post-Lock, Pre-Submission

6.5 Monthly OASIS Accuracy Audit

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors conducting an OASIS validation survey (specifically targeting OASIS accuracy under 42 CFR § 484.20 and § 484.55) will:

Select a sample of submitted OASIS assessments and independently re-assess the patients — or review the clinical record — to determine whether the submitted responses accurately reflected the patient's status at the time of the assessment. Surveyors are trained in OASIS and will identify item-specific errors.

Compare OASIS responses to narrative documentation — for every item reviewed, they will look for a narrative entry that supports the coded response. A response of "2 — Substantial/Maximal Assistance" for a GG item requires a narrative description of the patient performing the activity with that level of assistance.

Look for patterns of inaccuracy — a single item error may be cited as a standard-level deficiency; a systematic pattern of the same item being incorrectly coded across multiple patients suggests a systemic training or oversight failure and may be cited as a Condition-level deficiency.

Request the agency's OASIS quality review documentation — they will look for evidence that the agency has an active OASIS accuracy program, including Quality Reviewer documentation, accuracy audit results, and clinician feedback records.

Review the Authorized Assessor Roster — they will verify that OASIS items were completed by authorized RNs.

MAC Additional Documentation Request (ADR) auditors will specifically examine OASIS functional status items (GG section) and clinical severity items because these drive PDGM payment classification. Items coded at higher severity levels than the clinical record supports are the primary target of False Claims Act enforcement in home health.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All RNs authorized to conduct OASIS assessments shall complete the OASIS Competency Program per CL-CA-001 before independent assessment. The program shall include OASIS-specific training covering all high-risk item categories (GG functional, wound, behavioral health, medication management) with item-level decision-rule training per the OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual.

10.2 Annual OASIS accuracy refresher training shall be provided to all authorized RN assessors, incorporating: (a) any changes to the OASIS data set or CMS guidance since the prior training; (b) the agency's OASIS accuracy audit findings from the prior year, including the most common errors and corrective guidance; (c) OASIS response selection decision-practice exercises using case scenarios.

10.3 The OASIS Quality Reviewer shall complete advanced OASIS training (OASIS specialist certification or equivalent) and shall maintain current knowledge of CMS guidance updates. The Director of Nursing shall verify the OASIS Quality Reviewer's qualifications annually.

10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix D) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

11. Version Control

11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.

11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."

11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.

11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

CL-CA-003 — OASIS Transmission & Correction

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the requirements and timeframes for OASIS data transmission to the CMS OASIS repository and establishes the procedures for error identification and correction at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. OASIS transmission is the formal act by which the agency discharges its CMS-mandated obligation to report patient assessment data — and its failure is an absolute regulatory deficiency. There is no clinical, operational, or financial justification that excuses non-transmission or late transmission of OASIS data. Under 42 CFR § 484.20, CMS requires that all Medicare-certified home health agencies transmit OASIS data to the national repository within defined timeframes, and CMS conditions the agency's Medicare certification on ongoing compliance with this requirement. An agency that fails to transmit OASIS data loses its ability to receive Medicare payment for the affected episodes. An agency that transmits systematically inaccurate OASIS data — and fails to correct it — is generating distorted quality measures that misrepresent the care it provides to patients, its referral sources, and the public.

This policy establishes the complete transmission workflow: who is responsible for transmission, how transmission is performed, how transmission success is confirmed, how transmission failures are identified and resolved, how post-submission corrections are made when data errors are discovered, and how the agency maintains a complete, auditable record of all OASIS submissions and corrections. This policy implements 42 CFR § 484.20 (Reporting OASIS Information) and CMS OASIS data submission technical specifications.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

The designated OASIS Transmission Coordinator (the staff member responsible for managing the agency's OASIS data submission to CMS)

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager who provides clinical oversight of the transmission and correction process

The OASIS Quality Reviewer who identifies errors requiring correction

All authorized RN assessors whose assessments are subject to transmission and correction

IT staff supporting the EHR-to-CMS transmission infrastructure

The Compliance Officer who oversees OASIS data integrity

4. Policy Statement

4.1 All OASIS assessments shall be transmitted to the CMS OASIS national repository within the timeframes specified in Section 6.2. There are no exceptions to the CMS transmission deadline for Medicare-certified agencies.

4.2 The agency shall designate a qualified OASIS Transmission Coordinator who is responsible for: initiating and confirming all OASIS transmissions; monitoring the CMS response files for acceptance, rejection, and warning notifications; maintaining the agency's OASIS submission log; and escalating any transmission failure or rejection to the Director of Nursing within 24 hours.

4.3 Transmission shall be performed using the agency's certified OASIS submission software, connected to the CMS OASIS repository through the approved state data collection system or CMS direct submission pathway. The agency shall maintain current software licenses and system certifications required for OASIS transmission.

4.4 Every transmission shall be confirmed — the agency shall not assume transmission was successful without verifying the CMS acceptance response file. A transmission without a confirmed CMS acceptance is not a completed transmission regardless of whether the data was sent.

4.5 When a CMS response file identifies rejected records or warning messages, the OASIS Transmission Coordinator shall investigate and resolve every rejection within 5 business days of receipt of the response file. Rejected records shall be corrected and resubmitted within 5 business days of rejection identification.

4.6 When an error is identified in a submitted OASIS record — whether through the agency's internal audit process per CL-CA-002, through a CMS OASIS validation finding, through an ADR audit finding, or through any other means — the agency shall initiate the OASIS correction process within 5 business days of identifying the error. All corrections shall be clinically justified, documented, and processed through the CMS correction pathway.

4.7 OASIS data shall not be corrected for any reason other than to improve the accuracy of the record relative to the patient's actual clinical status at the time of the assessment. Revenue optimization, payment correction, or any other financial motivation for a correction is prohibited. All corrections shall be clinically justified in writing before processing.

4.8 The agency shall maintain a complete OASIS Submission Log documenting every transmission including: date of transmission, time point, number of records transmitted, CMS response status, any rejected records and the reason for rejection, corrections made, and resubmission dates.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 OASIS Data Preparation for Transmission

6.2 OASIS Transmission Deadlines and Process

6.3 CMS Response File Processing

6.4 OASIS Correction Process

6.5 Transmission Failure Response

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors will request the agency's OASIS Submission Log and verify that all assessments for the survey look-back period were transmitted within the 30-day deadline. They will also verify that the agency has a CMS response file review process and that rejections were resolved. Surveyors will specifically look for evidence of correction requests that appear financially motivated — corrections that always result in higher payment groups are a red flag for upcoding.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 The OASIS Transmission Coordinator shall receive training on the OASIS submission software, CMS data specifications, response file processing, rejection resolution, and correction procedures at hire and whenever the software or CMS specifications are updated.

10.2 The Director of Nursing and OASIS Quality Reviewer shall receive training on the correction justification requirements and the financial implications of corrections at hire and annually.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix D) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire.

CL-CA-004 — Recertification Assessment & Process

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the assessment, documentation, and physician approval process for recertification of home health eligibility at each 60-day certification period at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Recertification is simultaneously the most critically time-constrained clinical process in home health and the most consequential for both the patient and the agency's revenue integrity. A recertification that is not completed within the Day 56–60 window is a CMS compliance failure. A recertification that is completed but not supported by documented clinical findings of continued eligibility is a billing compliance failure. A recertification that is completed on time and documented but for which the physician signature is not obtained before the next episode's claims are submitted is a False Claims Act exposure.

The recertification assessment is not merely an administrative exercise — it is a genuine, comprehensive clinical assessment of the patient's current status, the legitimacy of their continued home health eligibility, and the appropriateness of the proposed plan for the next certification period. Every recertification must answer three questions with documented clinical evidence: (1) Does this patient continue to meet the Medicare homebound criteria? (2) Does this patient continue to require skilled services? (3) Is the proposed plan of care for the next period clinically appropriate and achievable? If any of these three questions cannot be answered affirmatively with documented clinical evidence, the patient shall not be recertified. This policy implements 42 CFR § 424.22(b) (recertification requirements for Medicare home health) and CMS recertification standards in the Home Health Benefit Manual, Chapter 7, § 30.2.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses conducting recertification assessments

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for recertification oversight

Clinical Coordinators managing the recertification tracking schedule per CL-CP-008

Physicians and allowed practitioners who certify continued eligibility

Medical Records staff managing recertification documentation

Revenue Cycle staff whose billing depends on completed recertifications

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every active home health patient receiving Medicare home health services shall be formally recertified for each subsequent 60-day certification period. There is no mechanism by which a patient may continue to receive Medicare home health services — and the agency may continue to bill for those services — without a completed, physician-signed recertification.

4.2 The recertification assessment — including all applicable OASIS data elements for the RECERT time point — shall be completed by a qualified RN within the Day 56–60 window of the current certification period. This 5-day window is defined by CMS and is non-negotiable: an assessment completed before Day 56 or after Day 60 is not a valid RECERT assessment.

4.3 The recertification assessment shall be a genuine, comprehensive re-evaluation of the patient's current clinical status — not a copy-forward or update of the SOC assessment, and not a perfunctory documentation exercise. Every RECERT OASIS item shall reflect the patient's actual status at the time of the RECERT assessment.

4.4 The recertification shall document, with patient-specific clinical evidence: (a) the patient's continued homebound status and the specific functional limitations that support the homebound determination; (b) the continued need for skilled services and why those services remain medically necessary; (c) the patient's progress (or lack thereof) during the current certification period; (d) the proposed plan of care for the next certification period including updated diagnoses, services, goals, and physician orders; (e) the clinical rationale for the proposed visit frequency in the next period.

4.5 If the recertification assessment reveals that the patient no longer meets the Medicare homebound criteria or no longer requires skilled services, the patient shall not be recertified. The clinician shall document the findings, notify the physician, and initiate discharge planning per CL-CP-006.

4.6 The recertification plan of care shall be transmitted to the certifying physician for signature no later than the last day of the current certification period. Physician signature tracking shall be maintained per CL-CP-008 and CL-CP-009. No Medicare claim for the new certification period shall be submitted without a physician-signed recertification on file.

4.7 Recertification assessments shall not be conducted more than 5 days before the start of the assessment window (Day 56) or at any time after the current certification period has expired (after Day 60). An assessment conducted outside the Day 56–60 window is not a valid RECERT assessment and must be submitted as a different time point or corrected per CL-CA-003.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Recertification Scheduling and Tracking

6.2 Recertification Assessment — Content and Conduct

6.3 Recertification Narrative Requirements

The recertification narrative is a required component of the recertification plan of care and shall contain the following elements, each documented with patient-specific clinical content:

6.4 Non-Recertification — When Recertification Is Not Appropriate

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

Surveyors will request all RECERT assessments for the look-back period and verify: the assessment date falls within the Day 56–60 window; the homebound and skilled need documentation is patient-specific and current; the recertification plan reflects the patient's actual current status; and the physician-signed recertification is on file. ADR auditors will cross-reference the RECERT OASIS responses against the visit note narratives from the last 30 days of the current period to verify that the RECERT accurately reflects the patient's trajectory.

CL-CA-005 — Homebound Status Determination & Documentation

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the criteria and documentation requirements for determining and verifying patient homebound status per CMS guidelines at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Homebound status is the gating criterion for Medicare home health eligibility — a patient who is not homebound is categorically ineligible for the Medicare home health benefit, regardless of how much skilled care they need or how medically complex they are. If a patient is not homebound and the agency bills Medicare for home health services, the claim is false. There is no amount of skilled need, physician documentation, or clinical good faith that makes a non-homebound patient billable.

Yet homebound determination is also one of the most consistently misunderstood, inconsistently documented, and frequently litigated aspects of home health compliance. ADR auditors deny claims for homebound documentation failures more frequently than for almost any other reason. The most common failures are: homebound documentation that is generic and not patient-specific; homebound documentation that is copied forward from the SOC without re-evaluation; homebound documentation that fails to address the "considerable and taxing effort" standard; and homebound documentation that focuses on the patient's diagnosis rather than their specific functional limitations. This policy establishes a clear, operationally precise framework for homebound determination and documentation that eliminates these failure modes. It implements 42 CFR § 409.42(a) (homebound criteria), the CMS Home Health Benefit Manual Chapter 7, § 30.1, and the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual homebound documentation requirements.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses conducting comprehensive assessments, recertification assessments, and all OASIS time point assessments

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for homebound documentation quality

The OASIS Quality Reviewer who validates homebound documentation at each assessment review

Revenue Cycle staff relying on homebound documentation to support billing

The Compliance Officer who oversees homebound documentation integrity

4. Policy Statement

4.1 A patient is homebound for purposes of Medicare home health eligibility if and only if both of the following criteria are met simultaneously: (a) Criterion 1 — Condition-Based Normal Inability to Leave Home: Due to illness or injury, the patient has a condition that restricts their ability to leave the home. This means the patient has a medical condition that would normally cause an individual to be confined to the home. A person who is not confined, or is confined only for non-medical reasons (e.g., lack of transportation, personal preference, caregiver unavailability), does not meet the homebound criteria. (b) Criterion 2 — Considerable and Taxing Effort: Leaving the home requires a considerable and taxing effort by the patient. The effort required must be caused by the patient's medical condition — not merely inconvenient or uncomfortable, but genuinely demanding due to the patient's illness or injury.

4.2 Both criteria must be met and both must be documented with patient-specific clinical language at every applicable assessment time point. Documentation that satisfies Criterion 1 but not Criterion 2, or Criterion 2 but not Criterion 1, is insufficient homebound documentation.

4.3 Homebound status does not mean the patient may never leave the home. A patient may leave the home for medical appointments, adult day care, religious services, or for other infrequent reasons and remain homebound — provided that leaving the home requires a considerable and taxing effort due to their medical condition.

4.4 Homebound status shall be assessed and documented at SOC, at each RECERT, and at any OASIS time point where homebound status is applicable. A homebound determination made at SOC does not automatically remain valid for subsequent periods — each period requires an independent homebound assessment.

4.5 Homebound documentation shall be written in patient-specific, functional, clinical language that describes: (a) the specific medical condition causing the normal inability to leave home; (b) the specific functional limitations that make leaving home require considerable and taxing effort; (c) the patient's current level of functional performance that supports the homebound determination. Generic, diagnosis-only, or template-driven homebound documentation is not sufficient.

4.6 The following factors are acceptable bases for homebound status when they create the conditions defined in Section 4.1: inability to ambulate without assistance of another person or an assistive device; dyspnea severe enough to restrict activity to short distances within the home; severe weakness, fatigue, or exercise intolerance; pain that significantly limits the patient's ability to leave the home; cognitive impairment that prevents safe unsupervised community mobility; paralysis or severe neurological deficits; post-surgical restrictions limiting mobility; psychiatric conditions that prevent leaving the home safely.

4.7 The following circumstances, standing alone, do not support homebound status: patient preference to remain at home; lack of transportation; caregiver unavailability; a diagnosis that sounds severe but does not functionally restrict the patient's ability to leave home; advanced age without functional limitation.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Homebound Determination at SOC

6.2 Homebound Assessment at Subsequent Time Points

6.3 Homebound Documentation Standards

All homebound documentation shall include the following elements, written with patient-specific clinical content:

6.4 Homebound Status Monitoring During the Episode

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

ADR auditors examining homebound documentation will look for: patient-specific language describing both CMS criteria; evidence that the documentation was freshly assessed at each time point; visit note entries that corroborate the homebound determination (e.g., visit notes describing the patient's functional limitations during visits are evidence of homebound status; visit notes describing the patient as "doing well" without any functional limitation documentation undermine it). Surveyors conducting standard CMS surveys will interview patients about their community mobility patterns and compare the patient's description to the homebound documentation in the clinical record.

CL-CA-006 — Face-to-Face Encounter Compliance

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the requirements for physician or allowed practitioner face-to-face (F2F) encounters for home health patients at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., per 42 CFR § 424.22. The face-to-face encounter requirement was established by the Affordable Care Act and implemented by CMS to reinforce physician oversight of home health services and to ensure that the certifying physician has directly assessed the patient within a defined timeframe of the home health episode's initiation. The F2F requirement is a threshold condition for Medicare home health billing: if the required F2F encounter is not present, properly documented, and appropriately timed, the agency's claims for that episode are not payable — regardless of the clinical validity of the care delivered.

The F2F requirement creates three distinct compliance obligations: (1) the encounter itself must have occurred within the required timeframe; (2) the encounter must have been conducted by a qualifying practitioner; and (3) the documentation of the encounter must meet the CMS content requirements. Failure in any one of these three dimensions is sufficient to render the episode's claims non-payable. This policy addresses all three dimensions with operational precision, establishing clear accountability for each element of the F2F compliance process, integrating F2F tracking into the agency's clinical workflow, and defining the escalation process when the F2F requirement is at risk of not being met. This policy implements 42 CFR § 424.22(a)(1)(iv) and CMS Home Health Benefit Manual, Chapter 7, § 30.5.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses conducting SOC assessments who are responsible for verifying F2F compliance at the initiation of each episode

Clinical Coordinators responsible for F2F tracking per CL-CA-007

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for F2F compliance oversight

Physicians and allowed practitioners who conduct F2F encounters and provide required documentation

Revenue Cycle staff who must verify F2F compliance before claim submission

Medical Records staff who obtain and file F2F documentation

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every patient admitted to Medicare home health services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall have a qualifying face-to-face encounter documented and on file before the agency submits a final claim for the episode. The F2F encounter is a condition of Medicare payment — not a documentation preference.

4.2 The F2F encounter must have occurred within the following CMS-defined timeframe: no more than 90 calendar days before the SOC date, or no more than 30 calendar days after the SOC date. An encounter outside this window does not satisfy the F2F requirement, regardless of its clinical content.

4.3 The F2F encounter must be conducted by a qualifying practitioner — a physician (MD or DO), nurse practitioner (NP), clinical nurse specialist (CNS), or physician assistant (PA) working in collaboration with a physician. The encounter must have occurred in a context where the practitioner directly evaluated the patient — a telephone consultation is not a qualifying F2F encounter.

4.4 The certifying physician must review and sign the certification statement that includes the F2F encounter attestation. The certifying physician may not be the same individual who owns or directly supervises the home health agency (Stark Law prohibition).

4.5 The F2F encounter documentation must include: the date of the encounter; the practitioner's clinical findings that support the home health plan of care; and attestation that the encounter occurred and was related to the reason the patient requires home health care. The documentation must be authored by the practitioner who conducted the encounter — the agency's clinical notes cannot substitute for the practitioner's own documentation.

4.6 The agency shall not coach, instruct, or pressure physicians or their staff to write specific language in F2F documentation. The agency may provide educational materials explaining the CMS content requirements to physicians as a general resource, but shall not draft F2F documentation on behalf of physicians or request language that is not supported by the physician's independent clinical assessment.

4.7 The agency shall track F2F compliance for every active episode using the F2F Tracking System per CL-CA-007. No final claim shall be submitted for any episode without confirmed, compliant F2F documentation on file.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 F2F Identification at Intake

6.2 F2F Documentation Acquisition

6.3 F2F for Post-SOC Encounters (Encounter After SOC Date)

6.4 F2F Compliance Verification Before Claim Submission

6.5 Telehealth F2F Encounters

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

ADR auditors examining F2F compliance will: verify that F2F documentation exists for the episode; verify the encounter date falls within the F2F window; verify the practitioner is a qualifying practitioner; and verify the documentation contains the required clinical findings and attestation. The absence of a qualifying practitioner's note — and substitution of the agency's clinical documentation — is an immediate denial. Surveyors will also verify that the certifying physician does not have a prohibited financial relationship with the agency.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All clinical coordinators and medical records staff involved in F2F documentation acquisition shall receive training on the F2F requirements at hire and annually, including: the F2F window calculation; qualifying practitioner requirements; documentation content requirements; and the billing hold process.

10.2 All revenue cycle staff shall receive training on F2F pre-bill verification requirements at hire and annually.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days.

CL-CA-007 — Face-to-Face Encounter Tracking & Expiration Monitoring

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines the operational tracking system for face-to-face (F2F) encounter documentation at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., including status dashboards, expiration alerts, escalation timelines, and accountability for preventing lapses that result in claim denials. The clinical requirements for F2F compliance are defined in CL-CA-006. This policy provides the operational infrastructure that makes those requirements executable — the tracking system that ensures no F2F deadline is missed, no compliant documentation requirement slips through without verification, and no claim is submitted without the billing team having confirmed F2F compliance on a patient-by-patient, episode-by-episode basis.

Without a systematic F2F tracking program, F2F compliance depends on the individual memory and diligence of intake staff, medical records coordinators, and revenue cycle staff — an inherently unreliable system that scales poorly as the agency's patient census grows. The F2F tracking system described in this policy replaces individual reliance with a structured, automated, status-visible workflow that generates alerts at defined milestones, assigns accountability at each stage, and creates an auditable record of every F2F compliance action taken for every episode.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

Clinical Coordinators who manage the F2F Tracking System daily

Medical Records staff who obtain and verify F2F documentation

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager who reviews F2F compliance status weekly

Revenue Cycle staff who perform pre-bill F2F verification

The Compliance Officer who oversees F2F compliance integrity

4. Policy Statement

4.1 The agency shall maintain a centralized F2F Tracking System (electronic, within the EHR or a dedicated tracking tool) that captures and displays the F2F compliance status of every active home health episode in real time.

4.2 Every new episode shall be entered into the F2F Tracking System within 24 hours of the SOC date, with all required data fields per Section 6.1. No episode shall be excluded from the tracking system.

4.3 The F2F Tracking System shall generate automated alerts at the following milestones: (a) Day 20 post-SOC — if F2F documentation has not been received and a post-SOC encounter is being relied upon to satisfy the requirement; (b) Day 25 post-SOC — escalation alert to Director of Nursing if F2F documentation is still not received; (c) Day 28 post-SOC — Administrator escalation if F2F documentation is still not received; (d) 5 business days after any SOC — alert if F2F documentation request has not been initiated.

4.4 The F2F Tracking System shall maintain a clear, real-time status display for every episode, categorized as: (a) F2F Compliant — qualifying encounter confirmed within window; compliant documentation received and filed; (b) F2F Pending — qualifying encounter identified; documentation requested but not yet received; (c) F2F At Risk — Day 20+ post-SOC; post-SOC encounter not yet occurred or documentation not received; (d) F2F Non-Compliant — window has closed without a qualifying encounter; claims for this episode shall not be submitted.

4.5 The Director of Nursing shall review the F2F Tracking System dashboard at minimum weekly and shall report F2F compliance status to the Administrator monthly and to the Governing Body quarterly.

4.6 Revenue Cycle shall not submit any claim for any episode that is not in F2F Compliant status in the tracking system. The F2F Tracking System status shall be a mandatory field in the pre-bill checklist.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 F2F Tracking System — New Episode Entry

6.2 F2F Tracking System — Daily Management

6.3 Milestone Alert Management and Escalation

6.4 Documentation Compliance Verification

6.5 Weekly Director of Nursing Review

6.6 Pre-Bill F2F Verification Integration

6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

ADR auditors will request the agency's F2F documentation for each episode under review. A robust, auditable F2F Tracking System demonstrates to auditors that the agency has an active F2F compliance program — not that the agency simply got lucky finding documentation when asked. Surveyors will also verify that the pre-bill verification process is active and documented.

8.3 Common Failure Points

10.2 Revenue Cycle staff shall receive training on the pre-bill F2F verification requirement at hire and annually.

CL-CD-001 — Clinical Documentation Standards

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the minimum standards for clinical documentation content, timeliness, accuracy, and authentication for all clinical records at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Clinical documentation is not administrative paperwork — it is the legal, clinical, and financial record of every professional act performed for every patient on every visit. It is simultaneously: the clinical record that governs the next clinician's decisions; the legal record that defines the agency's liability in litigation; the regulatory record that determines whether a CMS surveyor finds compliance or a deficiency; the billing record that justifies every Medicare payment; and the quality record that drives every QAPI improvement decision.

Documentation failures in home health are the single most consequential category of compliance failure. An undocumented clinical finding might as well not have occurred — legally and regulatorily, it did not. A visit note that does not demonstrate the skilled nature of the services provided is not a billable visit under Medicare, regardless of what the clinician actually did. A plan of care modification that is not reflected in the clinical record may be a billing compliance failure. Documentation that is not authenticated within required timeframes is not a completed record. Documentation that is altered without proper notation is a falsification. Documentation that contradicts itself across the record undermines the entire episode's clinical and billing integrity.

This policy establishes the universal clinical documentation standards that govern every clinical record entry at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., regardless of the discipline creating the entry, the type of visit, or the service delivered. Discipline-specific documentation requirements that supplement these universal standards are addressed in the respective discipline policies (CL-SD-001 through CL-SD-025) and the OASIS-specific documentation requirements are addressed in CL-OA-001 through CL-OA-019. This policy implements 42 CFR § 484.110 (Condition of Participation: Clinical Records) and CMS documentation standards established in the Home Health Benefit Manual and State Operations Manual.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses (RNs) creating clinical record entries

All licensed vocational nurses (LVNs) creating clinical record entries

All physical therapists (PTs) and physical therapist assistants (PTAs) creating clinical record entries

All occupational therapists (OTs) and certified occupational therapy assistants (COTAs) creating clinical record entries

All speech-language pathologists (SLPs) creating clinical record entries

All medical social workers (MSWs) creating clinical record entries

All home health aides (HHAs) creating visit documentation

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for documentation quality oversight

Medical Records staff responsible for clinical record management

The Compliance Officer responsible for documentation integrity oversight

This policy governs all entries in the patient's clinical record — visit notes, assessment documents, care coordination notes, physician communication notes, OASIS assessments, plan of care documents, and any other record that becomes part of the patient's permanent clinical record.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every entry in a patient's clinical record shall be: accurate — reflecting what was actually observed, assessed, performed, and communicated; complete — containing all required elements for the type of entry; timely — created within the timeframe specified for the entry type; legible — clearly readable in the format used (electronic or written); authenticated — identified with the author's name, credential, date, and time; and consistent — aligned with all related entries in the clinical record.

4.2 Every clinical record entry shall be individualized to the specific patient, the specific visit, and the specific clinical situation. Template-driven documentation that does not reflect the actual clinical encounter is prohibited. Copy-forward of prior entries without independent verification and documentation of the current status is prohibited, except as specifically authorized by the EHR workflow and where the copied content is explicitly verified as current at the time of copying.

4.3 Every visit note shall demonstrate the skilled nature of the service provided — specifically documenting why the service required the training, skill, and judgment of a licensed clinician. Visit notes that describe only routine observations, vital signs, or equipment checks without demonstrating clinical reasoning, skilled assessment, or skilled intervention do not support Medicare billing for skilled services.

4.4 Clinical record entries shall be made promptly after the visit or clinical event — the agency's maximum timeframe for visit note completion and locking is 24 hours after the visit, as defined in CL-CD-004. Notes made significantly after the clinical event are subject to the late entry standards defined in CO-DC-003.

4.5 All clinical record entries shall be authenticated by the clinician who performed the service. Authentication means the clinician signs the entry (physically or electronically) with their full name and credential (e.g., Jane Smith, RN), and the date and time of the entry are documented. Unsigned entries are not valid record entries.

4.6 Corrections to clinical record entries shall be made using the standards defined in CO-DC-003 (Late Entry, Correction & Amendment Standards). No entry shall be obliterated, deleted, or altered in a manner that conceals the original content. Electronic record alterations shall maintain an audit trail showing the original content, the change, the date, time, and identity of the person making the change.

4.7 The clinical record is the agency's primary tool for demonstrating the medical necessity, skilled nature, and appropriateness of all services billed to Medicare. Revenue Cycle shall not submit a claim for any visit or service that is not supported by a completed, authenticated, and medically necessary visit note in the patient's clinical record.

4.8 All clinical record entries shall respect patient privacy and confidentiality per HIPAA requirements and agency policy CO-HP-001. Entries shall not contain information about other patients, providers, or third parties that is not directly relevant to the patient's care.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 General Clinical Documentation Requirements — All Disciplines

6.2 Demonstrating Skilled Care in Documentation

This section provides the agency's standards for documentation that demonstrates the skilled nature of clinical services. These standards supplement the discipline-specific documentation requirements in CL-SD-001 through CL-SD-025.

6.3 Documentation Integrity Standards

6.4 Visit Note Authentication

6.5 Director of Nursing Documentation Quality Oversight

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G328 (42 CFR § 484.110) will examine the clinical record for: completeness and accuracy; authentication of all entries; timeliness of completion; evidence of individualization; internal consistency; and the presence of clinical reasoning demonstrating that services were skilled. ADR auditors will specifically examine whether visit notes demonstrate medical necessity and skilled care. A visit note that reads as a maintenance visit — without clinical assessment, clinical reasoning, or skilled intervention documentation — is a denial waiting to happen.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All clinical staff shall receive clinical documentation standards training within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: the universal required elements for visit notes; skilled documentation standards with examples from the agency's own audit findings; prohibited practices (copy-forward, pre-dating, falsification, unapproved abbreviations); the correction and late entry process per CO-DC-003; authentication requirements; and the consequences of documentation failures for billing and compliance.

10.2 All new clinical hires shall complete a supervised first visit with documentation review by the Director of Nursing or a designated preceptor before independently submitting documentation. The preceptor shall review the first visit note against the standards in this policy and provide written feedback before the new clinician's note is authenticated and locked.

10.3 The Approved Abbreviation List (Appendix A) shall be distributed to all clinical staff at hire and whenever the list is updated. All clinical staff shall acknowledge receipt and understanding of the approved abbreviations.

10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

10.5 The Director of Nursing shall maintain training completion records for all clinical staff and shall report non-compliance to the Administrator within 7 calendar days of any training deadline.

11. Version Control

11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.

11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy, as reflected in the policy header, is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."

11.3 Any substantive revision to this policy requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days of the revised effective date; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.

11.4 Non-substantive revisions (formatting, typographical corrections, updated cross-references) may be approved by the Administrator with notification to the Governing Body at the next regular meeting.

CL-CD-002 — Clinical Record Content & Organization

1. Policy Header

Field	Value

Policy ID	CL-CD-002

Policy Title	Clinical Record Content & Organization

Domain	CL — Clinical Operations

Subdomain	CD — Clinical Documentation

Classification Tier	REQUIRED

Status	ACTIVE

Review Cycle	Annual

Access Tier	Tier 2 — Restricted

Policy Owner / Steward	Director of Nursing

Version	1.0

Approved By	Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.

Last Reviewed	2025-07-10

Next Review Date	2026-07-10

Supersedes	N/A (Initial Version)

2. Purpose

This policy defines the minimum required content, organization standards, and retention requirements for patient clinical records at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The clinical record is the authoritative repository of everything that was done for and to a patient during their home health episode — every assessment, every visit, every physician order, every clinical communication, every OASIS data submission, every plan of care decision, and every clinical outcome. The clinical record serves five simultaneous and equally critical functions: (1) it is the clinical communication tool that informs every clinician's next decision for this patient; (2) it is the legal record that governs the agency's liability in litigation, administrative proceedings, and government investigations; (3) it is the regulatory compliance record that CMS surveyors review to determine whether the agency meets the Conditions of Participation; (4) it is the billing support document that justifies every Medicare and Medicaid claim submitted; and (5) it is the quality data source from which the agency's QAPI program draws its evidence base.

A clinical record that is missing required components is incomplete — and an incomplete clinical record creates a presumption of non-compliance during survey and a presumption of unsupported billing during ADR audit. A clinical record that is disorganized — where content cannot be found quickly by a surveyor, an auditor, or a treating clinician — creates the same risk as missing content: the record cannot serve its function if its contents cannot be located. A clinical record that is retained for a shorter period than legally required destroys evidence that may be needed years later in litigation, audit, or quality review.

This policy establishes the definitive standard for what must be in every patient's clinical record, how it must be organized, how it must be protected, and how long it must be retained. It implements 42 CFR § 484.110 — Condition of Participation: Clinical Records — which requires that the agency maintain a clinical record for each patient that contains all pertinent diagnoses, medications, treatment, and discharge information, and that the record be complete, promptly completed, organized, and retained per CMS requirements.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses, licensed vocational nurses, physical therapists, occupational therapists, speech-language pathologists, medical social workers, and home health aides creating clinical record entries

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for clinical record oversight

Medical Records staff responsible for record organization, storage, access management, and retention

The Compliance Officer responsible for record security, privacy, and retention compliance

Revenue Cycle staff who access clinical records to support billing

IT staff responsible for EHR system maintenance and data security

Any contracted entity that has access to or maintains agency clinical records on behalf of the agency

This policy governs every patient's clinical record — active and inactive, electronic and paper — for every episode of home health care provided by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a complete, organized, and readily accessible clinical record for every patient admitted to home health services. The clinical record shall begin at the time of patient intake and shall be maintained throughout the episode and for the retention period specified in Section 7 of this policy.

4.2 Every patient clinical record shall contain, at minimum, all content elements specified in Section 6.1 of this policy. The absence of any required element constitutes an incomplete clinical record — a deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.110 and a billing compliance risk.

4.3 Clinical records shall be organized in a consistent, standardized manner that enables any authorized clinician, surveyor, or auditor to locate any specific document category within the record within a reasonable time. The agency shall establish and enforce a standardized record organization structure for all electronic clinical records in the EHR.

4.4 Clinical records shall be legible, accurately written, promptly completed, and authenticated per the standards established in CL-CD-001, CL-CD-003, and CL-CD-004.

4.5 Access to clinical records shall be restricted to authorized individuals — specifically, those with a legitimate need to access the record for clinical, administrative, billing, compliance, or legal purposes. Access shall be controlled through the EHR's role-based access control system per IT-SC-002 and shall comply with HIPAA minimum necessary standards per CO-HP-004.

4.6 Clinical records shall be protected from unauthorized access, loss, destruction, tampering, and unauthorized disclosure throughout their active use and retention periods. Protection requirements apply equally to electronic records (cybersecurity, access control, backup) and any remaining paper records (physical security, fire protection, controlled access).

4.7 Clinical records shall be retained for the minimum periods specified in Section 7 of this policy and per policy CO-HP-007 (Record Retention & Destruction). Records shall not be destroyed before their minimum retention period has elapsed without authorization from the Compliance Officer.

4.8 Upon a patient's written request for access to their clinical record, the agency shall fulfill the request per the standards and timeframes established in CO-HP-006 (Patient Access to Records).

4.9 Upon CMS survey, ADR audit, or other authorized government request for clinical records, the agency shall make the requested records available in the manner and within the timeframe specified in the request. Clinical Coordinators and Medical Records staff shall be trained to respond to record requests without delay or obstruction.

5. Definitions

Term	Definition

Clinical Record	The complete, permanent collection of all clinical documentation for a specific patient's home health episode — encompassing all assessments, visit notes, care coordination records, physician orders, OASIS data, plan of care documents, and all other records created in connection with the patient's care.

Active Record	A clinical record for a patient currently receiving home health services from the agency. Active records shall be immediately accessible to all authorized treating clinicians.

Inactive Record	A clinical record for a patient who has been discharged from home health services. Inactive records shall be retained per the retention schedule in Section 7 and shall be retrievable within 48 hours of a legitimate request.

Electronic Health Record (EHR)	The agency's certified electronic clinical record system in which all patient clinical records are created, stored, authenticated, and retrieved.

Record Organization Structure	The standardized arrangement of document categories within the clinical record — the sequence and grouping of record elements that enables consistent, efficient navigation of the record.

Minimum Required Content	The set of document types and data elements that must be present in every complete patient clinical record, as defined in Section 6.1 of this policy and 42 CFR § 484.110.

Record Retention Period	The minimum period, measured from the date of discharge or the last date of service, during which the agency must retain each patient's complete clinical record before it may be destroyed.

Role-Based Access Control	The EHR security configuration that grants each user access only to the clinical record sections and functions necessary for their specific role — preventing unauthorized access while enabling authorized access.

Audit Trail	The electronic log maintained by the EHR system that records every access to and every change in patient clinical records, including the identity of the user, the date and time of access, and the content of any modification.

Minimum Necessary Standard	The HIPAA requirement that access to, use of, and disclosure of protected health information be limited to the minimum information necessary to accomplish the intended legitimate purpose.

6. Procedures

6.1 Minimum Required Content of Every Patient Clinical Record

The following content elements shall be present in every complete patient clinical record. The absence of any element shall be treated as a clinical record deficiency and shall be reported to the Director of Nursing for resolution.

#	Content Category	Required Elements	Responsible Party for Creation

1	Intake and Admission Documentation	Referral source documentation; admission criteria assessment; patient demographic information; insurance coverage verification; service area confirmation; informed consent for home health services (CL-PR-003)	Intake Staff / Administrator

2	Physician Orders — Initial	Signed or authenticated initial physician orders authorizing home health services; verbal order documentation where applicable (CL-CP-003, CL-CP-004)	Assigned RN / Medical Records

3	Face-to-Face Encounter Documentation	Qualifying practitioner's encounter documentation per CL-CA-006; within the required F2F window	Medical Records / Clinical Coordinator

4	Comprehensive Assessment — SOC	Complete OASIS-E2 SOC assessment with all applicable items; narrative assessment covering all domains per CL-CA-001; homebound status documentation per CL-CA-005; skilled need documentation	Assigned RN

5	Plan of Care — Initial	Physician-approved plan of care including all required elements per CL-CP-001; physician signature	Assigned RN / Certifying Physician

6	Visit Notes — All Disciplines	Authenticated visit note for every visit delivered; missed visit records for every scheduled visit not delivered (CL-SD-024); all containing universal required elements per CL-CD-001	All Clinical Disciplines

7	Medication Records	Complete medication list from SOC medication reconciliation (CL-SD-013); medication administration records for all medications administered by agency staff (CL-SD-012); medication list updates at each change	Assigned RN

8	Physician Orders — All Subsequent	All written and authenticated verbal orders issued during the episode; change orders; discharge orders; supplemental orders (CL-CP-003)	Assigned RN / Medical Records

9	Physician Communication Records	Documentation of all significant physician contacts per CL-CP-005 — date, time, information communicated, physician response, orders received	Assigned RN

10	OASIS Assessments — All Time Points	Complete OASIS assessment for every applicable time point (SOC, ROC, RECERT, FU/SCIC, TRN, DC); narrative documentation supporting all OASIS responses (CL-CA-002)	Assigned RN

11	Plan of Care — All Updates	Updated plan of care for each recertification period (CL-CA-004, CL-CP-002); mid-episode plan modifications with supporting physician orders	Assigned RN

12	Physician Recertification — All Periods	Physician-signed recertification for each certification period beyond the first; per CL-CA-004 and CL-CP-008	Certifying Physician / Medical Records

13	Advance Directive Documentation	Advance directive status documentation from SOC assessment; copy of advance directive if present (CL-PR-002, CL-SD-023)	Assigned RN / Medical Records

14	Care Coordination Records	Interdisciplinary communication notes; care conference documentation; physician notification records; referral documentation (CL-CP-005)	Assigned RN / All Disciplines

15	Patient and Caregiver Education Records	Education documentation at each visit per CL-SD-017; learning needs assessment; education plan	All Clinical Disciplines

16	Discipline-Specific Evaluations	PT initial evaluation and discharge summary (CL-SD-002); OT initial evaluation and discharge summary (CL-SD-003); SLP initial evaluation (CL-SD-004); MSW psychosocial assessment (CL-SD-005); when ordered	Respective Disciplines

17	HHA Care Plans and Supervisory Records	HHA care plan per CL-SD-006; HHA supervisory visit forms	Supervising RN

18	Incident and Adverse Event Reports	Reports for all incidents, falls, medication errors, and adverse events per RM-ER-002 and QA-AE-001	Assigned RN / Director of Nursing

19	Safety Assessment Documentation	Home environment safety assessment per RM-PS-001; fall risk assessment per CL-SD-015	Assigned RN

20	Discharge Documentation	Discharge OASIS assessment; discharge summary per CL-CP-006; discharge instructions provided to patient; physician discharge order; community referrals made	Assigned RN / Medical Records

21	Patient Rights Documentation	Patient rights notice delivery documentation per CL-PR-001; signed grievance form if applicable (OP-PA-001)	Intake Staff / Assigned RN

22	Policy Acknowledgments	Signed policy acknowledgment forms where required by specific policy	All Applicable Staff

6.2 Clinical Record Organization Structure

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.2.1	Medical Records / Director of Nursing	Ensure the agency's EHR is configured with the standardized clinical record organization structure (Appendix A) — a consistent sequence of document category folders that applies uniformly to every patient's clinical record. The organization structure shall group documents by category (assessments, orders, visit notes, care coordination, billing support, discharge) in the sequence defined in Appendix A.	Configuration established at EHR implementation; reviewed annually.

6.2.2	All Clinicians	When creating clinical record entries, classify each entry in the correct document category folder per the standardized organization structure. Do not create freeform entries outside the established category structure. If uncertain about classification, consult the Director of Nursing before filing.	At the time of each record entry.

6.2.3	Medical Records	Conduct a quarterly clinical record organization audit for a random sample of ≥10% of active and recently discharged patient records. Verify that all required content elements are present per Section 6.1 and that entries are correctly classified within the organization structure. Document audit findings and report to the Director of Nursing.	Quarterly.

6.2.4	Director of Nursing	Review quarterly audit findings and direct corrective action for any identified content gaps or organizational deficiencies. Provide individual feedback to clinicians responsible for missing or misclassified entries.	Within 14 calendar days of audit completion.

6.3 Clinical Record Access Controls

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.3.1	IT Director / Director of Nursing	Establish and maintain role-based access control in the EHR that restricts each user's access to the clinical record sections required for their specific role: (a) Treating clinicians — access to all sections of their assigned patients' complete clinical records; (b) Medical Records — access to all sections of all records for records management purposes; (c) Revenue Cycle — access to billing-relevant sections (visit notes, assessments, orders, plan of care) without access to sensitive psychosocial or behavioral health content not required for billing; (d) QAPI Coordinator — access to aggregate quality data and de-identified record sections for quality review; (e) Compliance Officer — access to all record sections for compliance oversight purposes.	At EHR implementation; reviewed annually and whenever roles change.

6.3.2	IT Director	Maintain the EHR audit trail that records every user's access to clinical records — including read-only access — with the user identity, date and time, and specific record accessed. Review the audit trail monthly for unauthorized access patterns per IT-SC-002.	Monthly audit trail review.

6.3.3	Director of Nursing	Terminate or modify EHR access for any clinician who is no longer employed by or contracted with the agency within 4 hours of separation from service. Notify IT Director and the Administrator of any separation requiring immediate access revocation.	Within 4 hours of separation.

6.3.4	All Users	Do not share EHR login credentials with any other person. Each user's access is individual and non-transferable. Do not access clinical records for any patient who is not assigned to your care or for whom you do not have a legitimate clinical, administrative, or compliance need. Report any suspected unauthorized access to the IT Director and Compliance Officer immediately.	At all times.

6.4 Clinical Record Completeness Review

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.4.1	Medical Records	Generate a weekly Clinical Record Completeness Report for all episodes approaching discharge and all episodes that have been discharged within the past 14 calendar days. The report shall identify any required content element per Section 6.1 that is missing or incomplete, and shall assign the missing element to the responsible clinician for completion.	Weekly.

6.4.2	Medical Records	Notify the Director of Nursing of any record that is missing a content element that creates a billing compliance risk (unsigned plan of care, missing F2F documentation, missing recertification, incomplete OASIS) within 24 hours of identification.	Within 24 hours of identification.

6.4.3	Director of Nursing	Direct completion of all missing required content elements as a priority before the episode's final claim is submitted. For missing elements that cannot be retroactively completed (e.g., a visit that was never made), document the absence with the reason and assess billing compliance with the Compliance Officer.	Before final claim submission.

6.4.4	Medical Records	At discharge, conduct a final record completeness review for every discharged patient's record before the record is moved to inactive status. Verify that all required elements per Section 6.1 are present and that the record is complete. Document the completeness review in the record.	Before moving to inactive status.

6.5 Inactive Record Storage and Retrieval

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.5.1	Medical Records	After a patient is discharged, retain the complete active record in the EHR's active record repository for at least 90 calendar days before moving to the inactive record archive. After 90 days, the record may be moved to the inactive archive within the EHR or a CMS-compliant off-system archive.	90 days post-discharge before archiving.

6.5.2	Medical Records	Maintain an inactive record index documenting: patient name, date of birth, MR#, SOC date, discharge date, and the archive location of the record. The index shall be searchable by patient name, MR#, and date range.	Maintained continuously; reviewed quarterly.

6.5.3	Medical Records	Upon any authorized request for an inactive clinical record — from a surveyor, auditor, attorney, treating provider, or patient — retrieve the record within 48 hours of the request. Document all record requests and retrievals in the Record Request Log (Appendix B).	Within 48 hours of request.

6.5.4	Medical Records	For records requested in connection with a CMS survey, ADR audit, MAC record request, or OIG investigation, retrieve and make available within the timeframe specified in the request — typically 1–3 business days. Notify the Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer of all government record requests immediately upon receipt.	Per the request's specified timeframe; Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer notification immediately.

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

Condition	Escalation Path	Corrective Action	Timeframe

A required content element cannot be retroactively completed because the clinical event did not occur (e.g., a physician signature that was never obtained despite documented follow-up)	Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer	Compliance Officer assesses the billing impact. If the missing element renders the claim unsupported, the claim shall not be submitted or shall be retracted. Document the situation thoroughly including all follow-up attempts.	Compliance Officer assessment within 5 business days.

A clinical record cannot be retrieved for a requested survey or audit	Medical Records and IT Director	Initiate an emergency record recovery from the EHR backup system per IT-DR-001. Notify the Compliance Officer immediately. Provide a written explanation of the technical difficulty to the requesting party. Document all recovery actions.	Recovery initiated within 4 hours; Compliance Officer notification immediately.

EHR audit trail reveals unauthorized access to patient clinical records	IT Director and Compliance Officer	Compliance Officer initiates an investigation per CO-CP-007. Revoke the unauthorized user's access immediately. Assess whether a HIPAA breach occurred per CO-HP-003. Notify the patient if required under HIPAA.	Investigation initiated within 24 hours; access revoked immediately.

A patient's clinical record is discovered to contain documentation for a different patient (record mix-up)	Medical Records and Director of Nursing	Immediately separate the misidentified records. Notify the Compliance Officer and the Director of Nursing. Assess whether any clinical decisions were made based on the erroneous information and whether patient safety was affected. Correct both records.	Records separated immediately; Compliance Officer within 24 hours.

7. Retention Requirements

All patient clinical records shall be retained for the minimum periods specified below. Retention periods are measured from the date of the patient's discharge or the last date of service, whichever is later. Records shall not be destroyed before the minimum retention period elapses without written authorization from the Compliance Officer.

Record Type	Minimum Retention Period	Basis

Complete patient clinical record (all content per Section 6.1)	7 years from date of discharge	CMS Conditions of Participation; California Health and Safety Code

Pediatric patient records (under age 18 at admission)	7 years from discharge OR until the patient's 21st birthday, whichever is longer	California law for minors

Records for deceased patients	7 years from date of death	California law

OASIS assessment data	7 years from date of submission	42 CFR § 484.20

Billing records and supporting documentation	10 years from date of service	CMS False Claims Act statute of limitations

Consent forms	7 years from date of discharge	HIPAA; California law

Advance directives	7 years from date of discharge; longer if the document remains in effect	California Probate Code

Records subject to pending litigation, audit, or investigation	Indefinitely, until the matter is finally resolved	Legal hold requirements

8. Documentation Requirements

Requirement	Document / Record	Responsible Party	Location	Timeframe

Complete patient clinical record	All required content per Section 6.1	All applicable clinicians	EHR — active and inactive record repositories	Per each element's creation timeline; retained per Section 7

Clinical Record Completeness Report	Weekly report of incomplete records	Medical Records	Medical Records file	Weekly; retained 7 years

Record Request Log	Record of all requests for clinical records	Medical Records	Medical Records file	At each request; retained 7 years

Quarterly organization audit	Audit findings for record organization and content completeness	Medical Records / Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file	Quarterly; retained 7 years

EHR access log	Role-based access configuration and current user roster	IT Director	IT security file	Updated within 4 hours of any access change; retained 7 years

Inactive record index	Searchable index of all archived records	Medical Records	Medical Records file	Maintained continuously

Policy acknowledgment	Signed acknowledgment — Appendix C	Each individual; Administrator (collection)	Policy acknowledgment file	Within 14 calendar days

9. Compliance & Audit Considerations

9.1 How Compliance Is Measured

Compliance Indicator	Measurement Method	Acceptable Standard

All required content elements present in every clinical record	Quarterly completeness audit	≥95% of audited records complete

Inactive records retrieved within 48 hours of request	Record Request Log audit	≥98%

No unauthorized EHR access identified	Monthly audit trail review	Zero unauthorized access incidents

All government record requests fulfilled within specified timeframe	Record Request Log audit	100%

Final record completeness review completed at discharge	Medical Records audit	100% of discharged records reviewed

9.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G328 (42 CFR § 484.110) will request the clinical records of selected active and recently discharged patients and verify: (a) that all required content elements per 42 CFR § 484.110 are present; (b) that the record is organized in a manner that enables efficient navigation; (c) that access is appropriately controlled. ADR auditors will request specific records and expect delivery within days — agencies that cannot produce records quickly or that produce incomplete records are cited for non-compliance.

9.3 Common Failure Points

Failure Point	Risk	Mitigation

Clinical record missing physician-signed plan of care	Billing non-compliance; survey deficiency	Weekly completeness report; billing hold until signed POC on file

HHA supervisory visit records not filed in clinical record	42 CFR § 484.80 deficiency	HHA supervisory form filed in EHR immediately after supervisory visit

Records retrieved slowly or not at all during survey	Operational deficiency; potential obstruction	Quarterly retrieval drills; Medical Records staff trained on urgent record requests

EHR access not revoked promptly after staff separation	HIPAA breach risk; unauthorized access	Separation checklist includes immediate IT notification; 4-hour revocation standard

11. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

11.1 All clinical staff shall receive training on clinical record content requirements and organization standards within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: required content per Section 6.1 and their responsibility for each element within their role; the EHR organization structure and correct document category classification; record access controls and prohibited access; and record retention requirements.

11.2 Medical Records staff shall receive training on clinical record completeness auditing, inactive record management, government record request response procedures, and retention and destruction protocols at hire and annually.

11.3 IT staff shall receive training on EHR audit trail review, role-based access control management, and access revocation procedures at hire and whenever system configurations change.

11.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.
