CL-CD-003 — Clinical Record Authentication & Signature Requirements

1. Policy Header

Field	Value

Policy ID	CL-CD-003

Policy Title	Clinical Record Authentication & Signature Requirements

Domain	CL — Clinical Operations

Subdomain	CD — Clinical Documentation

Classification Tier	REQUIRED

Status	ACTIVE

Review Cycle	Annual

Access Tier	Tier 2 — Restricted

Policy Owner / Steward	Director of Nursing

Effective Date	2025-07-10

Version	1.0

Approved By	Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.

Last Reviewed	2025-07-10

Next Review Date	2026-07-10

Supersedes	N/A (Initial Version)

2. Purpose

This policy establishes the requirements for authenticating all clinical record entries — including acceptable signature formats, electronic signature standards, timeliness of authentication, and co-signature requirements for supervised staff — at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. Authentication is the act by which a clinician formally claims authorship of a clinical record entry and attests that the entry accurately reflects what was observed, performed, or communicated. An unauthenticated entry is, legally and regulatorily, an entry that belongs to no one — it cannot be attributed to a specific clinician, cannot be relied upon in litigation, cannot be cited as support for a billing claim, and constitutes a deficiency under 42 CFR § 484.110.

Authentication is not a formality — it is the final act of professional accountability for every clinical record entry. When a clinician signs a visit note, they are attesting under the standards of their professional license that the content of that note accurately represents what they observed, assessed, and performed for that patient on that date. That attestation is a legal and professional act with consequences — falsely attesting to the accuracy of a fraudulent note is a crime; failing to authenticate a note for weeks after writing it suggests the author does not stand behind the content. Both extremes are compliance failures.

This policy establishes the complete authentication framework at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.: the formats that constitute valid authentication in the electronic record environment; the timeframes within which authentication must occur; the co-signature requirements that reflect California's scope of practice framework for supervised practitioners; and the escalation process when authentication lapses are identified. This policy implements 42 CFR § 484.110 (Clinical Records), California Nursing Practice Act authentication requirements, and CMS documentation integrity standards.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses (RNs) creating and authenticating clinical record entries

All licensed vocational nurses (LVNs) creating clinical record entries and requiring supervising RN co-signature

All physical therapists (PTs) creating and authenticating clinical record entries

All physical therapist assistants (PTAs) creating clinical record entries and requiring supervising PT co-signature

All occupational therapists (OTs) creating and authenticating clinical record entries

All certified occupational therapy assistants (COTAs) creating clinical record entries and requiring supervising OT co-signature

All speech-language pathologists (SLPs) creating and authenticating clinical record entries

All medical social workers (MSWs) creating and authenticating clinical record entries

All home health aides (HHAs) creating visit documentation

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for authentication compliance oversight

Medical Records staff responsible for monitoring authentication status

IT staff maintaining the EHR electronic signature system

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every entry in a patient's clinical record shall be authenticated by the clinician who created the entry. Authentication is mandatory — unauthenticated entries are not valid record entries and shall not be relied upon for clinical, billing, or legal purposes.

4.2 The agency's primary authentication method is the electronic signature function within the agency's EHR. Electronic signatures applied through the agency's EHR constitute valid, legally compliant authentication when the EHR system: (a) uniquely identifies each user through individual login credentials; (b) records the date and time of authentication at the moment of signature application; (c) links the authentication permanently and irreversibly to the specific entry; and (d) maintains an audit trail of all authentications per CO-DC-001.

4.3 Electronic signatures shall include, at minimum: the clinician's full legal name; their professional credential (RN, LVN, PT, PTA, OT, COTA, SLP, MSW, HHA); and the date and time of authentication — automatically populated by the EHR at the moment the signature is applied.

4.4 Authentication shall occur within the timeframes specified in Section 6.2. Authentication of visit notes shall occur at the time of note submission in the EHR, which shall be within 24 hours of the visit per CL-CD-004. Notes not authenticated within 24 hours are subject to the overdue authentication escalation process per Section 6.4.

4.5 Co-signature requirements shall be applied in accordance with California scope of practice law and agency supervision policies: (a) LVN entries — require a supervising RN co-signature within 14 calendar days; (b) PTA entries — require a supervising PT co-signature within 14 calendar days; (c) COTA entries — require a supervising OT co-signature within 14 calendar days; (d) HHA entries — do not require a co-signature but are subject to the supervising RN's review as part of the 14-day supervisory visit process per CL-SD-006.

4.6 No person may apply their electronic signature to a clinical record entry that they did not personally create, based on content they did not personally observe or perform. Co-signatures by supervising clinicians attest that the supervising clinician has reviewed the entry, that it appears consistent with their knowledge of the patient, and that they accept supervisory responsibility — not that they personally observed or performed every item documented.

4.7 Physician and allowed practitioner authentication of orders, plans of care, and recertifications shall be obtained within the timeframes defined in CL-CP-003, CL-CP-004, and CL-CP-008, and tracked per CL-CP-009. These physician signature requirements are governed by the Order Management policies; this policy governs authentication of entries made by agency clinical staff.

5. Definitions

Term	Definition

Authentication	The formal act by which a clinician identifies themselves as the author of a clinical record entry and attests to its accuracy, accomplished through application of an electronic signature within the EHR.

Electronic Signature	A digital identifier applied through the agency's EHR that uniquely associates a specific user with a specific clinical record entry, including the user's name, credential, and the date and time of signature application.

Co-Signature	A second authentication applied by a supervising clinician to a clinical record entry made by a supervised practitioner — attesting to the supervisor's review of and acceptance of supervisory responsibility for the entry.

Author	The clinician who personally observed or performed the clinical activities documented in the entry and who is solely responsible for the accuracy of the content.

Supervisor	The licensed clinician who has supervisory responsibility for the clinical work of a supervised practitioner, who reviews the supervised practitioner's entries, and who applies a co-signature as required.

Unauthenticated Entry	A clinical record entry that has been created but not yet authenticated by the author. Unauthenticated entries are incomplete and shall not be relied upon for clinical, billing, or legal purposes.

Overdue Authentication	An authentication that has not been applied within the required timeframe, triggering an escalation process per Section 6.4.

Authentication Timestamp	The date and time, automatically recorded by the EHR, at which a specific authentication was applied.

Audit Trail	The EHR-generated electronic log that permanently records every authentication and co-signature applied to every clinical record entry, including the authenticating user's identity and the authentication timestamp.

6. Procedures

6.1 Electronic Signature Application — Standard Process

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.1.1	All Clinicians	Log into the EHR using your individual, personal login credentials. Never share your login credentials with another person. Never use another person's credentials to access the EHR or to authenticate entries. Login credentials are personal, non-transferable, and the use of another person's credentials constitutes both a HIPAA violation and a potential falsification of records.	At every EHR session.

6.1.2	All Clinicians	After creating a clinical record entry, review the complete entry for accuracy, completeness, and internal consistency before applying the electronic signature. The act of signing is an attestation to the accuracy of the entire entry — do not authenticate an entry without reading it.	Before each authentication.

6.1.3	All Clinicians	Apply the electronic signature using the EHR's designated signature function. Verify that the EHR displays your full legal name, your professional credential, and the current date and time at the moment of authentication. If any of these elements is incorrect (wrong name, wrong credential, wrong date), do not authenticate — contact IT to resolve the issue before signing.	At each authentication.

6.1.4	All Clinicians	After authenticating, verify that the EHR confirms the authentication was successfully applied and that the entry is now in "authenticated" or "locked" status. If the EHR does not confirm successful authentication, attempt the process again. If the issue persists, notify IT and the Director of Nursing within 4 hours.	After each authentication.

6.1.5	All Clinicians	Never authenticate a clinical record entry for a visit that you did not personally conduct or a clinical interaction you did not personally participate in. Authentication is personal attestation of personal activity.	At all times.

6.2 Authentication Timeliness Requirements by Entry Type

Entry Type	Author Authentication Deadline	Co-Signature Deadline (if applicable)	Reference

Visit note (all disciplines)	Within 24 hours of the visit	Within 14 calendar days of the visit date (LVN/PTA/COTA entries only)	CL-CD-004

OASIS assessment	Within 5 calendar days of the assessment date, prior to submission	N/A — RN only	CL-CA-002

Plan of care	Within 24 hours of development	N/A (physician signature tracked separately per CL-CP-009)	CL-CP-001

Physician communication note	Within 24 hours of the communication	N/A	CL-CD-001

Care coordination note	Within 24 hours of the coordinated event	N/A	CL-CD-001

Missed visit record	Within 4 hours of the scheduled visit time	N/A	CL-SD-024

Medication administration record	At time of administration or within 24 hours	N/A	CL-SD-012

Incident report	Within 24 hours of the incident	N/A	RM-ER-002

HHA visit documentation	At or immediately after each visit; by end of business day	RN review within 14 calendar days	CL-SD-006

Discharge documentation	Within 2 calendar days of discharge	N/A	CL-CP-006

Late entry	At time of late entry creation; note must identify as late entry per CO-DC-003	Per the entry type's co-signature requirement	CO-DC-003

6.3 Co-Signature Requirements and Process

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.3.1	Supervising RN (for LVN entries)	Review every clinical record entry authored by each assigned LVN within 14 calendar days of the entry date. The review shall assess: (a) clinical accuracy — does the entry reflect appropriate nursing practice within LVN scope? (b) documentation adequacy — does the entry meet the standards of CL-CD-001? (c) internal consistency — is the entry consistent with other entries in the patient's record?	Within 14 calendar days of LVN entry date.

6.3.2	Supervising RN	If the LVN entry is accurate, adequate, and consistent, apply the co-signature in the EHR using the designated co-signature function. The co-signature shall display the supervising RN's full legal name, credential (RN), and the date and time of the co-signature.	Within 14 calendar days.

6.3.3	Supervising RN	If the LVN entry contains an inaccuracy, inadequacy, or inconsistency: (a) do not apply the co-signature until the issue is resolved; (b) contact the LVN to discuss the concern; (c) if the entry requires correction, direct the LVN to create a late entry amendment per CO-DC-003; (d) notify the Director of Nursing of any significant clinical accuracy concern identified in an LVN entry.	Within 14 calendar days; correction before co-signature.

6.3.4	Supervising PT (for PTA entries)	Apply the same co-signature review and authentication process per steps 6.3.1 through 6.3.3 for all PTA entries, consistent with California Physical Therapy Practice Act supervision requirements.	Within 14 calendar days.

6.3.5	Supervising OT (for COTA entries)	Apply the same co-signature review and authentication process per steps 6.3.1 through 6.3.3 for all COTA entries, consistent with California Board of Occupational Therapy supervision requirements.	Within 14 calendar days.

6.3.6	Director of Nursing	Monitor co-signature compliance for all supervised practitioners monthly through the EHR's authentication compliance report. Any co-signature that is overdue beyond 14 calendar days shall be identified and escalated per Section 6.4.	Monthly.

6.4 Overdue Authentication Escalation Process

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.4.1	Medical Records / Director of Nursing	Generate a daily Unauthenticated Entry Report from the EHR listing all clinical record entries that have not been authenticated within their required timeframe. Distribute the report to the Director of Nursing and to each clinician with unauthenticated entries.	Daily by 9:00 AM.

6.4.2	Director of Nursing	For any visit note unauthenticated beyond 48 hours, contact the responsible clinician directly by telephone or in person and direct immediate authentication. Document the contact.	Within 48 hours of the authentication deadline.

6.4.3	Director of Nursing	For any visit note unauthenticated beyond 72 hours, place a formal clinical record flag on the entry in the EHR and initiate corrective counseling with the clinician. If the note is not authenticated within 96 hours, initiate a disciplinary action referral to HR per HR-ER-002. Document all actions.	By 72 hours; HR referral at 96 hours if not resolved.

6.4.4	Director of Nursing	For co-signatures overdue beyond 14 calendar days, contact the supervising clinician directly. If the co-signature is not applied within 21 calendar days, initiate a disciplinary action referral to HR per HR-ER-002.	At 14 calendar days; HR referral at 21 calendar days.

6.4.5	Director of Nursing	Assess the billing impact of any unauthenticated entry on a claim that is being prepared for submission. Do not submit a claim supported by a visit note that is not authenticated. Place the claim on billing hold until the authentication is resolved.	Billing hold immediately upon identification; release only after authentication.

6.5 Authentication of Late Entries and Corrections

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.5.1	All Clinicians	When creating a late entry, authenticate the entry at the time of creation. The late entry's authentication timestamp will reflect the date and time the entry was created — which will differ from the date of the clinical event. This difference is expected for late entries and shall be explained per CO-DC-003.	At the time of late entry creation.

6.5.2	All Clinicians	When correcting a previously authenticated entry using the EHR's amendment function, authenticate the amendment separately and at the time of the amendment. The EHR shall maintain the original authenticated entry, the amendment, and the separate authentication of the amendment per CO-DC-001.	At the time of amendment.

6.5.3	All Clinicians	Never delete, overwrite, or in any way eliminate a previously authenticated entry from the clinical record. If a prior entry is inaccurate, the correction is made by amendment — the original entry remains in the record with the amendment linked to it. Deletion of authenticated entries is falsification of records.	At all times.

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

Condition	Escalation Path	Corrective Action	Timeframe

EHR electronic signature system is unavailable due to system outage	IT Director and Director of Nursing	Clinicians shall document visits on paper backup forms during the outage. Paper entries shall be scanned into the EHR and electronically authenticated within 24 hours of system restoration. IT shall restore EHR functionality within 4 hours per IT-DR-002.	System restoration within 4 hours; electronic authentication within 24 hours of restoration.

A clinician is found to have authenticated entries for visits they did not personally conduct (surrogate signing)	Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer	Compliance Officer initiates a compliance investigation per CO-CP-007. Immediately revoke the responsible clinician's EHR access pending investigation. Assess all entries authenticated by the individual for validity. If billing has occurred on falsely authenticated entries, assess for False Claims Act exposure.	Investigation within 24 hours; EHR access revocation immediately.

A clinical record entry is identified as authenticated by the wrong clinician (e.g., wrong user logged in, shared credentials)	IT Director and Director of Nursing	Flag the entry as potentially invalid. Identify the correct author. Have the correct author create a late entry amendment per CO-DC-003 authenticating the correct authorship. Report the shared credentials violation to the Compliance Officer per CO-HP-002.	Amendment within 5 business days; Compliance Officer within 24 hours.

Supervising clinician refuses to co-sign a supervised practitioner's entry	Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing investigates the reason. If the entry contains a clinical inaccuracy that prevents co-signature, direct the supervised practitioner to amend the entry. If the refusal is unjustified, Director of Nursing provides clinical adjudication and determines whether co-signature should be applied.	Resolution within 7 calendar days.

7. Documentation Requirements

Requirement	Document / Record	Responsible Party	Location	Timeframe

Authenticated clinical record entries	All visit notes, assessments, coordination records with electronic signature	All clinicians	EHR — clinical record	Per Section 6.2 timeliness requirements; retained 7 years

Authentication audit trail	EHR-generated log of all authentications	EHR system (automatic)	EHR — audit trail	Maintained continuously; retained 7 years

Daily Unauthenticated Entry Report	Report of all overdue authentications	Medical Records / Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file	Daily; retained 7 years

Co-signature compliance report	Monthly report of co-signature status for all supervised practitioners	Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file	Monthly; retained 7 years

Overdue authentication corrective actions	Documentation of contacts, counseling, and HR referrals	Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file; HR file	At each event; retained 7 years

Policy acknowledgment	Signed acknowledgment — Appendix A	Each individual; Administrator (collection)	Policy acknowledgment file	Within 14 calendar days

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

Compliance Indicator	Measurement Method	Acceptable Standard

Visit notes authenticated within 24 hours	Daily Unauthenticated Entry Report; EHR timestamp audit	≥98% authenticated within 24 hours

LVN/PTA/COTA entries co-signed within 14 calendar days	Monthly co-signature compliance report	≥98% co-signed within 14 calendar days

No entries authenticated by anyone other than the author	Monthly audit trail review for shared credential indicators	Zero violations

No unauthenticated entries in records of patients under active billing	Pre-bill documentation verification	100% — zero unauthenticated entries in billed records

Daily Unauthenticated Entry Report generated and reviewed	Report log audit	100% daily

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors will examine clinical records for unauthenticated entries, entries authenticated significantly later than the date of service (without late entry notation), and entries where the authenticated author does not match the clinician who delivered the service as described. ADR auditors frequently flag entries authenticated many days after the visit date as potential integrity concerns. The authentication timestamp is a permanent, auditable record — there is no way to authenticate a note and hide when it was signed.

8.3 Common Failure Points

Failure Point	Risk	Mitigation

Visit notes not authenticated for days after the visit	Survey deficiency; ADR integrity concern	Daily Unauthenticated Entry Report; 48-hour escalation; 96-hour HR referral

Shared login credentials — one clinician signing for another	HIPAA violation; falsification of records; False Claims Act risk	Annual training on credential sharing prohibition; EHR security configuration preventing simultaneous login from multiple locations

LVN entries never co-signed	Supervision deficiency; California scope of practice violation	Monthly co-signature compliance report; Director of Nursing escalation at Day 14

Clinician authenticates entry without reading it	Attestation of inaccurate content; liability exposure	Training on authentication as professional attestation; Director of Nursing pre-authentication review check

10.1 All clinical staff shall receive training on authentication requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: the legal and professional significance of electronic authentication; the prohibition on shared credentials and surrogate signing; the timeliness requirements by entry type; co-signature requirements for supervised practitioners; and the late entry and correction authentication process.

2. Purpose

This policy mandates specific timeframes for clinical documentation completion and record locking following each patient encounter at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., with escalation protocols for overdue entries and supervisory review requirements. The timeliness of clinical documentation is not a bureaucratic preference — it is a clinical safety imperative, a legal requirement, and a billing compliance standard simultaneously.

Clinical safety requires that documentation be completed promptly because the next clinician to see the patient — whether the same clinician at the next visit or another discipline responding to a change in condition — relies on the prior visit note to inform their clinical decisions. A visit note completed 4 days after the visit cannot serve this function. In a home health environment where patients are seen intermittently, a missed clinical finding not promptly documented may not be known to the team for days — by which time the patient's condition may have deteriorated to the point of hospitalization.

Legal and regulatory standards require that documentation be contemporaneous with the clinical event. CMS surveyors view notes completed significantly after the visit date as evidence that the documentation does not accurately reflect what occurred during the visit. ADR auditors apply a similar presumption. Courts hearing malpractice claims treat late documentation as evidence of either memory deficiency (the clinician had to reconstruct what happened from memory) or fabrication (the clinician created the note after the fact). Neither inference serves the agency's interests.

Billing compliance requires that documentation be available before a claim is submitted. Medicare requires that claims be supported by documentation of the services provided — a claim submitted before the visit note is completed is a claim submitted without documentation. If the note is subsequently found to not support the billed service, there is no contemporaneous documentation to defend the claim.

This policy establishes the complete timeliness framework for every category of clinical documentation at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. — the deadlines, the monitoring system, the escalation process, and the supervisory review requirements that enforce those deadlines with the rigor their clinical, legal, and financial importance demands.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses (RNs) creating clinical documentation

All licensed vocational nurses (LVNs) creating clinical documentation

All physical therapists (PTs) and physical therapist assistants (PTAs) creating clinical documentation

All occupational therapists (OTs) and certified occupational therapy assistants (COTAs) creating clinical documentation

All speech-language pathologists (SLPs) creating clinical documentation

All medical social workers (MSWs) creating clinical documentation

All home health aides (HHAs) creating visit documentation

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager responsible for documentation timeliness oversight

Medical Records staff who monitor documentation completion status

Revenue Cycle staff who must verify documentation completeness before claim submission

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every clinical record entry shall be completed, authenticated, and locked in the EHR within the timeframes specified in Section 6.1 of this policy. These timeframes are mandatory — they are not targets, preferences, or guidelines.

4.2 A clinical record entry is "complete" when it contains all required content elements per CL-CD-001 and the applicable discipline-specific documentation policy. A clinical record entry is "authenticated" when the author has applied their electronic signature per CL-CD-003. A clinical record entry is "locked" when the EHR system has finalized the entry and prevented further untracked modification.

4.3 The agency's standard documentation completion deadline for all visit notes across all clinical disciplines is 24 hours from the time the visit ended. This 24-hour standard applies to every visit note, by every discipline, regardless of visit complexity, clinician schedule, or any other operational factor. The 24-hour standard is the maximum — clinicians are encouraged to complete documentation at or immediately following the visit.

4.4 An entry not completed within its required timeframe shall be treated as a late entry per CO-DC-003. The late entry shall be clearly identified as late in the EHR, including the date of the clinical event and the date the entry was created, per the late entry notation requirements in CO-DC-003.

4.5 Revenue Cycle shall maintain a documentation status verification step as part of the pre-bill checklist per FN-BC-001. No claim shall be submitted for any visit for which the corresponding visit note is not completed, authenticated, and locked in the EHR.

4.6 The Director of Nursing shall review documentation timeliness compliance monthly as part of the Documentation Quality Dashboard per CL-CD-001, and shall present timeliness data to the QAPI Committee quarterly.

4.7 Clinicians with a pattern of late documentation — defined as 3 or more visit notes in any 30-day period that are not completed within 24 hours of the visit — shall receive formal corrective counseling per HR-ER-002 and a documentation improvement plan with defined timelines and consequences.

5. Definitions

Term	Definition

Documentation Completion	The state of a clinical record entry that contains all required content elements for the type of entry, has been authored by the responsible clinician, has been reviewed by the author, has been authenticated by the author, and has been locked in the EHR.

Locked Entry	A clinical record entry that has been finalized in the EHR in a manner that prevents further modification without generating an auditable amendment record. A locked entry is the permanent version of the clinical record entry.

Documentation Deadline	The specific time after a clinical event (visit, assessment, communication, incident) by which the corresponding clinical record entry must be completed and locked.

Overdue Entry	A clinical record entry that has not been completed and locked by its documentation deadline.

Late Entry	A clinical record entry that is created after the deadline applicable to the type of entry, documented with a clear notation that it is a late entry per CO-DC-003.

Contemporaneous Documentation	Documentation created at or immediately following the clinical event it describes — the gold standard for clinical documentation integrity.

Documentation Timeliness Compliance Rate	The percentage of clinical record entries, measured across a defined period and sample, that were completed, authenticated, and locked within the applicable deadline.

Pre-Bill Documentation Verification	The mandatory Revenue Cycle step that confirms all visit notes for an episode are completed and locked before a claim is submitted for the episode.

6. Procedures

6.1 Documentation Deadlines by Entry Type

Entry Type	Completion Deadline	Responsible Clinician	Reference

Visit note — all clinical disciplines (RN, LVN, PT, PTA, OT, COTA, SLP, MSW)	24 hours from visit end time	Visiting clinician	CL-CD-001

HHA visit documentation	By end of the same business day as the visit; no later than 24 hours	HHA	CL-SD-006

OASIS assessment — all time points	Within 5 calendar days of the assessment date	Authorized RN assessor	CL-CA-001, CL-CA-002

Missed visit record	Within 4 hours of the scheduled visit time	Clinical Coordinator / Visiting Clinician	CL-SD-024

Plan of care — initial	Within 24 hours of the SOC assessment	Assigned RN	CL-CP-001

Plan of care — recertification	Within 24 hours of the RECERT assessment	Assigned RN	CL-CA-004

Physician communication note	Within 24 hours of the communication	Assigned RN	CL-CD-001

Care coordination note	Within 24 hours of the coordinated event	Documenting clinician	CL-CD-001

Medication administration record	At time of administration or within 24 hours	Administering clinician	CL-SD-012

Wound care documentation (in visit note)	Within 24 hours of wound care delivery	Assigned RN / LVN	CL-SD-011

Incident report	Within 24 hours of the incident	Assigned RN / Witnessing Clinician	RM-ER-002

Adverse event report	Within 24 hours of adverse event identification	Assigned RN / Director of Nursing	QA-AE-001

Transfer documentation — warm handoff note	At the time of transfer or within 24 hours for emergent transfers	Assigned RN	CL-CP-007

Discharge summary	Within 2 calendar days of discharge date	Assigned RN	CL-CP-006

Discharge OASIS	Within 2 calendar days of discharge date	Authorized RN assessor	CL-CA-001

Behavioral health screening results	Within 24 hours of the assessment	Assigned RN	CL-SD-022

Goals-of-care conversation documentation	Within 24 hours of the conversation	Assigned RN / MSW	CL-SD-023

Post-fall assessment documentation	Within 24 hours of the fall	Assigned RN	CL-SD-015

6.2 Documentation Timeliness Monitoring System

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.2.1	Medical Records / Director of Nursing	Generate a daily Documentation Timeliness Report from the EHR that displays, for every patient visit from the prior 24 hours: (a) patient name and MR#; (b) visit date and time; (c) discipline and clinician name; (d) documentation completion status (completed and locked / pending / overdue); (e) time elapsed since the visit for pending entries.	Daily by 9:00 AM.

6.2.2	Medical Records	Review the daily report and identify all entries that are overdue (visit note not completed and locked within 24 hours of the visit end time). Flag each overdue entry in the EHR and generate an alert to the responsible clinician and to the Director of Nursing.	Daily by 10:00 AM.

6.2.3	Director of Nursing	Review the daily Documentation Timeliness Report. For each overdue entry, assess the reason for the delay and direct the responsible clinician to complete and lock the entry immediately. Document the corrective direction and the entry's completion date.	Daily review; corrective direction within the business day.

6.2.4	Director of Nursing	Generate a weekly Documentation Timeliness Compliance Report summarizing: (a) total visits delivered in the prior week; (b) percentage of visit notes completed within 24 hours; (c) percentage completed within 48 hours; (d) overdue entries by discipline and by individual clinician; (e) entries still pending authentication beyond 72 hours.	Weekly; report distributed to Administrator.

6.2.5	Director of Nursing	Include documentation timeliness data in the monthly Documentation Quality Dashboard per CL-CD-001. Present discipline-level and individual clinician-level timeliness compliance rates. Present to the QAPI Committee quarterly with trend analysis.	Monthly dashboard; quarterly QAPI presentation.

6.3 Visit Note Completion Best Practices

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.3.1	All Clinicians	Begin documenting visit findings during the visit rather than after the visit. Mobile EHR applications shall be used at the patient's bedside whenever feasible. Document objective findings, vital signs, and assessments in real time during the visit — this is the most accurate approach and minimizes post-visit documentation burden.	During the visit.

6.3.2	All Clinicians	If the complete visit note cannot be finished during the visit (acceptable for complex visits), complete the remaining documentation within 4 hours of leaving the patient's home, before beginning the next patient visit. Do not accumulate incomplete notes across multiple consecutive visits.	Within 4 hours of leaving the patient's home.

6.3.3	All Clinicians	If a clinical situation genuinely prevents documentation completion within 24 hours (e.g., the clinician was involved in an emergency with a patient and required several hours of clinical response), notify the Director of Nursing of the reason and complete the documentation as a late entry per CO-DC-003 with an explanation of the delay.	At the earliest opportunity; Director of Nursing notification within 24 hours.

6.3.4	Director of Nursing	Assess monthly whether clinicians are systematically completing documentation at or near the end of each visit day (suggesting real-time or near-real-time documentation) vs. completing documentation in clusters late in the day or the following day (suggesting documentation is being delayed and reconstructed from memory). Address systematic late-documentation patterns with individual coaching.	Monthly.

6.4 Overdue Entry Escalation Protocol

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.4.1	Medical Records	When a visit note is identified as overdue (not completed and locked within 24 hours of the visit), generate an automated EHR alert to the responsible clinician and to the Director of Nursing. Document the alert in the Documentation Timeliness Report.	At 24 hours post-visit.

6.4.2	Director of Nursing	Contact the responsible clinician by telephone or in person within 2 hours of the 24-hour alert and direct immediate documentation completion. Document the contact and the clinician's stated reason for the delay.	Within 26 hours of the visit (2 hours after the 24-hour deadline).

6.4.3	Director of Nursing	For any visit note not completed within 48 hours of the visit: (a) place a formal flag on the patient's clinical record in the EHR noting the documentation gap; (b) place a billing hold on any claim associated with the visit per the Revenue Cycle pre-bill checklist; (c) document the escalation and the clinician's reason for the delay.	At 48 hours post-visit.

6.4.4	Director of Nursing	For any visit note not completed within 72 hours of the visit: (a) initiate formal corrective counseling per HR-ER-002; (b) document the counseling in the clinician's personnel file; (c) require the clinician to complete the late entry with a documented explanation per CO-DC-003.	At 72 hours post-visit.

6.4.5	Director of Nursing	For any clinician with 3 or more visit notes overdue beyond 24 hours within any 30-day period: (a) initiate a formal Documentation Improvement Plan per HR-ER-001 with defined improvement timelines, monitoring frequency, and consequences for continued non-compliance; (b) increase supervisory documentation review frequency to weekly for the affected clinician; (c) notify the Administrator.	Within 7 calendar days of the third overdue note in any 30-day period.

6.5 Pre-Bill Documentation Verification Integration

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.5.1	Revenue Cycle	Before preparing any claim for submission, verify in the EHR that every visit to be billed in the claim episode has a corresponding visit note that is: (a) completed with all required content elements; (b) authenticated by the visiting clinician; (c) locked in the EHR. Document the verification in the pre-bill checklist per FN-BC-001.	Before each claim submission.

6.5.2	Revenue Cycle	If any visit note for an episode is not completed, authenticated, and locked, place the episode on billing hold. Notify the Clinical Coordinator and Director of Nursing of the specific note(s) blocking claim submission and the specific visit date(s) and clinician(s) involved.	Immediately upon identifying an incomplete visit note during pre-bill review.

6.5.3	Revenue Cycle	Release the billing hold and proceed with claim submission only after the Director of Nursing confirms that the documentation gap has been resolved — either by the clinician completing the note with a compliant late entry per CO-DC-003, or by the Director of Nursing determining that a specific visit cannot be supported and directing that the visit be removed from the claim.	After Director of Nursing confirmation.

6.6 OASIS Assessment Timeliness

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.6.1	Assigned RN	Complete, authenticate, and lock the OASIS assessment in the EHR within 5 calendar days of the assessment date. This is the CMS submission deadline — the agency's operational target is 3 calendar days to allow the OASIS Quality Reviewer time to review before submission per CL-CA-002.	Within 5 calendar days of assessment; operational target of 3 calendar days.

6.6.2	Director of Nursing	Monitor OASIS completion timeliness through the EHR's assessment status dashboard. Generate an alert at Day 3 post-assessment for any OASIS not yet locked, and at Day 4 for any OASIS not yet submitted to the OASIS Quality Reviewer.	Days 3 and 4 post-assessment for unlocked OASIS.

6.6.3	OASIS Transmission Coordinator	Generate an alert at Day 4 post-assessment for any OASIS not yet transmitted to the CMS repository, regardless of the reason for delay. Immediately investigate and resolve to ensure transmission before the Day 5 deadline.	Day 4 post-assessment for untransmitted OASIS.

6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

Condition	Escalation Path	Corrective Action	Timeframe

The EHR system is unavailable and prevents documentation from being completed within the required timeframe	Director of Nursing and IT Director	IT Director initiates EHR recovery per IT-DR-002. Clinicians document visits on paper backup forms. Upon EHR restoration, scan and upload paper documentation and authenticate electronically — with a late entry notation explaining the system outage as the reason for delay per CO-DC-003.	EHR recovery within 4 hours; paper documentation immediately during outage; electronic entry within 24 hours of restoration.

A clinician is hospitalized or experiences a personal emergency preventing documentation completion	Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing assesses available documentation. The EHR audit trail will show the visit was scheduled. Director of Nursing shall not create documentation on the clinician's behalf — instead, create an administrative note explaining the situation and the steps being taken. Contact the Compliance Officer regarding billing implications for any affected visits.	Administrative note within 24 hours; Compliance Officer consultation within 48 hours.

Documentation is completed on time but is subsequently identified as materially inadequate (does not support the billed service)	Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer	Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer assess whether the visit can be supported based on the available documentation and the clinical context. If the visit cannot be supported as a skilled service, retract the claim or adjust the claim. Do not allow the clinician to retrospectively add clinical content that was not present at the time of the visit.	Assessment within 5 business days; claim action within 10 business days.

7. Documentation Requirements

Requirement	Document / Record	Responsible Party	Location	Timeframe

Daily Documentation Timeliness Report	Daily overdue entry report	Medical Records / Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file	Daily; retained 7 years

Weekly Documentation Timeliness Compliance Report	Weekly summary by discipline and individual clinician	Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file	Weekly; retained 7 years

Overdue entry escalation documentation	Record of contacts, reasons, and corrective actions for each overdue entry	Director of Nursing	Director of Nursing supervisory file; HR file	At each escalation event; retained 7 years

Documentation Improvement Plans	Formal improvement plan for clinicians with chronic late documentation	Director of Nursing / HR	Personnel file	At each initiation; retained 7 years

Pre-bill documentation verification	Revenue Cycle checklist confirming all visit notes locked before claim submission	Revenue Cycle	Revenue Cycle records	Before each claim; retained 7 years

Monthly Documentation Quality Dashboard	Timeliness data integrated into the monthly documentation audit	Director of Nursing	QAPI records	Monthly; retained 7 years

Policy acknowledgment	Signed acknowledgment — Appendix A	Each individual; Administrator (collection)	Policy acknowledgment file	Within 14 calendar days

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

Compliance Indicator	Measurement Method	Acceptable Standard

Visit notes completed and locked within 24 hours	EHR timestamp audit; daily timeliness report	≥95% within 24 hours; ≥99% within 48 hours

OASIS assessments locked within 5 calendar days	OASIS lock date audit	≥98% within 5 calendar days

No claims submitted with unlocked visit notes	Pre-bill documentation verification audit	100% — zero claims with unlocked notes

Overdue entry escalation protocol activated for all overdue entries	Escalation documentation audit	100% of overdue entries with documented escalation

Documentation Improvement Plan initiated for clinicians with chronic late documentation	Plan initiation log	100% of qualifying clinicians

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors and ADR auditors examine the timestamps of clinical record entries. An EHR records the date and time every entry was created and every time it was modified. If a clinician's visit notes are consistently completed 3–5 days after the visit, surveyors and auditors will question whether the notes accurately reflect what occurred during the visit or were constructed from memory (or from other sources). ADR auditors treat late documentation as a documentation integrity concern and may use it to support a determination that the services were not actually delivered as documented.

8.3 Common Failure Points

Failure Point	Risk	Mitigation

Clinicians accumulate multiple incomplete notes and complete them all at end of the week	Documentation integrity concern; ADR risk	Daily timeliness report; escalation at 24 hours; billing hold at 48 hours

HHA visit logs submitted weekly rather than daily	HHA documentation gap; survey deficiency	EHR mobile app for real-time HHA documentation; Director of Nursing weekly HHA log audit

Visit notes completed on time but OASIS locked after the 5-day window	CMS compliance deficiency; OASIS submission delay	OASIS deadline alerts at Day 3 and Day 4; Director of Nursing daily OASIS status review

Late documentation not identified as late entry	Documentation integrity failure; audit trail does not reveal the lateness	EHR-enforced late entry notation for all entries created beyond the applicable deadline

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All clinical staff shall receive training on documentation timeliness requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: the 24-hour standard for all visit notes; the specific deadlines for OASIS and other entry types; the late entry process when deadlines cannot be met; the consequences of chronic late documentation; and the pre-bill verification process.

10.2 The Director of Nursing shall receive training on the documentation timeliness monitoring system, escalation protocol, and Documentation Improvement Plan process at hire and annually.

10.3 Revenue Cycle staff shall receive training on the pre-bill documentation verification requirement at hire and annually.

10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

11. Version Control

11.1 Maintained per EN-LC-001. Only the most current approved version is valid. 11.2 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and policy index update per EN-TG-001. 11.3 Non-substantive revisions approved by Administrator with Governing Body notification.

CL-PR-001 — Patient Rights & Responsibilities

1. Policy Header

Field	Value

Policy ID	CL-PR-001

Policy Title	Patient Rights & Responsibilities

Domain	CL — Clinical Operations

Subdomain	PR — Patient Rights & Safety

Classification Tier	REQUIRED

Status	ACTIVE

Review Cycle	Annual

Policy Owner / Steward	Director of Nursing

Approved By	Governing Body Chair — Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.

Last Reviewed	2025-07-10

Next Review Date	2026-07-10

Supersedes	N/A (Initial Version)

2. Purpose

This policy ensures that patient rights are communicated, documented, and protected for every patient admitted to home health services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., per 42 CFR § 484.50 — Condition of Participation: Patient Rights. The right to receive home health care with dignity, to participate meaningfully in one's own care decisions, to be free from discrimination and abuse, to understand one's care and to ask questions, to refuse treatment, and to voice grievances without fear of retaliation — these are not aspirational values. They are federally mandated rights that the agency is legally obligated to both communicate and uphold.

Patient rights in home health carry a dimension not present in institutional settings: the patient is in their own home. The clinician is a guest. The power dynamic is different, the patient has chosen to invite the agency into their most private space, and the agency's obligations to respect that space and the person within it are correspondingly elevated. A patient rights framework that protects the patient within the home health relationship must address not only the right to information and to participate in care decisions — but the right to refuse entry, to disagree with the clinical team, to have a representative speak for them, and to receive services without being subjected to discrimination, coercion, or disrespect of any kind.

This policy establishes the complete patient rights framework at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., including: what rights patients hold; when, how, and in what form those rights must be communicated; how the communication must be documented; how the agency responds when rights are potentially violated; and how patients access the grievance process when they believe their rights have not been honored. This policy is the parent policy for the more specific patient rights protections addressed in CL-PR-002 (Advance Directive Compliance) and CL-PR-003 (Informed Consent). It implements 42 CFR § 484.50 and the related requirements of California Health and Safety Code § 1599.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All agency personnel who have direct contact with patients — including all clinical disciplines, intake staff, scheduling staff, and any administrative personnel who interact with patients or their representatives

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

The Administrator responsible for overall patient rights compliance

The Compliance Officer responsible for patient rights grievance oversight

All contracted clinical staff providing services under the agency's authorization

This policy protects every patient admitted to home health services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., including patients who are cognitively impaired, patients who have designated healthcare representatives, pediatric patients (whose rights are exercised through parents or legal guardians), and patients who do not speak English as their primary language.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall provide every patient with a written notice of their rights prior to furnishing care, in a language and manner the patient can understand, and shall document that this notice was provided.

4.2 Every patient has the right to be fully informed of all of the rights and responsibilities described in this policy. The agency shall not condition the provision of services on the patient's agreement with or response to any aspect of the patient rights notice — the notice is informational and non-coercive.

4.3 Every patient has the following rights, which the agency shall actively protect and shall not infringe upon:

(a) The right to be notified of rights. The patient has the right to receive written notice of their rights, in advance of receiving care, in a format and language they can understand.

(b) The right to exercise rights without discrimination or reprisal. The patient has the right to exercise any right described in this policy without fear of discrimination, retaliation, or any adverse effect on the care or services provided.

(c) The right to have rights exercised by a representative. If the patient is unable to exercise their rights, those rights may be exercised by the patient's legally authorized representative (guardian, healthcare agent, or legally authorized decision-maker).

(d) The right to receive care with dignity. The patient has the right to be treated with consideration, respect, and full recognition of their dignity and individuality, including recognition of their cultural background, religious beliefs, and personal values.

(e) The right to receive care without discrimination. The patient has the right to receive care without discrimination on the basis of race, color, national origin, disability, age, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity, religion, or any other protected characteristic.

(f) The right to be notified of the care plan. The patient has the right to be informed of the plan of care prior to or at the initiation of services and to receive advance notice of any significant changes to the plan of care.

(g) The right to participate in care planning. The patient has the right to participate in the development, implementation, and revision of the plan of care, to the extent the patient chooses to participate.

(h) The right to refuse care. The patient has the right to refuse care or any specific aspect of care, including specific visits, specific services, or specific medications. The agency shall document the refusal, the patient's stated reason, and the education provided regarding the clinical implications of the refusal.

(i) The right to choose from among providers. The patient has the right to choose their own home health agency and their own attending physician, free from coercion.

(j) The right to information about the agency. The patient has the right to know the identity and professional qualifications of the agency staff providing care; to know the identity of the supervising physician if applicable; and to know how to contact the agency's supervisory staff.

(k) The right to receive care free from abuse, neglect, and exploitation. The patient has the right to be free from verbal, mental, sexual, and physical abuse; corporal punishment; involuntary seclusion; and misappropriation of patient property by anyone.

(l) The right to confidentiality. The patient has the right to have their clinical record and personal information treated with confidentiality, and to approve or refuse their release, as provided by HIPAA and applicable law.

(m) The right to access their clinical record. The patient has the right to access, inspect, and obtain a copy of their clinical record per CO-HP-006 and applicable law.

(n) The right to voice grievances. The patient has the right to voice grievances regarding treatment, care, or the agency's failure to respect rights, and to expect that the agency will investigate and respond to the grievance promptly and without retaliation.

(o) The right to be free from restraint or seclusion. The patient has the right to be free from any physical or chemical restraint or involuntary seclusion for the purpose of convenience or discipline, per CL-PR-004.

(p) The right to be informed of financial obligations. The patient has the right to receive written information about the care to be provided, the charges for that care, and the payment policies of the agency, in advance of the provision of care.

(q) The right to be informed of the OASIS privacy notice. The patient has the right to be informed that the agency is required to collect OASIS assessment data, and that the patient may request the OASIS privacy notice.

4.4 Every patient has the following responsibilities, which the agency shall communicate to the patient as part of the patient rights notice:

To provide accurate and complete information about their health status, medications, and medical history, to the best of their ability.

To notify the agency if they cannot keep a scheduled appointment or if a situation arises that affects their ability to receive care.

To cooperate with the clinical staff in the implementation of the plan of care.

To ask questions when they do not understand instructions or information provided by the clinical team.

To notify the agency promptly of any changes in their condition.

To fulfill their financial obligations to the agency as mutually agreed.

To treat agency staff with respect and courtesy.

4.5 The patient rights notice shall be provided in the patient's primary language. If written translation is not available in the patient's language, oral interpretation shall be provided per OP-PA-003, and the provision of oral interpretation shall be documented.

5. Definitions

Term	Definition

Patient Rights	The legally mandated entitlements of every home health patient, defined in 42 CFR § 484.50, that the agency is obligated to communicate, protect, and uphold.

Patient Rights Notice	The written document provided to every patient at or prior to the initiation of services that communicates the patient's rights in full, as required by 42 CFR § 484.50(a).

Legally Authorized Representative	A person recognized by California law as authorized to make healthcare decisions on behalf of a patient who lacks decision-making capacity — including a legal guardian, healthcare agent under a durable power of attorney for healthcare, or court-appointed conservator.

Patient Responsibilities	The patient's obligations in the home health relationship — the counterpart to patient rights — that are communicated to promote a productive, safe, and cooperative care relationship.

Grievance	A complaint by a patient or the patient's representative regarding the agency's care, treatment, or failure to respect the patient's rights, which the patient has the right to voice and the agency has an obligation to investigate and resolve.

Discrimination	Differential treatment of a patient based on a protected characteristic — including race, color, national origin, disability, age, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity, or religion — that affects the quality or availability of care provided.

Abuse	Any act or failure to act that causes unreasonable suffering, misery, or harm to a patient — encompassing physical abuse, verbal abuse, mental abuse, sexual abuse, and neglect, as defined in CL-PR-006.

OASIS Privacy Notice	The CMS-required written notice that informs patients that OASIS data will be collected and may be used for research and quality reporting purposes.

Non-Retaliation	The prohibition against any adverse action toward a patient who exercises their rights — including but not limited to changing the quality or frequency of care, threatening termination of services, or treating the patient with hostility.

6. Procedures

6.1 Patient Rights Notice — Provision and Documentation

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.1.1	Intake Staff / Assigned RN	At or prior to the initiation of home health services — specifically, before or at the first visit — provide the patient with the agency's written Patient Rights Notice (Appendix A). The notice shall be provided in the patient's primary language. If the patient's primary language is not English and a written translation is not available, provide oral interpretation through the agency's interpreter services per OP-PA-003 and document the provision of oral interpretation.	At or before the first visit.

6.1.2	Assigned RN	Review the Patient Rights Notice with the patient and/or their legally authorized representative verbally, using plain language. Provide the patient an opportunity to ask questions about any right described in the notice. Answer questions honestly and without minimizing or dismissing any right.	At or before the first visit.

6.1.3	Assigned RN	Obtain the patient's or legally authorized representative's signature on the Patient Rights Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) confirming that the notice was received, reviewed, and that the patient had the opportunity to ask questions. If the patient is unable to sign due to a physical limitation, document the reason and the alternative method of acknowledgment (e.g., "patient verbal acknowledgment obtained in the presence of caregiver; patient unable to sign due to right hand paralysis").	At or before the first visit; filed in the clinical record.

6.1.4	Assigned RN	Provide the patient with the OASIS Patient Privacy Notice (Appendix C) informing the patient that OASIS assessment data will be collected as required by CMS and describing how that data may be used. Document that the OASIS privacy notice was provided.	At the SOC visit; filed in the clinical record.

6.1.5	Assigned RN	If the patient elects to designate a legally authorized representative to exercise their rights, document the representative's name, relationship, and contact information in the clinical record. Verify that the representative's authority is legally valid — request documentation of guardianship, healthcare power of attorney, or other legal authorization and file a copy in the clinical record.	At the SOC visit or when the representative designation is identified.

6.1.6	Medical Records	Verify that the signed Patient Rights Acknowledgment Form is present in every patient's clinical record within 5 business days of the SOC. Any record without this acknowledgment shall be flagged for immediate follow-up with the assigned clinician.	Within 5 business days of SOC.

6.2 Protecting Patient Rights During Care Delivery

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.2.1	All Agency Personnel	Treat every patient with dignity and respect at every interaction. This is not a procedural requirement — it is a professional and ethical obligation. Specific behaviors that are prohibited: demeaning language directed at patients; dismissing patients' concerns; discussing patient information in the presence of non-treating third parties; entering the patient's home without announcement and consent; providing care in a rushed or dismissive manner that does not allow for patient participation.	At all times.

6.2.2	All Clinical Disciplines	Before initiating any procedure or treatment at each visit, explain to the patient what you are going to do and why. This is not a formal informed consent process (governed by CL-PR-003) but rather the routine respect for patient autonomy that allows the patient to anticipate and cooperate with care.	At each visit.

6.2.3	All Clinical Disciplines	When a patient exercises the right to refuse care — whether a specific procedure, a specific visit, or a specific medication — honor the refusal, document it per CL-CD-001, provide education about the clinical implications of the refusal, and notify the physician within 24 hours per CL-CP-005. Do not pressure, coerce, or penalize the patient for exercising the right to refuse.	When a refusal occurs.

6.2.4	All Agency Personnel	When a patient or family member voices a concern, complaint, or grievance: listen respectfully; acknowledge the concern without dismissing it; inform the patient of their right to voice grievances and describe the grievance process per OP-PA-001; provide the patient with the agency's contact information for filing a formal grievance; and document that the concern was raised and that the patient was informed of the grievance process.	When any concern or complaint is expressed.

6.2.5	All Agency Personnel	Report any suspected violation of patient rights to the Director of Nursing immediately — including but not limited to: suspected abuse, neglect, or exploitation by a staff member or caregiver; discrimination; denial of care based on a protected characteristic; unauthorized access to patient records; or any coercive or disrespectful treatment of a patient.	Immediately upon identification.

6.3 Non-Discrimination Compliance

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.3.1	Administrator / Director of Nursing	Ensure that patient admission criteria are applied uniformly regardless of race, color, national origin, disability, age, gender, sexual orientation, gender identity, religion, or any other protected characteristic. Admission decisions shall be based solely on clinical criteria, geographic service area, and licensed scope of services.	At all times; reviewed annually.

6.3.2	Intake Staff	When declining a patient referral, document the specific clinical or operational reason for the declination. Non-clinical reasons for declination (e.g., the patient's neighborhood, the patient's demographic characteristics) are not acceptable. If a pattern of declinations affecting specific patient populations is identified, report to the Compliance Officer immediately.	At each declination.

6.3.3	Director of Nursing	Conduct an annual review of admission and declination patterns to identify any demographic disparities in service access. Report findings to the Governing Body and to the QAPI Committee.	Annually.

6.3.4	Administrator	Post the agency's non-discrimination notice, in compliance with Section 1557 of the Affordable Care Act, in a conspicuous location in the agency office and on any patient-facing written materials. The notice shall describe the availability of language access services and auxiliary aids for patients with disabilities.	Continuously posted.

6.4 Grievance Response Process

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.4.1	Any Agency Personnel	Upon receiving any patient or representative grievance — whether verbal or written, formal or informal — document the grievance in the Grievance Log (Appendix D) immediately. Provide the patient with acknowledgment that the grievance has been received and will be investigated.	Within 24 hours of receipt.

6.4.2	Director of Nursing / Compliance Officer	Acknowledge the grievance in writing to the patient or representative within 5 calendar days of receipt, confirming that the grievance has been received, identifying the individual responsible for investigating it, and providing an estimated timeframe for resolution.	Within 5 calendar days.

6.4.3	Director of Nursing / Compliance Officer	Complete the grievance investigation within 14 calendar days of receipt. The investigation shall include: interview of the patient or representative; interview of the involved staff member(s); review of the relevant clinical records; and a determination of whether the grievance is substantiated.	Within 14 calendar days.

6.4.4	Director of Nursing / Compliance Officer	Provide a written response to the patient or representative within 14 calendar days of the grievance receipt — or as soon as the investigation is complete, whichever is sooner — documenting: the findings of the investigation; the determination (substantiated or unsubstantiated); any corrective actions taken; and the patient's right to escalate the grievance to the California Department of Public Health or CMS if not satisfied with the response.	Within 14 calendar days.

6.4.5	Compliance Officer	If the grievance involves a potential patient rights violation that may constitute abuse, neglect, or exploitation, escalate to mandatory reporting per CL-PR-006 and HR-ER-009, in addition to the grievance investigation.	Immediately upon identification.

6.5 Patient Rights in Emergency Situations

Step	Responsible Party	Action	Timeframe

6.5.1	All Clinicians	In a clinical emergency, the patient's right to refuse treatment may be superseded by the obligation to protect life — specifically, the clinician's obligation to call 911 when the patient's life is in immediate danger, even if the patient is objecting. Document the emergency situation, the clinical findings, the action taken, and the patient's objection if any.	In each emergency.

6.5.2	All Clinicians	In a non-emergent situation where a patient refuses treatment, respect the refusal per Section 6.2.3. A patient with decision-making capacity has the absolute right to refuse even life-sustaining treatment. When the patient has an advance directive, honor it per CL-PR-002.	In each refusal situation.

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

Condition	Escalation Path	Corrective Action	Timeframe

A staff member is found to have violated a patient's rights — including verbal abuse, discriminatory treatment, coercion, or unauthorized disclosure of patient information	Director of Nursing and Compliance Officer	Director of Nursing immediately removes the staff member from patient care pending investigation. Compliance Officer initiates investigation per CO-CP-007. If abuse is substantiated, report to CDPH per CL-PR-006 and initiate termination proceedings per HR-ER-002.	Staff member removed from patient care immediately; investigation within 24 hours.

A patient or representative reports that a patient rights notice was never provided at admission	Director of Nursing and Medical Records	Director of Nursing confirms whether the notice was provided and the documentation is simply missing vs. the notice was genuinely not provided. If not provided, immediately provide the notice and acknowledgment. File a corrective action report.	Notice provided within 24 hours of identification.

A patient files a grievance with CDPH or CMS directly without first filing with the agency	Administrator and Compliance Officer	Respond to the CDPH or CMS inquiry cooperatively and promptly. Conduct an internal investigation regardless of the external filing. Document all actions.	Response to CDPH/CMS per their specified timeframe; internal investigation within 7 calendar days.

7. Documentation Requirements

Requirement	Document / Record	Responsible Party	Location	Timeframe

Patient Rights Notice	Written notice provided to every patient at or before the first visit (Appendix A)	Intake Staff / Assigned RN	EHR — Patient Rights folder (Folder 02)	At or before the first visit; retained 7 years

Patient Rights Acknowledgment Form	Signed acknowledgment by patient or authorized representative (Appendix B)	Assigned RN (collection); patient/representative (signature)	EHR — Patient Rights folder (Folder 02)	At or before the first visit; retained 7 years

OASIS Patient Privacy Notice	Written notice of OASIS data collection (Appendix C)	Assigned RN	EHR — Patient Rights folder (Folder 02)	At the SOC visit; retained 7 years

Legally authorized representative documentation	Copy of guardianship order, healthcare power of attorney, or equivalent	Assigned RN / Medical Records	EHR — Admission & Intake folder (Folder 01)	When designated; retained 7 years

Grievance Log	Record of every grievance received (Appendix D)	Any Agency Personnel (initial); Director of Nursing / Compliance Officer (management)	Compliance file; OP-PA-001 Grievance Log	At receipt; retained 7 years

Grievance acknowledgment and response letters	Written acknowledgment (5 calendar days); written resolution (14 calendar days)	Director of Nursing / Compliance Officer	Compliance file	Per grievance timelines; retained 7 years

Annual non-discrimination review	Documentation of annual admission and declination pattern review	Director of Nursing / Administrator	QAPI file	Annually

Policy acknowledgment	Signed acknowledgment — Appendix E	Each individual; Administrator (collection)	Policy acknowledgment file	Within 14 calendar days

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

Compliance Indicator	Measurement Method	Acceptable Standard

Patient Rights Acknowledgment Form present in every patient record	Medical Records quarterly completeness audit	100%

Patient rights notice provided in patient's primary language	Chart audit and patient interview	≥98%

All grievances logged within 24 hours of receipt	Grievance Log audit	100%

Grievance written acknowledgment provided within 5 calendar days	Grievance Log; correspondence dates	≥95%

Grievance investigation completed within 14 calendar days	Grievance Log; investigation completion dates	≥95%

No staff members with substantiated patient rights violations currently providing direct patient care	HR and Compliance file review	100%

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G155 through Tag G177 (42 CFR § 484.50) will interview patients to verify that they received the patient rights notice and that they understood the rights described. Surveyors will request the Patient Rights Acknowledgment Form from the clinical record and verify it is present for every patient. They will review the grievance log for completeness and for evidence that grievances triggered investigations and written responses. Surveyors may also review clinical records for documentation of patient refusals to assess whether refusals were honored and documented appropriately without pressure or retaliation.

8.3 Common Failure Points

Failure Point	Risk	Mitigation

Patient Rights Notice provided but Acknowledgment Form not signed or not filed	Survey deficiency; missing required documentation	Medical Records verification within 5 business days of SOC; EHR alert for missing acknowledgment

Patient rights notice provided in English to a non-English-speaking patient without interpretation	Non-discrimination violation; survey citation	Interpreter services required when English is not the patient's primary language; documented in the clinical record

Verbal complaints not logged — only formal written grievances tracked	Underreporting of rights concerns	Train all staff that any patient complaint constitutes a grievance requiring logging; all staff can initiate a grievance log entry

Grievance log exists but investigations are not completed or documented	Survey deficiency; inability to demonstrate rights protection	Compliance Officer quarterly grievance log audit; investigation completion tracked in the log

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All agency personnel who have contact with patients shall receive training on patient rights within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: the complete enumeration of patient rights per Section 4.3; the patient rights notice provision and acknowledgment process; the non-discrimination obligation; the non-retaliation prohibition; the grievance process and how to initiate a grievance log entry; and the reporting requirement when a rights violation is suspected.

10.2 Clinical staff shall receive additional training on: honoring patient refusals without coercion; documenting care in a manner that respects the patient's dignity; the interaction between patient rights and advance directives; and recognizing and reporting potential rights violations.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix E) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

11. Version Control

11.1 Maintained per EN-LC-001. Only the most current approved version is valid. 11.2 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and policy index update per EN-TG-001. 11.3 Non-substantive revisions approved by Administrator with Governing Body notification.

Provide accurate health information.

Cooperate with your care team.

Ask questions when you do not understand.

Notify us of changes in your condition.

Meet your financial obligations.

Treat agency staff with respect.
