CL-PR-006 — Abuse, Neglect & Exploitation Reporting

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy mandates the identification, documentation, reporting, and response to suspected abuse, neglect, and exploitation of patients receiving home health services at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The home health setting creates a unique and elevated obligation with respect to abuse and neglect reporting. Unlike institutional care settings where multiple eyes observe the patient daily, home health patients are seen intermittently, often in private spaces with only a single caregiver present between visits. The home health clinician may be the only professional to enter the patient's home on a weekly basis — making the clinician the primary sentinel for the identification of abuse, neglect, or exploitation that might otherwise go undetected.

The population served by home health is among the most vulnerable in the healthcare system: elderly patients with cognitive impairment, patients with physical disabilities, pediatric patients, patients with limited social support, and patients who are financially dependent on or emotionally bonded to their caregivers. Perpetrators of elder and dependent adult abuse are frequently family members or paid caregivers — individuals who control the patient's environment, their finances, their medications, and their access to the outside world. A patient who is being abused by a caregiver cannot easily seek help; they may be physically unable to leave the home, cognitively unable to articulate the abuse, emotionally conflicted about reporting a family member, or afraid of retaliation.

California law is unambiguous: every licensed healthcare professional is a mandated reporter under California Penal Code § 11165.7 (for child abuse) and California Welfare and Institutions Code § 15630 (for elder and dependent adult abuse). The reporting obligation is triggered by reasonable suspicion — not proof, not certainty, not a completed investigation. A single bruise in an unusual location, a patient who flinches when a caregiver enters the room, unexplained dehydration, a patient whose prescribed medications are inexplicably missing, an elder whose bank accounts are being drained — any of these may constitute grounds for a mandated report. This policy establishes the complete framework for recognizing, reporting, documenting, and following up on suspected abuse, neglect, and exploitation, consistent with California mandated reporting law and the agency's obligations under 42 CFR § 484.50 (Patient Rights) and CMS conditions for patient abuse-free care.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All agency personnel — every individual employed by or contracted with Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. who has any contact with patients or who becomes aware of information suggesting abuse, neglect, or exploitation of a patient. This explicitly includes: all clinical disciplines (RN, LVN, PT, OT, SLP, MSW, HHA); intake staff; scheduling staff; administrative staff; and contracted clinical staff.

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager

The Compliance Officer who oversees reporting compliance and investigation coordination

The Administrator who bears organizational accountability for patient rights protection

Human Resources — for situations where the alleged abuser is an agency employee

This policy applies to every patient served by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc., regardless of age — including adult patients, elderly patients, dependent adults, and pediatric patients (for whom the separate mandatory reporting standard under California Penal Code § 11165.7 also applies).

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall maintain a comprehensive abuse, neglect, and exploitation prevention, identification, and reporting program that ensures every patient receives care in an environment free from all forms of abuse, neglect, exploitation, and misappropriation of property.

4.2 Every employee and contractor of Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. who has any contact with patients is a mandated reporter under California law and is personally, professionally, and legally obligated to report known or reasonably suspected abuse, neglect, or exploitation of: (a) patients under age 18 — to law enforcement or the California Department of Social Services child protective services per California Penal Code § 11165.7 and § 11166; (b) patients aged 18 or older who are elders (age 65 or older) or dependent adults — to Adult Protective Services (APS) or the local law enforcement agency per California Welfare and Institutions Code § 15630.

4.3 The reporting obligation is triggered by reasonable suspicion alone — without requiring proof, confirmation, investigation findings, or supervisor approval. A mandated reporter who observes or becomes aware of information that would lead a reasonable person to suspect abuse, neglect, or exploitation is required by law to report. The failure to report when reasonable suspicion exists is a criminal misdemeanor under California law.

4.4 The mandated reporter's obligation is individual and personal — it cannot be delegated to a supervisor, transferred to the agency, or satisfied by notifying the Director of Nursing. Notifying the Director of Nursing is required by this policy and is an important organizational step — but it does not fulfill the individual reporter's personal legal obligation to report to the appropriate government authority.

4.5 The agency shall not discourage, obstruct, delay, or retaliate against any individual who makes a mandated report in good faith, regardless of whether the report is subsequently substantiated. Retaliation against a mandated reporter is a violation of California law, agency policy CO-CP-005, and the patient rights protections of 42 CFR § 484.50.

4.6 No employee or contractor of the agency shall investigate suspected abuse independently, confront the suspected abuser, or take any action that could compromise the investigation being conducted by the appropriate government agency. The agency's role is to report, to ensure the patient's immediate safety, and to cooperate with the investigating authority — not to conduct an independent investigation.

4.7 When the alleged or suspected abuser is a person employed by or contracted through the agency — including any HHA, clinical staff member, intake staff, or other agency personnel — the Director of Nursing shall immediately remove that individual from patient contact pending investigation, and the Compliance Officer shall be notified within 2 hours to coordinate the agency's response.

4.8 Any patient who has been or is suspected of being abused, neglected, or exploited shall receive a prompt clinical assessment of their safety and healthcare needs, and the agency shall take all clinically appropriate steps to ensure the patient's safety — including coordination with APS, law enforcement, or other protective authorities — while maintaining the patient's dignity and respecting their rights.

4.9 The agency shall document all observations, reports, patient assessments, contacts with government agencies, and follow-up actions related to every suspected abuse, neglect, or exploitation situation in the patient's clinical record and in the agency's Incident Report and Compliance Investigation files.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Recognition — Indicators of Abuse, Neglect, and Exploitation

All agency personnel shall be trained to recognize the following indicators of potential abuse, neglect, or exploitation. The presence of one or more of these indicators, particularly in the absence of a plausible clinical or accidental explanation, constitutes a basis for reasonable suspicion and triggers the reporting obligation.

6.1.1 Physical Abuse Indicators:

Unexplained bruising, welts, lacerations, or burns — particularly in locations unusual for accidental injury (torso, upper arms, posterior trunk, genitalia, buttocks)

Bilateral injuries (both sides of the body) suggesting deliberate force rather than accidental fall

Multiple injuries in different stages of healing

Injuries with a pattern consistent with an object (belt buckle shape, cord marks, hand prints)

Delay between injury occurrence and medical treatment

Explanation of injury inconsistent with the injury's nature, severity, or the patient's physical capabilities

Patient or caregiver appears anxious, rehearsed, or inconsistent when explaining an injury

Patient flinches, cowers, or shows extreme anxiety in the presence of the caregiver

6.1.2 Verbal/Emotional Abuse Indicators:

Patient appears fearful, withdrawn, depressed, or agitated in the caregiver's presence but calmer when the caregiver is absent

Caregiver uses demeaning, threatening, or infantilizing language toward the patient in the clinician's presence

Patient exhibits new behavioral changes including social withdrawal, loss of appetite, or regression

Patient expresses fear of the caregiver or fear of what will happen to them after the clinician leaves

Patient is not allowed to speak privately with the clinician or to communicate without the caregiver present and monitoring

6.1.3 Sexual Abuse Indicators:

Unexplained injuries or trauma to the genital or rectal areas

Presence of sexually transmitted infection with no identified source

Unexplained bleeding from vaginal or rectal areas

Sexually inappropriate behaviors newly exhibited by a patient with cognitive impairment

Patient's report, even partial or confused, of unwanted sexual contact

6.1.4 Financial Exploitation Indicators:

Unpaid bills or utilities despite apparent adequate income

Sudden changes in bank account activity, account ownership, or beneficiary designations

Unexplained disappearance of funds, assets, or personal property from the home

Caregiver shows unusual interest in the patient's finances, insists on controlling all financial transactions, or prevents the patient from accessing their own money

Patient is not receiving adequate food, clothing, or medical care despite financial resources

New or unexplained changes to wills, powers of attorney, or property titles

Patient reports that personal belongings or money are missing

6.1.5 Neglect Indicators:

Patient is dehydrated, malnourished, or losing weight without a medical explanation

Patient has untreated injuries, infections, or medical conditions

Patient lacks adequate food, water, medications, or basic personal care supplies despite caregiver responsibility

Patient's living environment is extremely unsafe, unsanitary, or inadequate (no heat, no food, animal waste in the patient's care area)

The caregiver is consistently absent or fails to provide ordered care

Patient's medications are missing or not being administered

Patient has developed pressure injuries that are inconsistent with the level of care they should be receiving

6.1.6 Exploitation and Misappropriation Indicators:

Personal belongings or valuables missing from the patient's home between visits

Caregiver admits to borrowing money or property from the patient

Patient reports giving money or valuables to the caregiver

HHA or other staff member is found to possess items belonging to the patient

6.2 Immediate Response Upon Identifying Suspected Abuse, Neglect, or Exploitation

6.3 Patient Safety Assessment and Clinical Follow-Up

6.4 When the Alleged Abuser Is an Agency Employee or Contractor

6.5 Documentation Requirements for All Abuse-Related Situations

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G167 through Tag G177 (42 CFR § 484.50) will specifically verify:

Evidence of a zero-tolerance abuse policy. Surveyors will request the agency's abuse policy and will evaluate whether it clearly prohibits all forms of abuse and provides specific reporting requirements.

Evidence of annual abuse awareness and mandatory reporting training. Surveyors will request training records for all staff. A staff member who cannot describe what constitutes abuse, the mandatory reporting threshold, or the reporting process is a training deficiency citation.

Evidence that the agency takes prompt action when abuse is suspected. Surveyors may review clinical records and incident reports to verify that identified abuse indicators triggered reports and patient safety assessments. They will look for patterns where abuse indicators were documented in clinical records without corresponding mandatory reports.

Evidence that employees suspected of abuse are immediately removed from patient contact. Surveyors will review any incidents involving suspected employee abuse and verify the agency's response timeline.

Interview patients and caregivers about whether they feel safe and whether they know how to report concerns about their care.

Evidence that the non-retaliation policy is operational. Surveyors may ask staff members whether they know they are protected from retaliation for making a report.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 State Requirements

9.3 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All agency personnel — including every clinical discipline, intake staff, scheduling staff, and administrative staff — shall receive initial training on mandatory abuse reporting within 14 calendar days of hire. This training shall be specifically validated as complete by signature — acknowledgment of this policy satisfies the written attestation requirement under California Penal Code § 11166.5 for child abuse mandated reporters.

10.2 Annual refresher training on mandatory abuse reporting shall be completed by all agency personnel before the anniversary of their hire date. The Director of Nursing shall report any training non-compliance to the Administrator within 7 calendar days of any training deadline.

10.3 Annual training shall include: the definitions of abuse, neglect, exploitation, and misappropriation; the specific clinical indicators for each type; the reasonable suspicion standard; the personal, individual, non-delegable nature of the reporting obligation; the verbal and written report requirements and timelines; the identity of the reporting authorities and their contact information; the non-retaliation protection; and the consequences for failure to report.

10.4 Scenario-based training using realistic home health clinical situations shall be incorporated into the annual training to ensure that clinicians can recognize abuse indicators in practice — not just in the abstract.

10.5 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix B) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment. This acknowledgment also satisfies the California Penal Code § 11166.5 written statement requirement confirming the employee's knowledge of mandatory child abuse reporting obligations.

11. Version Control

11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.

11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy is valid for any operational, compliance, or regulatory purpose. All superseded versions must be archived and clearly marked as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."

11.3 Any substantive revision requires: (a) review and approval by the Governing Body, documented in meeting minutes; (b) re-acknowledgment by all personnel within scope within 14 calendar days; (c) update to the enterprise policy index per EN-TG-001.

11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with Governing Body notification at the next regular meeting.

CL-OA-001 — OASIS Completion Timeliness & Accountability

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy defines required timeframes for OASIS completion at each assessment time point and establishes individual and organizational accountability for late or incomplete assessments at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. OASIS timeliness is simultaneously a regulatory compliance requirement, a billing compliance requirement, and a clinical quality imperative.

Regulatory compliance: 42 CFR § 484.55(a) specifies that the comprehensive assessment must be completed within 5 calendar days of the start of the care episode. 42 CFR § 484.20 requires that OASIS data be transmitted to the national repository within defined timeframes. An OASIS assessment that is not completed within the required window is a deficiency under the Conditions of Participation — subject to survey citation regardless of the clinical quality of the care being provided.

Billing compliance: The OASIS assessment drives the patient's PDGM payment group. An assessment completed late — or completed with data that does not accurately reflect the patient's status at the correct point in time — produces a payment group based on inaccurate data. This creates either an underpayment (the agency is paid less than it earned because the patient's complexity was understated at a late assessment) or an overpayment (the patient was coded at a higher level of need than actually present at the correct time point because conditions changed between the actual clinical event and the late completion).

Clinical quality: The OASIS assessment initiates the clinical care plan. An assessment completed 4 days after the SOC visit is an assessment based on 4-day-old clinical observations — potentially already inaccurate given the dynamic nature of home health patients' conditions. The clinical record serves the next clinician. A comprehensive assessment that is not yet complete when the second visit occurs leaves that clinician without the baseline clinical picture that should be guiding the care plan.

This policy establishes the complete timeliness accountability system — the deadlines, the tracking mechanism, the individual accountability framework, the escalation structure, and the supervisory oversight process — that ensures OASIS assessments are completed accurately, completely, and on time, every time.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses authorized to conduct comprehensive OASIS assessments (per the OASIS Authorized Assessor Roster maintained per CL-CA-002 and CL-OA-003)

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager who is ultimately accountable for OASIS completion timeliness across the agency's entire active census

The OASIS Transmission Coordinator who monitors OASIS completion status and initiates escalation

Medical Records staff who maintain the OASIS completion tracking system

Revenue Cycle staff who rely on completed OASIS assessments for payment classification and claim submission

This policy governs OASIS completion timeliness at all six assessment time points: SOC, ROC, RECERT, FU (SCIC), TRN, and DC.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every OASIS assessment at every applicable time point shall be completed, authenticated, and locked in the EHR within the timeframes specified in Section 6.1 of this policy. These timeframes are absolute regulatory requirements — not targets, preferences, or guidelines. An OASIS assessment not completed and locked within the applicable deadline is a CMS compliance deficiency for that assessment event.

4.2 The agency's internal operational target for OASIS completion is more stringent than the CMS regulatory deadline, providing a buffer for quality review before submission. The operational target and the regulatory deadline for each time point are both specified in Section 6.1.

4.3 An OASIS assessment is not considered "complete" until: (a) all applicable OASIS-E2 items for the relevant time point are entered; (b) no item is left at a default value without clinical justification; (c) the pre-submission quality review checklist per CL-OA-019 has been completed; and (d) the assessing RN has authenticated the assessment in the EHR using their individual electronic signature. Partial completion, draft status, or unauthenticated assessments are not valid completed assessments.

4.4 The agency shall maintain a centralized OASIS Completion Tracking Dashboard that displays, for every active patient at every applicable time point, the current completion status of the OASIS assessment, the operational target date, and the regulatory deadline. The Dashboard shall be reviewed daily by the OASIS Transmission Coordinator and weekly by the Director of Nursing.

4.5 Individual RN assessors are accountable for completing their assigned OASIS assessments within the applicable deadlines. The Director of Nursing is organizationally accountable for the agency's aggregate OASIS timeliness compliance. Both levels of accountability are tracked and reported.

4.6 An OASIS assessment that cannot be completed within the regulatory deadline due to circumstances beyond the clinician's control — patient hospitalization during the assessment window, documented EHR system outage, clinician medical emergency — shall be documented with a clinical explanation of the reason for the delay. CMS does not provide a regulatory extension for these circumstances, but documented good-faith efforts and clinical explanations mitigate survey findings.

4.7 Revenue Cycle shall not submit a claim for any episode or service dependent on an OASIS assessment that has not been completed, authenticated, and locked. The OASIS completion status for every active episode shall be verified as part of the pre-bill checklist per FN-BC-001.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 OASIS Assessment Time Point Deadlines

The following table defines the assessment window, operational target, and regulatory deadline for each OASIS time point. All deadlines are measured in calendar days unless otherwise noted.

6.2 OASIS Completion Tracking Dashboard — Daily Management

6.3 Specific Time Point Management — High-Risk Scenarios

6.4 Escalation — Late Assessments

6.5 Individual RN Assessor Accountability

6.6 QAPI Reporting and Continuous Monitoring

6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors under Tag G140 (42 CFR § 484.55) and OASIS validation survey protocols will:

Request OASIS lock dates for a sample of episodes and compare them to the trigger event dates. A pattern of assessments locked after the 5-day regulatory deadline is a direct CoP compliance deficiency.

Examine RECERT assessments specifically to verify that the assessment was conducted between Day 56 and Day 60 — not before Day 56 and not after Day 60. An assessment conducted on Day 55 is as non-compliant as one conducted on Day 61.

Look for evidence of an active timeliness monitoring program — a tracking dashboard, escalation documentation, and QAPI reporting demonstrating that the agency actively monitors and addresses timeliness failures.

Request the agency's corrective counseling records for any assessors with documented late completions, to verify that the agency holds individual assessors accountable.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 CMS Guidance

9.3 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All authorized RN assessors shall receive training on OASIS completion timeliness requirements within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: time point definitions and their triggers; operational target and regulatory deadline for each time point; the OASIS Completion Tracking Dashboard and how to monitor their own status; the process for reporting barriers to timely completion; the escalation consequences for missed deadlines; and the EHR workflow for completing and locking assessments on mobile devices during or immediately following visits.

10.2 The OASIS Transmission Coordinator shall receive training on Dashboard management, escalation protocols, and reporting requirements at hire and whenever the tracking system is updated.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

CL-OA-002 — OASIS Quality Review & Error Correction

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes a structured, multi-layered quality review process for completed OASIS assessments and defines the error correction and resubmission process at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. OASIS quality review is the clinical governance mechanism that stands between an assessor's work product and the consequences that OASIS data produces — PDGM payment classification, quality measure calculation, public reporting, and survey documentation. Without a rigorous quality review layer, individual assessor errors propagate unchecked into payment distortions, quality measure inaccuracies, and billing compliance vulnerabilities.

The OASIS quality review program serves two distinct functions that are equally essential. The pre-submission quality review — conducted before the OASIS is locked and transmitted — is the opportunity to correct errors before they create consequences. An error caught at pre-submission is simply corrected; it costs time but produces no regulatory, financial, or billing harm. The post-submission audit — conducted on a sample basis after transmission — is the quality assurance mechanism that identifies systemic patterns in OASIS accuracy, drives targeted training, validates that the pre-submission review is effective, and generates the data the QAPI program needs to measure clinical assessment quality over time.

A robust error correction process is equally critical. Errors will occur — the OASIS is a complex, multi-item instrument requiring clinical judgment, and human error is inevitable. The question is not whether errors will be made, but whether the agency has a process for identifying them and correcting them promptly, accurately, and in compliance with CMS correction methodology requirements. This policy establishes both layers — review and correction — with the specificity and accountability required to make them operationally effective.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All authorized RN assessors who conduct comprehensive OASIS assessments

The designated OASIS Quality Reviewer (a role defined in Section 5 and CL-CA-002) — typically the Director of Nursing or a clinician with advanced OASIS training designated by the Director of Nursing

The Director of Nursing / Clinical Manager who oversees the quality review program

The OASIS Transmission Coordinator who processes corrections

The Compliance Officer who is notified of systemic accuracy failures and payment-affecting corrections

Revenue Cycle staff who must hold claims pending correction of identified errors

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Every OASIS assessment submitted by Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. shall undergo a structured quality review process before submission to the CMS repository. The pre-submission quality review is not optional for any assessor and is not waivable for any time point.

4.2 The agency shall designate a qualified OASIS Quality Reviewer who holds current, advanced OASIS competency and is authorized to conduct independent clinical review of OASIS assessments. The OASIS Quality Reviewer shall not be the same individual as the assessing RN for the assessment under review.

4.3 The quality review process shall operate at three levels: (a) Level 1 — Self-Review: the assessing RN completes the Pre-Submission Quality Review Checklist per CL-OA-019 before locking every assessment; (b) Level 2 — OASIS Quality Reviewer Review: the designated Quality Reviewer independently reviews all SOC assessments within 5 business days of locking; quarterly random samples of all other time point assessments; (c) Level 3 — Director of Nursing Monthly Audit: the Director of Nursing conducts a monthly audit of ≥5% of all locked assessments from the prior month per CL-CA-002.

4.4 When the Level 1, Level 2, or Level 3 review identifies an error in a locked assessment — whether before or after submission — the error shall be corrected using the CMS-compliant correction process defined in Section 6.4. The correction shall be clinically justified and documented before processing. No correction shall be made for any reason other than to improve the accuracy of the assessment relative to the patient's actual clinical status at the time of the original assessment.

4.5 The prohibition on financially motivated corrections is absolute: OASIS corrections shall not be initiated for the purpose of increasing or decreasing the patient's PDGM payment group, improving quality measure scores, or any other financial or administrative purpose. Every correction must be clinically justified in writing. The Compliance Officer shall review all corrections that result in a change to the patient's PDGM payment group.

4.6 The agency's target OASIS data accuracy rate — defined as the percentage of audited items where the agency's response correctly reflects the CMS guidance for the patient's clinical status — is ≥95% across all item categories. An accuracy rate below 95% in any item category in any quarter shall trigger a targeted Performance Improvement Project per QA-PI-001.

4.7 Individual RN assessor accuracy rates shall be tracked and reported to the Director of Nursing monthly. Assessors with item category accuracy rates below 85% shall receive targeted retraining within 14 calendar days of the finding.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Level 1 Review — Assessing RN Self-Review

6.2 Level 2 Review — OASIS Quality Reviewer Independent Review

6.3 Level 3 Review — Director of Nursing Monthly Audit

6.4 Error Correction Process — Pre-Submission and Post-Submission

6.5 Inactivation Process

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS OASIS validation survey teams will independently assess a sample of patients and compare their clinical findings to the submitted OASIS data. They will:

Request the agency's Quality Review Program documentation — including the Quality Review Tool, Quality Review Log, Accuracy Dashboard, and corrective action records. An agency without a documented, active quality review program cannot demonstrate survey readiness.

Cross-reference OASIS responses with clinical record narrative — for every item in their sample, surveyors will verify that the clinical record contains objective documentation supporting the coded response. An OASIS response unmoored from clinical documentation is an accuracy deficiency.

Review the Correction Log — to assess whether corrections appear clinically justified or financially motivated. A pattern of corrections that consistently move PDGM payment groups upward is a red flag for upcoding.

Interview the OASIS Quality Reviewer — to verify that the person understands their role, has appropriate OASIS expertise, and can describe the quality review process.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All authorized RN assessors shall receive training on the OASIS quality review process within 14 calendar days of hire and annually thereafter, including: the three-level review structure; the self-review process and checklist; how to respond to Quality Reviewer findings; the error correction process; and the prohibition on financially motivated corrections.

10.2 The OASIS Quality Reviewer shall hold and maintain current advanced OASIS competency — validated through external OASIS specialist certification or equivalent rigorous assessment — and shall demonstrate current knowledge of the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual at hire and annually.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

11. Version Control

CL-OA-003 — OASIS Clinician Authorization & Competency

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy restricts OASIS completion to clinicians who have demonstrated competency through validated assessment and maintains a current authorization roster at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The OASIS instrument is the most consequential clinical document the agency produces — it drives payment, generates quality measures, and creates the clinical baseline against which all subsequent care is measured. Its completion requires not only an active California RN license and general clinical knowledge, but specific, validated competency in: the structure and logic of the OASIS data set; the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual response selection rules for each item category; the application of standardized assessment tools (BIMS, PHQ-2/PHQ-9) in validated protocols; documentation standards that substantiate every OASIS response; and the pre-submission quality review process.

An RN who is clinically excellent — an outstanding bedside nurse with years of experience — is not automatically qualified to conduct an independent OASIS assessment. The instrument has its own learning curve, its own rules, and its own pitfalls. BIMS administration is frequently performed incorrectly by assessors who were never specifically trained on the validated administration protocol. GG functional items are frequently miscoded because assessors apply intuitive clinical judgment rather than the precise CMS response selection rules. Wound staging errors — particularly reverse-staging of healing pressure injuries — occur repeatedly by clinicians who are otherwise competent wound care nurses.

This policy establishes the complete competency framework for OASIS authorization: the training program required before independent assessment; the competency validation process that gates authorization; the Authorized Assessor Roster that controls who may independently conduct assessments; the annual re-competency process that maintains authorization; and the supervisory oversight process for newly authorized assessors. It ensures that every OASIS assessment submitted by this agency was completed by someone who was specifically trained, specifically evaluated, and specifically authorized to do so.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All registered nurses who are assigned or wish to be assigned to conduct comprehensive OASIS assessments

The Director of Nursing who maintains the Authorized Assessor Roster and the competency validation program

HR Director who maintains competency documentation in personnel files

IT Director who configures EHR access restrictions to OASIS assessment modules

Who may NOT independently complete OASIS assessments: Licensed Vocational Nurses (LVNs); Physical Therapists and Physical Therapist Assistants; Occupational Therapists and Certified Occupational Therapy Assistants; Speech-Language Pathologists; Medical Social Workers; Home Health Aides; any administrative or non-clinical staff. These individuals may contribute clinical data to the assessing RN during the assessment process, but they may not independently initiate, complete, or authenticate an OASIS assessment.

4. Policy Statement

4.1 Only registered nurses who have successfully completed the agency's OASIS Competency Program, been independently evaluated through the OASIS Competency Validation Assessment, and been listed on the current OASIS Authorized Assessor Roster by the Director of Nursing may independently conduct comprehensive OASIS assessments at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.

4.2 No RN — regardless of years of experience, prior employer's OASIS training, or OASIS certification from another organization — shall independently conduct comprehensive OASIS assessments until they have completed the agency's specific OASIS Competency Program and have been added to the Authorized Assessor Roster. Prior experience is relevant but not sufficient.

4.3 OASIS authorization is maintained through annual re-competency. An RN's authorization lapses on the anniversary of their most recent competency validation if the annual re-competency has not been completed. An assessor whose authorization has lapsed shall not independently conduct OASIS assessments until annual re-competency is completed and authorization is restored.

4.4 The OASIS Authorized Assessor Roster shall be maintained by the Director of Nursing in a format that is accessible to Medical Records staff, the OASIS Quality Reviewer, Revenue Cycle staff, and Clinical Coordinators — all of whom need to verify that a given assessor was authorized at the time of a specific assessment.

4.5 The EHR shall be configured to restrict access to the OASIS assessment module to individuals listed on the current Authorized Assessor Roster. This technical control is a backstop — it reinforces but does not replace the administrative controls established by this policy.

4.6 A newly hired RN who was an authorized OASIS assessor at a previous agency may be granted provisional authorization — allowing supervised OASIS completion alongside a current authorized assessor — for a period not to exceed 30 calendar days while completing the agency's specific OASIS Competency Program. Provisionally authorized assessors may not independently lock any OASIS assessment; every assessment initiated during the provisional period must be co-reviewed and co-authenticated by a current authorized assessor.

4.7 The OASIS Quality Reviewer role requires an additional, higher level of OASIS competency beyond general assessor authorization. The OASIS Quality Reviewer must hold advanced OASIS expertise — evidenced by external OASIS certification, completion of an advanced OASIS training program, or equivalent experience assessed and documented by the Director of Nursing. The OASIS Quality Reviewer designation is noted separately on the Authorized Assessor Roster.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 OASIS Competency Program — Initial Authorization

6.2 Authorized Assessor Roster — Maintenance

6.3 Annual Re-Competency — Authorization Maintenance

6.4 Provisional Authorization — Management

6.5 OASIS Quality Reviewer — Advanced Authorization

6.6 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors will request the agency's OASIS Authorized Assessor Roster and will cross-reference it against the assessors who completed submitted OASIS assessments during the survey look-back period. They will request personnel files for assessors and verify that competency validation documentation is present. They will ask the Director of Nursing to describe the authorization process. An OASIS assessment completed by a non-RN or by an unauthorized RN is a Condition-level deficiency.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 Initial OASIS authorization requires completion of the full OASIS Competency Program and passage of all three components of the Competency Validation Assessment per Section 6.1.

10.2 Annual re-competency per Section 6.3 is required to maintain authorization. The Director of Nursing shall track and enforce completion before each assessor's annual authorization expiration.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix C) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

CL-OA-004 — OASIS Item-Level Guidance Compliance

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy requires all OASIS responses to align with the current CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual and prohibits agency-created coding interpretations that conflict with CMS guidance at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. The OASIS Guidance Manual is the definitive authority for every coding decision — not the assessor's clinical intuition, not the agency's internally developed "cheat sheets," not the guidance from a prior version of OASIS, not the interpretation provided by a revenue cycle vendor, and not what the agency believes CMS "should" mean by a particular item definition.

The history of home health compliance enforcement is littered with agencies that developed their own internal OASIS coding conventions — often driven by payment optimization concerns — that deviated from or directly contradicted the CMS Guidance Manual. These deviations, when identified through OASIS validation surveys, MAC audits, or OIG investigations, produced findings of systematic upcoding or inaccurate data reporting that led to recoupments, corrective action plans, and in some cases False Claims Act settlements. The agency that codes the GG functional items by asking the patient what they can do, rather than by observing what they actually do during the visit, is not just making a clinical error — it is systematically overstating functional impairment in a way that increases PDGM payment. The agency that codes wound stages without applying the NPIAP staging system is not just making an assessment error — it is generating invalid quality measure data that misrepresents the agency's patient outcomes.

This policy establishes the absolute authority of the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual, the process for accessing and maintaining current guidance, the prohibition on agency-created alternative interpretations, the process for resolving item-level ambiguity through legitimate channels, and the governance of guidance updates when CMS revises the OASIS instrument or the Guidance Manual.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All authorized OASIS assessors who make item-level coding decisions

The Director of Nursing who oversees coding guidance compliance

The OASIS Quality Reviewer who validates item-level coding against the Guidance Manual

The Compliance Officer who oversees the prohibition on financially motivated coding deviations

Any educational materials, training programs, reference tools, or coding aids used by the agency in connection with OASIS assessment

4. Policy Statement

4.1 The CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual — the most current version, as published by CMS — is the sole authoritative source for all OASIS item-level coding decisions at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. No other source has equivalent authority.

4.2 When an assessor has a question about the correct coding of any OASIS item for a specific patient situation, the correct process is: first, consult the current CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual for the specific item; second, review the item-specific FAQ or Q&A materials published by CMS or the relevant state OASIS contractor; third, consult the Director of Nursing or OASIS Quality Reviewer for clinical adjudication; fourth, if the guidance remains unclear, submit the question to the CMS OASIS contractor or the state OASIS coordinator through the official Q&A submission process. At no point in this process shall the assessor create an agency-specific interpretation that is not grounded in and consistent with CMS guidance.

4.3 The agency shall maintain a current, accessible copy of the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual in the EHR reference library and shall update it within 30 calendar days of any CMS revision to the Guidance Manual. Assessors shall access the Guidance Manual from the agency's EHR reference library — not from memory, from prior-version documents, or from third-party materials that have not been verified for consistency with the current Guidance Manual.

4.4 The following practices are explicitly prohibited at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc.:

(a) Using a prior version of the OASIS Guidance Manual as the basis for any coding decision after a new version has been published and adopted.

(b) Applying an internally developed "cheat sheet," "quick reference guide," or coding shortcut that selects OASIS response options through a decision pathway not grounded in the current CMS Guidance Manual. All quick reference materials used in OASIS coding must be verified against the current Guidance Manual and must explicitly note that they are supplements to — not replacements of — the Guidance Manual.

(c) Coding OASIS items in a systematic manner that departs from the Guidance Manual's response selection rules — including but not limited to: coding GG functional items based solely on patient or caregiver report without observing the patient perform the activity; coding wound items without undressing dressings and directly observing wounds; coding behavioral health items without administering the required standardized tools in validated protocols; or reverse-staging healing pressure injuries.

(d) Accepting OASIS coding instructions from revenue cycle staff, billing consultants, or management personnel that deviate from the CMS Guidance Manual's response selection rules, for any reason — including payment optimization.

(e) Retaining in the agency's reference library any OASIS coding guidance, training material, or reference tool that conflicts with the current version of the CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual, without clearly marking the conflicting material as "SUPERSEDED — DO NOT USE."

4.5 When the CMS Guidance Manual provides an unclear or ambiguous response to a specific clinical scenario — a situation that genuinely exists for some items — the assessor shall use the clinical reasoning documentation process per CL-OA-012 to document the specific ambiguity, the interpretive approach applied, and the clinical basis for the selected response. This documentation does not authorize deviation from CMS guidance — it documents the assessor's good-faith application of judgment where the guidance itself is ambiguous.

4.6 The agency shall track CMS OASIS guidance updates through the CMS website, CMS OASIS listservs, and state OASIS contractor communications, and shall disseminate updates to all authorized assessors within 14 calendar days of any change to the Guidance Manual.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Access to Current CMS OASIS Guidance

6.2 Item-Level Ambiguity Resolution

6.3 Audit of Coding Materials for Guidance Compliance

6.4 Governance of Third-Party OASIS Training and Reference Materials

6.5 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

Surveyors will select specific OASIS items from a patient sample and will bring the current CMS OASIS-E2 Guidance Manual to compare the agency's coding decisions against. They will verify that the assessor can articulate the CMS guidance basis for the selected response. They will also review the agency's coding reference materials and assess whether any of these materials contain response-selection shortcuts that bypass or misrepresent the Guidance Manual's rules.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All authorized assessors shall receive training on Guidance Manual compliance requirements within their initial OASIS Competency Program and annually at re-competency, including: accessing the current Guidance Manual in the EHR reference library; the prohibition on agency-created coding interpretations; the ambiguity resolution process; and the prohibition on revenue-motivated coding.

10.2 When CMS issues a Guidance Manual update, all authorized assessors shall complete an update training session and sign a receipt acknowledgment before coding items affected by the update.

10.3 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

Appendix A — Policy Acknowledgment Form

Collected By: _________________________ Date Filed: _____________

CL-OA-005 — OASIS Data Integrity & Security

1. Policy Header

2. Purpose

This policy establishes controls to ensure OASIS data is accurate, complete, securely transmitted, and protected from unauthorized access or modification at Care Indeed Home Health Care, Inc. OASIS data occupies a unique position at the intersection of clinical accuracy, financial integrity, and data security. It is simultaneously: (a) protected health information (PHI) under HIPAA — subject to all access control, transmission security, and breach notification requirements; (b) a clinical quality record — the accuracy of which determines the validity of every quality measure the agency reports publicly; (c) a financial record — the content of which drives Medicare payment classification and creates billing compliance obligations; and (d) a regulatory submission — transmitted to a federal database and subject to the full enforcement authority of CMS, OIG, and the Department of Justice.

The security and integrity dimensions of OASIS data are frequently underemphasized in home health agency governance. Agencies focus intensely on coding accuracy — and rightly so — but the controls that ensure data integrity (is this actually what the assessor coded?) and security (who can access, modify, or transmit this data?) are equally essential and equally subject to regulatory scrutiny. An OASIS database that allows non-clinical staff to modify assessment responses, that transmits data over unencrypted channels, that lacks an audit trail of every modification, or that cannot demonstrate the chain of custody from the assessor's clinical evaluation to the transmitted CMS record has failed the data integrity and security obligations that underpin the validity of the entire OASIS program.

This policy establishes the complete data integrity and security framework for OASIS data at this agency — the access controls, the transmission security standards, the audit trail requirements, the data retention protocols, and the breach and unauthorized modification response process.

3. Scope

This policy applies to:

All authorized OASIS assessors who create, access, and authenticate OASIS data

The Director of Nursing who oversees OASIS data governance

IT Director / CISO who configures and maintains EHR security controls for OASIS data

OASIS Transmission Coordinator who manages data transmission to CMS

Medical Records staff who manage completed OASIS data within the clinical record

The Compliance Officer who oversees HIPAA compliance and data breach response

Revenue Cycle staff who access OASIS data for payment classification purposes

4. Policy Statement

4.1 OASIS data — including all draft and completed OASIS assessments, all transmission records, all quality review records, and all correction documentation — is protected health information (PHI) under HIPAA and shall be protected in accordance with all HIPAA Privacy and Security Rule requirements per policies CO-HP-001 and CO-HP-002.

4.2 Access to OASIS assessment data within the EHR shall be restricted through role-based access controls per IT-SC-002. Only the following roles may access OASIS data: authorized RN assessors (for assessments in their assigned caseload); the OASIS Quality Reviewer; the Director of Nursing; Revenue Cycle staff (read-only access for payment classification verification); the Compliance Officer; and Medical Records staff. No other role — including non-clinical administrative staff, HHAs, LVNs, and therapy staff — shall have access to the OASIS assessment module.

4.3 OASIS data shall not be modified by any person other than the original assessing RN or the Director of Nursing / OASIS Quality Reviewer conducting a legitimate error correction per CL-OA-002. Any modification to a locked OASIS assessment shall be captured in an auditable amendment record per CO-DC-001, identifying the person who made the modification, the date and time, the original content, and the modified content.

4.4 OASIS data shall be transmitted to the CMS national repository using only CMS-approved, encrypted transmission channels through the agency's certified OASIS submission software. Transmission over unsecured networks, unencrypted channels, or through unauthorized software is prohibited.

4.5 The EHR shall maintain a complete, unalterable audit trail of all access to and all modifications of OASIS assessment data, per CO-DC-001. The audit trail shall record: the user identity; the date and time of access or modification; the specific data accessed or modified; and the type of action (view, create, modify, delete). The audit trail itself shall be protected from modification and shall be retained for a minimum of 7 years.

4.6 Any suspected unauthorized access to, unauthorized modification of, or breach of OASIS data shall be reported to the IT Director and Compliance Officer within 2 hours of identification, and shall be investigated and addressed per the HIPAA Breach Notification requirements of CO-HP-003.

4.7 OASIS data shall be retained in the EHR for a minimum of 7 years from the date of submission to the CMS repository, consistent with the retention requirements of CO-HP-007 and CMS record retention standards.

4.8 OASIS data shall not be stored, processed, or transmitted outside the agency's HIPAA-compliant systems and networks. OASIS data shall not be stored on personal devices, personal cloud storage, unencrypted USB drives, or any other storage medium that is not managed and secured under the agency's IT security program per IT-SC-002 through IT-SC-006.

5. Definitions

6. Procedures

6.1 Access Control Configuration and Maintenance

6.2 Audit Trail Management

6.3 Secure Transmission — OASIS Data to CMS Repository

6.4 Data Storage Security

6.5 Unauthorized Access and Modification Response

6.6 Data Retention and Secure Destruction

6.7 Escalation and Exception Handling

7. Documentation Requirements

8. Compliance & Audit Considerations

8.1 How Compliance Is Measured

8.2 What Surveyors and Auditors Will Look For

CMS surveyors conducting HIPAA security compliance reviews and OASIS validation surveys will:

Verify that only authorized personnel can access OASIS data — surveyors will request the EHR access configuration and verify it restricts access to appropriate roles.

Verify that an audit trail exists for OASIS data — surveyors will request the audit trail for selected assessment records and verify that all accesses and modifications are documented.

Verify secure transmission — surveyors will request the transmission software documentation and may ask about the encryption standards used.

Assess the agency's response to any identified security incidents — surveyors will request documentation of any unauthorized access incidents and the agency's breach assessment and response.

8.3 Common Failure Points

9. References

9.1 Federal Regulations

9.2 Cross-Referenced Agency Policies

10. Training & Acknowledgment Requirements

10.1 All authorized OASIS assessors shall receive training on OASIS data security requirements within 14 calendar days of initial authorization and annually thereafter, including: the prohibition on storing OASIS data outside the HIPAA-compliant EHR; the prohibition on transmitting OASIS data through unapproved channels; the audit trail and its implications for data integrity; and the reporting requirement for suspected unauthorized access.

10.2 IT staff responsible for EHR OASIS access controls shall receive training on the access control configuration requirements of this policy and on the audit trail review obligation at implementation and annually.

10.3 The Compliance Officer shall ensure that OASIS data security is incorporated into the agency's annual HIPAA security risk analysis per CO-HP-002.

10.4 All personnel within scope shall sign the Policy Acknowledgment Form (Appendix A) within 14 calendar days of the effective date, any revision, or hire/appointment.

11. Version Control

11.1 This policy is maintained under the agency's enterprise policy lifecycle management system per policy EN-LC-001.

11.2 Only the most current approved version of this policy is valid. Superseded versions shall be archived as "SUPERSEDED — NOT FOR USE."

11.3 Substantive revisions require Governing Body approval, re-acknowledgment within 14 calendar days, and policy index update per EN-TG-001.

11.4 Non-substantive revisions may be approved by the Administrator with Governing Body notification at the next regular meeting.
